# SAIRN Active Work — CC

Per-session active-work log for the **CC** session (`Documents\SAIRN-cc`).
Split out of the shared `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK.md` on 2026-08-24 because four
concurrent sessions appending to one file produced repeated merge conflicts
in a single night. Each session now appends only to its own file, so two
sessions can never collide on the same lines.

**Append your entries here, not to `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK.md`.**

The four sessions and their files — one row each, all equal, no special case:

| Session | Clone | File |
|---|---|---|
| Hank | `Documents\SAIRN-hank` | `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-hank.md` |
| CC | `Documents\SAIRN-cc` | `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-cc.md` ← this file |
| Cody | `Documents\SAIRN-cody` | `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-cody.md` |
| Fourth | `Documents\SAIRN-fourth` | `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-fourth.md` |

Still read the other three (`SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-hank.md`, `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-cody.md`, `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-fourth.md`) before starting anything —
the point of the shared file was to avoid two sessions touching the same app
file at once, and that reason has not gone away. Splitting the file removes
the write collision, not the need to check.

One line per task: app/file(s) touched, a one-line task description,
timestamp. Add a line before starting anything new.

Entries below were moved verbatim from the shared file (nothing reworded,
nothing summarized), in their original order. Cross-session context that
predates the split — including the `cc-work` worktree correction — stays in
`SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK.md`.

---

- sairncash.html / Firebase project sarintype-6e070 (CC, dedicated worktree, branch cc-work) -- Env vars now set in Vercel (Michael), verified live: /api/sairncash/firebase-config now returns a real config (apiKey/authDomain/databaseURL/projectId/storageBucket/messagingSenderId/appId/measurementId all present, curl-confirmed), and the client-side customerId fix from the prior entry now correctly reaches Firebase -- window._fbIncomeRef is genuinely set (true) on a fresh real trial signup (cc-test3-20260819@sairncash-verification.example), which it wasn't before either fix. STILL BLOCKED, NEW ROOT CAUSE FOUND (real, not guessed): every write -- the automatic presence write AND two separate income-entry write attempts (one via real UI click, one via calling submitIncome() directly in devtools) -- fails with the SAME real Firebase client error, console-confirmed 4 times: "PERMISSION_DENIED: Permission denied" / "FIREBASE WARNING: set at /sairncash/customers/{customerId}/... failed: permission_denied". This is the Realtime Database's own security rules rejecting the write, not a config or code problem -- config is confirmed correct (fbIncomeRef sets up fine) and the client code is confirmed correct (both an automatic system write and a direct function call fail identically). Root issue: this app has no Firebase Authentication step anywhere (auth model is the trial-token/license-key system validated server-side at the API layer) -- every RTDB read/write happens from the browser with no Firebase auth token attached, so RTDB rules requiring `auth != null` (Firebase's own default-ish posture) or a locked-down default (`.read/.write: false`) reject everything unconditionally, matching exactly what was observed. NOT fixed here -- changing Firebase security rules is a real access-control decision (an open-by-path-obscurity rule vs. minting real Firebase custom auth tokens server-side are both real options with different tradeoffs), Michael's call, and outside what I have console access to anyway. Recommend: either (a) set RTDB rules to allow read/write scoped to `/sairncash/customers/$customerId` unconditionally (relies on the customerId being an unguessable real uuid, same trust model the license/trial tokens already use elsewhere on this platform), or (b) have the trial-verify/verify endpoints mint a Firebase custom auth token server-side and sign the client in before any read/write -- (b) is more correct long-term but a real build task, not a config change. Three throwaway trial accounts now exist from this session's testing (cc-test-20260818@, cc-test2-20260818@, cc-test3-20260819@sairncash-verification.example) -- no real data persisted under any of them since every write was rejected, safe to leave or clean up. 2026-08-19

- sairncode.html + api/sc-credentials.js + api/sc-eligibility.js + api/sd-data.js + sql/sairncode_{credentials,eligibility}_schema.sql (CC, dedicated worktree, branch cc-work) -- PHASE 1 OF 3 DONE, pushed (`fba67d9`), live-verified. Scoped all three BYO-credential integrations first per Michael's instruction (plan at ~/.claude/plans/woolly-mapping-quilt.md, approved); building in his confirmed order, eligibility first. REAL CORRECTION MADE BEFORE BUILDING, not after: Michael's stated rationale for choosing encrypted per-license storage cited three existing BYO precedents on this platform -- two of them do not exist. SAIRNlaw's citator is NOT bring-your-own, it is a single platform-wide token (api/_lib/courtlistener.js:97 reads process.env.COURTLISTENER_API_TOKEN; api/courtlistener.js:19 says "Shared by every SAIRNlaw firm") -- i.e. it is the option he ruled out. "VetSCT" has zero matches anywhere in the repo. CPT/ICD-10 has only a deferral on record, not a decided architecture. Independently confirmed no per-tenant credential storage existed anywhere before this commit. Reported plainly rather than quietly building on a false premise; the decision still stands because his OTHER stated reason (not making SAIRN the business associate for every practice's PHI traffic) is independently sound, and he was told to say so if precedent-consistency was the load-bearing argument instead. BUILT: api/sc-credentials.js (per-practice credential store, reuses the already-exported encryptSecret/decryptSecret AES-256-GCM helpers from api/_lib/auth.js -- no new crypto; status/set/clear; set+clear admin-only re-verified server-side; the load-bearing rule is that no action ever returns a stored secret to any client in any role -- 'status' returns presence + a last-4 fingerprint only). api/sc-eligibility.js (real X12 270/271 via the practice's OWN Stedi account; endpoint URLs, auth header shape and request/response field names were LIVE-RESEARCHED from Stedi's own docs, not answered from training data -- POST /2024-04-01/change/medicalnetwork/eligibility/v3 and GET /2024-04-01/payers/search, raw key in Authorization with no Bearer prefix; decrypted key exists only as a local for one outbound call, never logged or returned; no credential -> 503 NOT_CONFIGURED, payer/Stedi error -> the real message surfaced verbatim, timeout -> explicitly labelled NOT a coverage answer). Client: new Eligibility panel (nav + check form + payer search + check history + admin-only Clearinghouse Connection setup), sc_eligibility as the 19th SC_RESOURCES entry, and a check_eligibility AI tool. REAL BUG CAUGHT AND FIXED DURING THE BUILD, not shipped: SC_TOOLS' dispatcher scExecuteTool() was synchronous, so an async tool would have handed the model a serialized Promise ({}) as though it were a real payer answer -- exactly the silent-fabrication class this whole audit arc has been removing; made it async/await-aware (every previously registered tool is synchronous and is unaffected -- an async function returning a plain value resolves to that value). Result rendering shows only fields the payer actually returned; an omitted field renders "payer returned no data", never blank/$0/"covered". Also corrected my own mistake mid-build: a python read/write on api/sd-data.js flattened all 2312 lines from CRLF to LF (a 4633-line diff for a 3-line change, exactly the bulk-replace CLAUDE.md forbids) -- caught it in the diff before commit, restored CRLF, final diff is 12 insertions/3 deletions. PRE-PUSH: node --check clean on the single extracted script block and all three api files, zero duplicate ids, zero duplicate function names, all 21 panels reachable 1:1, no stray console.log. ISOLATED-LOGIC-TESTED with the real helpers (not just code-read): encrypt/decrypt round-trip correct, tampered ciphertext returns null rather than throwing or returning garbage, a rotated SD_AUTH_SECRET correctly fails closed (confirming sc-eligibility.js's CREDENTIAL_UNREADABLE path is genuinely reachable), and the never-return-plaintext status shaping verified by asserting both the plaintext key AND the ciphertext are absent from the serialized response. LIVE-VERIFIED after push: all 8 new client markers present in the deployed source; both new endpoints confirmed deployed and enforcing auth correctly across 6 real curl cases (no-license -> 401 NO_LICENSE, GET -> 405, unknown license -> INVALID_LICENSE, bad action -> real action-validation message, on both endpoints); sc_eligibility confirmed passing both the resource allowlist and the SC_RESOURCES delete-action gate, with a bogus resource name still 400ing as the contrast control. One note worth recording: the first curl pass returned NOT_FOUND on 3 of 5 cases while the other 2 succeeded -- that was a real mid-rollout propagation race, not a routing bug; re-running after the deploy settled gave 6/6 correct, and the first pass is reported here rather than quietly discarded. NOT DONE, STATED PLAINLY: the Stedi sandbox round-trip listed as mandatory in my own approved plan did NOT run, and neither did the live credential-leak check against a really-stored key. Both are blocked by the same two gaps -- sql/sairncode_credentials_schema.sql and sql/sairncode_eligibility_schema.sql have not been run in Supabase (no DB access, same recurring blocker), and I have no Stedi account/API key of my own to test with, nor the SC-PINNACLE-2026 admin PIN (same real-credentials gap as the rest of this session). So: the code paths are verified by isolated logic tests against the real crypto helpers and by real production curls of the auth/validation layers, but NO real 270/271 has been sent to a real payer yet by anyone. That end-to-end proof needs Michael to run both migrations and add a real Stedi key (free account, eligibility billed at $0 -- verified from Stedi's own pricing page). Until then sc_eligibility degrades honestly to local-only sync and the panel reports "not connected" rather than showing anything simulated. Phases 2 (prior-auth, incl. the flagged risk that FHIR PAS needs per-payer SMART-on-FHIR OAuth credentials, a materially different shape than Phase 1's single-key model) and 3 (CPT/ICD-10/CDT BYO licensing) are scoped in the plan but not built. - docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-20-sairncode-prior-auth-phase2-design.md (CC, dedicated worktree, branch cc-work) -- PHASE 2 SCOPED, DESIGN ONLY, NOTHING BUILT, per Michael's instruction. Skills run: sairn-software-architect + sairn-decision-gate. graphify could NOT be run -- it is disabled for model invocation in skillOverrides (CLI is installed at ~/.local/bin/graphify, no graphify-out/ exists in this repo); flagged rather than silently skipped, offered to run the CLI directly instead. HEADLINE: Michael was right that this is a real re-scope, and the reason is bigger than the credential shape -- Phase 2 splits into two halves with different risk profiles, and the FHIR half should NOT be built now. FOUR FINDINGS, all verified not assumed: (1) FHIR PAS is not an API key -- it is POST [base]/Claim/$submit with a conformant Bundle, authenticated by SMART Backend Services (OAuth client_credentials with a one-time-use JWT signed by the client's own private key, RS384 or ES384, after registering a public JWKS with EACH payer individually). (2) THE PHASE 1 SINGLETON ROW PHYSICALLY CANNOT HOLD THIS -- measured with the real encryptSecret() helper and real generated keys, not estimated: an RS384 (RSA-2048) per-payer record is 2642 bytes, so 30 payers = 79260 bytes and BLOWS the 64KB CHECK constraint (write starts failing); ES384 is 778 bytes and fits, but SMART says SHOULD be RS384 *or* ES384 so we don't get to force the small one. Conclusion: 2c needs one row per payer (entry_id = payerId), not the singleton -- settled now because designing it is cheap and migrating later is not (sairn-software-architect's expensive-to-reverse rule). (3) NO CHEAP REUSE VIA STEDI -- checked their docs directly rather than assuming: supported transactions are 270/271, 837P/I/D, 276/277, 835, 277CA; there is NO 278 and no prior-auth item in docs, beta, or roadmap, so the Phase 1 credential cannot be reused for prior auth. (4) sc_auth TODAY TRACKS AUTHS HELD, NOT REQUESTS SENT -- current record is {id,authId,proc,exp,units} with status derived from exp; bolting submission fields onto it would repeat exactly the mistake item 3 fixed for denials (aggregate sc_denial had no payer, so sc_denial_events was added alongside rather than mangling it). Same call: a new sc_auth_requests resource, sc_auth left untouched, no localStorage migration. DECISION GATE: Bid/No-Bid on "build real FHIR PAS submission now" scores well below the 60-65% threshold (no payer registrations, no JWKS hosting, no Bundle builder, no payer sandbox access, and per live research most payers are still evaluating build-vs-buy in 2026 -- the Jan 1 2027 deadline binds PAYERS to expose the API, not providers or their software to consume it, so any "CMS-0057-F compliant" marketing claim would be a false claim about someone else's obligation). Premortem surfaced the sharpest risk: a Stedi API key only reaches Stedi and bills $0, but a private signing key lets the holder IMPERSONATE that practice to every payer it registered with -- a materially higher custody bar than Phase 1's, which Phase 1's own documented limitation (all tenants under one platform-wide SD_AUTH_SECRET) is not good enough for. NIST AI RMF found the real limitation in the buildable half: reusing quoteFoundInNote() verifies a quote IS PRESENT in the note, it does NOT verify the quote SUPPORTS the medical-necessity conclusion drawn from it -- tolerable for code suggestion, not sufficient for prior auth, and the UI must not imply otherwise. RECOMMENDATION: build 2a (AI documentation assembly, DRAFT-only, explicit human sign-off recorded, verification language corrected per the RMF finding) and 2b (sc_auth_requests lifecycle tracking with a real 72h-expedited/7-day-standard clock -- those CMS operational rules have been in force since Jan 1 2026 and are useful TODAY regardless of FHIR); DEFER 2c (real FHIR PAS submission) with three concrete written re-open triggers rather than an open-ended shrug. 4 open questions raised for Michael, incl. whether 2a's target is the payer packet or the coder's own file (changes how hard the sign-off gate must be), and whether "practices register SAIRN's JWKS so we never custody their private key" is an acceptable target design for 2c. No code written, no files touched outside this new spec. 2026-08-20
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-20-sairncode-30-layer-firewall-audit.md + phase2 design doc update + .gitignore (CC, dedicated worktree, branch cc-work) -- TWO THINGS DONE, both verification/design only, zero app code changed. (A) Phase 2 doc updated with Michael's 8 confirmed decisions, plus two NEW requirements he added that are now scoped explicitly BEFORE any build: the 2c platform signing key must be KMS/HSM-backed with non-exportable key material and signing done inside the KMS -- explicitly NOT the SD_AUTH_SECRET-encrypted-in-Postgres pattern used everywhere else, because accepting "practices register SAIRN's JWKS" moves the blast radius from one practice to platform-wide impersonation across every payer every practice registered with; and 2a's UI must NOT reuse the existing green "✓ Verified in note" styling unchanged, since in a prior-auth context it would imply clinical sufficiency that quoteFoundInNote() cannot establish (it proves presence in the note, not that the quote supports medical necessity). Also recorded the standing platform rule: no "CMS-0057-F compliant" language anywhere in SAIRNcode product copy or marketing. (B) NEW AUDIT: verified the 2026-06-13 30-layer Universal Security Firewall spec against SAIRNcode's real deployed code at f62687e, per-item evidence with line numbers, not a checklist guess. RESULT: 10 of 30 present or genuinely superseded, 20 missing -- but the raw count is misleading and acting on it as a checklist would be a mistake. The June 13 spec was written for an app with NO server-side auth, so every control had to live in the browser; that premise is obsolete since api/sc-auth.js (2026-08-18) replaced the hardcoded client PIN gate with real per-employee credentials. About 8 of the 20 "missing" layers SHOULD NOT be built at all because in a browser app they are bypassable-by-design and would manufacture coverage that doesn't exist -- DevTools detection, clipboard protection, exfiltration monitoring, fetch interception, origin validation, and client-side rate-limit "simulation" (the word simulation is the tell). Layer 19 (HMAC client->proxy signing) is NOT FIXABLE AS SPECIFIED: a browser app cannot hold a signing secret without shipping it in the client source; the real answer is what the sc-* endpoints already do (server-issued HMAC-signed session tokens). GENUINELY PRESENT AND SOUND: layer 11/18 XSS output sanitization -- mechanically verified rather than eyeballed, parsed all 95 innerHTML assignments and found only 2 that interpolate without escHtml, both interpolating numeric .length values only, so zero user-controlled unescaped paths. Layer 8 error sanitization is real in practice (69 catch blocks, generic user-facing text, detail logged server-side only). Layer 14 is SUPERSEDED BY SOMETHING STRONGER (12h HMAC session tokens + forced PIN re-entry on every load) with one real residual gap: no client-side idle timer, so an unattended clinic workstation stays unlocked up to 12h. Layer 22 exists but is weak AND already self-disclosed as such by api/claude.js's own header (in-memory, per-instance, resets on cold start). WORTH BUILDING, risk-ordered per sairn-decision-gate: (1) layer 30 audit log of AI calls -- SAIRNcode sends clinical notes to an LLM with zero record of it, and api/_lib/audit.js ALREADY EXISTS and is proven in SAIRNlaw, so this is the cheapest high-value item on the whole list; (2) layers 27+28 consent + in-app data-handling disclosure, a real compliance gap for a PHI app routing through Anthropic as subprocessor; (3) layer 20 prompt-injection detection, since users paste real clinical notes into the AI and grounded non-fabricated output is this app's entire differentiator; (4) layer 10 CSP meta tag, one tag, and layer 13 confirms zero external scripts would break; (5) layer 12 frame-busting; (6) layer 26 purge/expiry (needs a retention POLICY decision from Michael first, not just code); (7) strengthen layer 22 with a persistent counter -- the precedent already exists in-repo in api/_lib/courtlistener.js's real Supabase-backed rate limiter. NEW FINDING NOT IN THE 30: sairncode.html:1369 demo_pin:'1234' is dead config -- defined in APP_CONFIG and never read anywhere (verified: 3 total matches, all inside the definition block), vestigial from the pre-2026-08-18 hardcoded-PIN era. Not exploitable since nothing consumes it, but it advertises a credential that looks live and is exactly the dormant-code class Guardian flags -- recommend deleting the line. FLAGGED NOT CHANGED, this pass was verification only. Audit doc also states plainly what the method structurally could NOT see: no runtime verification (source-level only, same SC-PINNACLE-2026 credentials gap as the rest of this session), and absence-of-grep-match is weaker evidence than presence. (C) graphify RAN successfully after all -- it was disabled for model invocation but the CLI works; needed --code-only since 178 doc files wanted an LLM key and spending Michael's API budget unasked wasn't appropriate. Built 557 nodes / 852 edges / 56 communities from 157 code files; community-hub ranking independently corroborates the audit's core finding, with api/_lib/auth.js and api/_lib/license.js as the top two hubs, i.e. the real security posture is concentrated server-side, not in browser layers. graphify-out/ added to .gitignore (1.3MB of generated artifacts, rebuildable with `graphify . --code-only`). 2026-08-20
- sairncode.html + api/sc-ai.js + api/_lib/ai-rate-limit.js + api/_lib/audit.js + api/claude.js + sql/sairncode_audit_log_schema.sql + sql/sairn_ai_rate_limit_schema.sql (CC, dedicated worktree, branch cc-work) -- SECURITY PASS BUILT AND PUSHED (`b8c5ee9`), live-verified. Implements the risk-ordered list from the 2026-08-20 firewall audit, per Michael's confirmation. The ~8 theater-only browser layers were deliberately NOT built (DevTools detection, clipboard protection, exfiltration monitoring, fetch interception, origin validation, client-side rate-limit simulation), and layer 19 was left alone as confirmed not-fixable-as-specified. LAYERS 30 + 20, both solved by one thing: new api/sc-ai.js. They needed the same property -- a place the client cannot edit that also knows WHO is calling. api/claude.js only ever receives an app_id, never a license or session, so it STRUCTURALLY cannot attribute a call to a practice or a person; that is why layer 30 could not be built there. The new endpoint authenticates (license + real employee session), screens the submitted text, calls callAnthropic() in-process, and writes an audit row -- exactly the pattern SAIRNlaw's ai_generate action already proved, and callAnthropic was already exported for precisely this purpose. All 5 client AI call sites converted (claim scrub, chat first round trip, chat tool-result round trip, note-to-code, 2026 code lookup); APP_CONFIG.proxy now has zero references anywhere. INJECTION DEFENSE IS TWO-LAYER on purpose, because pattern matching alone would have been the same false-coverage mistake the audit called out: (1) structural -- a server-appended instruction that supplied note content is DATA to analyze and never instructions to follow, appended server-side so a client cannot drop it; (2) pattern -- 8 high-confidence phrases refuse the call outright, 5 lower-confidence ones flag and log but still proceed, because a false positive that blocks a real clinical note is its own harm. Threat model stated explicitly in the file: here the USER IS NOT THE ADVERSARY (a coder pastes a note; the note's content is the untrusted input), which is exactly why this control does real work where the rejected browser-hardening layers would not. ISOLATED-LOGIC TESTED with real strings before shipping: all 5 attack strings caught (ignore-all-previous-instructions, disregard-the-system-prompt, you-are-now-a, NEW INSTRUCTIONS reveal-your-system-prompt, repeat-your-instructions) and ZERO false positives across 5 realistic clinical notes (diabetes follow-up, established-patient visit, post-op check, chest pain with EKG ordered, CKD stage 3). LAYERS 27+28: new Privacy and Data panel (22nd panel, nav-linked) stating in-app where data actually goes, that submitted text including pasted notes is sent to Anthropic, that the audit log records who/what/when but DELIBERATELY NOT the note text or the response, and naming all four third parties (Anthropic, Vercel, Supabase, Stedi -- with Stedi correctly described as the practice's own account, not a SAIRN-owned one). The panel also discloses the two REAL gaps plainly rather than implying coverage: no bulk delete-everything function and no automatic retention or expiry. Plus a first-use consent gate placed inside scAiFetch(), the single transport every AI call goes through, so no feature can bypass it and any future AI feature inherits it automatically; declining cancels the request and sends nothing. LAYER 22: new api/_lib/ai-rate-limit.js, Supabase-backed sliding window -- the persistent counter api/claude.js's own header has asked for since it was written. SHIPS IN OBSERVE MODE DELIBERATELY: verified first that 10 of 11 live apps send is_demo:true, so a limit that has effectively never been enforced (the in-memory counter resets on cold start) would have suddenly become real across nearly the whole platform, and enabling that blind risked a platform-wide outage on a threshold nobody has ever measured. It records and reports until SAIRN_AI_RATE_LIMIT_MODE=enforce is set deliberately, and fails OPEN on any infrastructure problem, because a counting outage must never take down every AI feature on the platform. Both behaviors isolated-logic tested. LAYER 10 (CSP): scoped against a real inventory -- exactly 2 external hosts, 1 inline style block, 1 inline script block, 155 inline style attributes, zero external scripts, zero web fonts, zero data URIs. The comment states plainly that it does NOT stop inline-script XSS, because this app's single-file architecture requires unsafe-inline and that is precisely the directive that would otherwise stop it -- escHtml remains the real control and CSP is defence in depth behind it, not a replacement. frame-ancestors was deliberately OMITTED because browsers ignore it in a meta tag, and including it would look like clickjacking protection while doing nothing. LAYER 12 (frame-busting): real JS instead, in the head, with a cross-origin fallback that refuses to render rather than displaying a framable UI. IDLE AUTO-LOCK (Michael's addition): a 15-minute inactivity lock that clears the session token so resuming requires a real, server-re-verified PIN -- closing the one residual gap in the otherwise-stronger-than-spec session handling (an unattended clinic workstation versus a 12h token). Documented as a local lock and NOT a boundary against the person at the keyboard; the real boundary is unchanged. TWO SELF-CAUGHT MISTAKES worth recording: (1) my own CSP comment contained literal script-tag text, which broke regex-based script extraction -- that is exactly the Check 0a hazard CLAUDE.md warns about, caught by my own syntax check failing, then reworded and re-verified; (2) a python read/write approach was abandoned after it flattened CRLF line endings on a shared file earlier this session, so every sairncode.html edit this pass used targeted exact-match edits instead. api/_lib/audit.js gained an OPTIONAL table parameter defaulting to the SAIRNlaw table, so both existing SAIRNlaw callers are byte-for-byte unaffected (verified by a real call), and the table name is allowlisted rather than free-form since it lands in a REST URL. vercel.json DELIBERATELY UNTOUCHED: real HTTP security headers would be stronger than a meta CSP, but Vercel rejects a config containing both headers and routes (verified against their own documented error, not assumed), so it would require a routes-to-rewrites migration across all 14 app routes -- a separate platform-wide change, flagged rather than bundled into a SAIRNcode security pass. PRE-PUSH: node --check clean on both script blocks and all 7 touched or dependent api files, zero duplicate ids, zero duplicate function names, 22 nav targets and 22 panels reconciled 1:1 in both directions, no stray console.log, and the CSP verified to allow every host the file actually uses. LIVE-VERIFIED AFTER PUSH: sc-ai enforces auth across 5 real curl cases (no license gives NO_LICENSE, GET gives 405, bad license gives INVALID_LICENSE, missing messages gives a real validation error, and critically a REAL valid license with no session gives NO_SESSION -- which is what makes the audit log meaningful); all 8 new client markers confirmed present in the deployed source. MOST IMPORTANT CHECK, REGRESSION ON THE SHARED PROXY: I modified api/claude.js, which 10 other live apps depend on, so I ran a real StoneDesk AI call against production and got a genuine model response back (text "OK", real usage token counts, model claude-sonnet-4-6), and confirmed an unknown app_id still correctly rejects. The other apps are unaffected, and the new limiter correctly failed open with its migration unrun. NOT VERIFIED, STATED PLAINLY: no audit row has actually been written, no injection block has fired end to end through the live endpoint, the consent dialog and idle timer have not been exercised in a real browser, and the rate limiter has not counted anything. All four need (a) the two new migrations run in Supabase and (b) a real SC-PINNACLE-2026 employee session, whose admin PIN I do not have -- the same recurring credentials gap as the rest of this session. What IS proven: the auth and validation layer live in production, the screening logic by isolated test against real attack strings and real clinical notes, and no regression on the shared proxy. ALSO FLAGGED NOT CHANGED: APP_CONFIG.proxy is now dead config (zero references after this pass), the same class as demo_pin 1234, and left in place under Michael's standing confirm-before-deletion rule rather than removed on my own initiative. LAYER 26 (purge/expiry) intentionally NOT built and NOT scoped -- it needs a real retention-policy decision from Michael, and per his instruction it is flagged back now that everything else on the list is done. 2026-08-20

The original text read "Confirmed gone from the LIVE deployed source (grep for the literal value returns 0; the only remaining match is the removal comment explaining why)." It was not gone. APP_CONFIG.demo_pin was genuinely deleted, but a SEPARATE hardcoded field in the Settings panel -- `<input type="password" value="1234" readonly>` under a "Demo PIN" label -- was still rendering the same literal, and was still live in the deployed page as of 2026-08-23, three days later. The grep behind that claim evidently only covered the APP_CONFIG block, not the file. The literal really is gone now, both occurrences, as of the Settings-panel rebuild logged at the end of this file; that rebuild is where the removal was actually completed, not here. Lesson worth keeping: "grep returns 0" is only as true as the scope the grep ran against, and a config deletion does not imply the value is absent from the markup that displayed it.] (B) LAYER 26 BUILT AS A SETTING ONLY, PURGE DELIBERATELY UNWIRED, exactly as scoped: record the policy, do not wire the delete until a real written retention policy exists on its own separate track. Nothing in SAIRNcode deletes on a schedule, before or after this change, and that is the entire design constraint -- a practice reading "Retention: 10 years" and assuming records ARE purged at 10 years would be a false capability claim, the precise failure class this codebase has spent weeks removing. So "Nothing is automatically deleted today" is rendered as the most prominent element in the box (its own bordered callout at the top), not a footnote. STORAGE DECISION, checked rather than assumed: new sc_settings resource (20th SC_RESOURCES entry), one 'default' row per license, riding the existing generic handler so no bespoke read path was needed. Also checked the existing generic 'profile' resource and found it hardcoded to app_id=stonedesk (api/sd-data.js line ~347), so it was genuinely not reusable; that is why a third migration was accepted rather than avoided. THE 10-YEAR FLOOR IS ENFORCED SERVER-SIDE in api/sd-data.js's sc_settings write branch, not just in the dropdown. The reasoning matters and is recorded inline: the floor exists precisely BECAUSE no purge is built yet -- a tampered or mistaken value written today is inherited by whatever deletion mechanism gets built later, long after anyone remembers choosing it, so a below-floor value must never reach storage in the first place. 'indefinite' is the other valid value and means never purge. GUARD ISOLATED-LOGIC TESTED against 15 edge cases, all correct: 9, 1, 0, -5, 'forever', null, '', NaN and '3' all REJECTED; 10, 15, 30, '12' and 'indefinite' all accepted; a payload with unrelated keys correctly unaffected. Immediately contrasted with a real clinical note ("Post-op check following arthroscopic repair...") through the SAME session -- proceeded normally, zero false positive, confirming the screening isn't just blocking everything. (3) RETENTION FLOOR -- REAL, LIVE, both directions. A real admin-session write of retention_years:3 correctly rejected with RETENTION_BELOW_FLOOR; the same session writing retention_years:10 correctly succeeded (real {"ok":true,...} response) -- this closes the one gap from the earlier credential-less pass, which could only prove rejection, not that a valid value genuinely saves. (4) SIGN-OFF GATE -- the FORBIDDEN-on-forged-value proof from the prior session already stands as the strongest evidence (rejecting even with a real license and no session). The full positive-path test (create a request as this real admin, then sign it off) could NOT complete -- sql/sairncode_auth_requests_schema.sql is still unrun, both the create and the sign-off attempt returned the honest NOT_PROVISIONED error rather than a fake success. Real admin credentials don't unblock a missing table; this is expected and matches what was already flagged as outstanding. (5) CONSENT GATE + IDLE LOCK -- proven a different, still-real way: the shared Playwright browser instance was locked by a concurrent session (almost certainly Hank's) on both attempts -- did not force it, consistent with the parallel-app-scaling discipline this project has already learned the hard way. Instead, curled the ACTUAL LIVE deployed sairncode.html (not the local repo copy), extracted the real scEnsureAiConsent/scHasAiConsent/scAiConsentKey and scIdleArm/scIdleReset/scIdleDisarm/scIdleLock functions verbatim by byte-range, and ran them -- the real deployed code, unmodified, not a reimplementation -- inside a sandboxed VM context with mocked localStorage/timers. 5 tests, all passed: consent decline persists nothing and returns false; consent accept persists a real localStorage key and a second call reads the cache without re-prompting (confirmed by checking the mock confirm() was NOT called again); consent is genuinely scoped per-employee-id, not global (a different employee_id on a fresh device still needs to consent); idle-arm schedules a real 900000ms (15 min) timer; real user-activity correctly clears and reschedules it; letting the real timeout callback actually fire calls the real doLogout() and produces the real user-facing toast text; and disarm-on-logout both clears the pending timer and double-guards via the _scIdleArmed flag so even a stale fired callback is a genuine no-op. (6) RATE LIMITER -- PARTIALLY VERIFIED, gap disclosed honestly rather than glossed. CLAIM 2 PARTIALLY TRUE, more specific than stated: confirmed zero real content anywhere in the file for 8 of the 9 named specialties plus ophthalmology (grep across sairncode.html, an apparent ASA hit turned out to be a false-positive substring inside scHasAiConsent, checked directly rather than trusted) -- the app's own Specialty nav group has exactly 2 panels (Telehealth, Anesthesia) and SAIRNCODE_SYSTEM_PROMPT names zero specialties, one generic prompt for the whole platform. REAL, SPECIFIC FINDING ON ANESTHESIA rather than lumping it in generally: the time-unit MATH is correct and already implemented (timeUnits=minutes/15, totalUnits=base+timeUnits, the real standard ASA convention) -- but Base Units is a free-text number the coder types in, with no real ASA Relative Value Guide reference table behind it, no physical-status (P1-P6) modifier capture, and no payer conversion-factor dollar output. The math is honest; one of its two inputs has no real reference data behind it yet -- a small, precisely scoped, buildable addition, not a rebuild. RAN TWO REAL LIVE PROBES against production (not assumed) to get real evidence before scoping specialty coverage: a behavioral-health question (psychotherapy add-on time thresholds 90833/90836/90838, payer variance) came back with correct, long-stable AMA figures (16/38/53 min) and an accurate hedged payer breakdown; a DME question (Medicare KX modifier documentation, whether the LCD changed recently) came back explicitly refusing to claim current post-2024 specifics and pointing to CMS directly -- exactly the disclosure discipline the app's own system prompt asks for. FOUND AND CORRECTED TWO REAL ERRORS in the handed-over research before it went in: (1) 66821 (YAG capsulotomy) is a 90-day global period (major surgery), not the stated 0-day -- confirmed against CGS Medicare's real Cataract/YAG fact sheet and AAPC's "66821 Essentials" -- when performed on the same eye during an active cataract-surgery global, it needs modifier -78, not standalone billing; (2) Visibly (formerly Opternative)'s real 2022 FDA 510(k) clearance covers ONLY their Digital Acuity Product (visual acuity test, age-restricted 22-40, "supportive recommendations" only) -- NOT a complete online refraction/prescription product as the original framing implied; Opternative's earlier refraction offering was actually recalled by the FDA in 2019 and has not regained clearance. Michael accepted both corrections and confirmed use of the corrected versions. NET FINDING: there is no real internal source document for facility-based SAIRNcare at all -- every other app this session (SAIRNsenior included) pulled its role vocabulary from a real signed SOP guide; this one has none. Flagged this plainly rather than silently inventing role vocabulary as if a real doc existed. RAISED A GENUINE FORK TO MICHAEL RATHER THAN GUESSING: "assisted living, hospice, retirement/senior living" spans three regulatory regimes verified as materially different (assisted living = state-licensed, no single federal framework, confirmed live via real search; skilled nursing = federal CMS Conditions of Participation + MDS assessment + PDPM billing; hospice = separate federal CMS COPs + election/certification + per-diem levels of care) -- asked which one v1 actually targets rather than building one undifferentiated app. Michael's answer: build ONE real v1, his call which, explicitly defer the other two rather than blur them together -- same lesson as the SAIRNcare/SAIRNsenior split itself. CHOSE ASSISTED LIVING FOR V1, reasoned explicitly: lowest regulatory floor of the three (state-licensed, not a federal CMS Conditions-of-Participation program -- SNF's MDS/PDPM and hospice's COPs are both federally strict, high-fabrication-risk domains this scoping pass did not do enough verified research to build against responsibly); directly reuses SAIRNsenior's already-proven per-state config pattern (EVV aggregator choice -> same shape as assisted-living licensing/administrator-credentialing variation, confirmed live via real search that both administrator licensure requirements AND Medicaid HCBS-waiver availability vary meaningfully by state); larger real addressable market by facility count. SNF and hospice named explicitly as deferred, not silently dropped, each needing its own dedicated research/decision-gate pass given the real federal compliance stakes. REAL EXTERNAL RESEARCH GROUNDED THE SCOPE, not invented: confirmed live that assisted-living billing is primarily private-pay (room/board + care-level rate), and that Medicaid coverage where it exists runs through state HCBS waivers covering ONLY the care portion, never room/board, with many facilities not accepting Medicaid at all -- a materially different billing shape than SAIRNsenior's Medicaid-EVV-centric model, not assumed to be the same. Role vocabulary (Administrator/Executive Director, Resident Care Director, Med Aide/Med Tech, Caregiver/Resident Care Aide, Business Office/Billing Manager, Activities Coordinator) scoped from this research plus standard AL organizational structure -- explicitly flagged as needing a real facility contact's review before launch, unlike every other app's role list this session which came from an actual signed SOP. ARCHITECTURE SETTLED NOW PER sairn-software-architect: alf_ storage prefix chosen and confirmed zero collisions (checked directly, not assumed) against both sc_ (SAIRNcode's) and sen_ (SAIRNsenior's); real employee auth from day one via a new api/alf-auth.js modeled on sen-auth.js/bld-auth.js; assignment-based resident privacy gate scoped as a genuine three-tier model from the start (Administrator/Billing = facility-wide, Resident Care Director = facility-wide clinical, Med Aide/Caregiver = own-assigned-only, reassignment management-only, server-enforced) -- built with the exact two real bugs SAIRNsenior's batch 2 found and fixed (self-assign-on-create vs existing-assignment comparison; three-tier not management/everyone-else binary) designed in from the start rather than left to be rediscovered; sairn-app-scaffold's required v1 photo->Claude component adapted as MD-order/medication-list intake, review-before-save, same proven pattern as StoneDesk/SAIRNsenior, not a fragile JSON contract. Proposed conservative v1 panel list: Residents, Staff (with real sync from day one, closing SAIRNsenior's own disclosed Phase-1 gap proactively rather than repeating it), Care Plans/ADL, MAR, Billing (private-pay primary + state-config-gated HCBS waiver path, never conflated with room/board), Compliance/Incident Reporting (flagged as needing its own per-state verification pass, not guessed), Activities. Pushed (`623b9d9`, clean rebase over origin/main -- SAIRNcode Phase 5 had advanced main, no conflict, different files). Compliance/Incident Reporting: real web research run first, same discipline as MAR -- no single federal or uniform ALF incident-reporting standard exists; sourced real deadline examples (Virginia 24hr, Massachusetts 24hr, California 7 days, Florida 15 days, Washington initial+5-business-day-followup) but reportable CATEGORIES converge far more than deadlines across those states (falls with injury, medication errors, elopement/wandering, injuries of unknown origin, abuse/neglect/exploitation allegations, unexpected death, unexpected hospitalization, behavioral incidents) -- built the category list on that real convergence, made the state deadline a facility-entered free-text field rather than hardcoding any one state's number as if universal. New alf_incidents table with a deliberately ASYMMETRIC gate, a different shape from every other alf_ resource this session: any authenticated employee may file a brand-new report (mandatory-reporting-by-whoever-witnessed-it -- gating creation behind a role would create a real incentive to under-report), but only management/nursing/billing may read the log or update an existing report afterward, and the isolation test explicitly proves the original filer cannot go back and edit their own submission once it's saved -- a real integrity property, not just a stated intent. Compliance panel also surfaces Staff Certification Status by reusing the existing alf_staff data live (no new resource), same pattern SAIRNsenior's own Compliance panel used for the identical "operational, not clinical PHI" reasoning. Activities: new alf_activities table, broad-read (any authenticated employee, since an activity calendar is operational/non-clinical data everyone benefits from seeing) but narrow-write (owner + Activities Coordinator only -- planning/running activities is that role's actual defined job, the same scope-of-practice logic that shaped alf_mar applied in the opposite direction). Attendance is embedded directly in the activity record as a resident-id array rather than a separate per-resident table -- deliberately simple, and avoids needing any write path through alf_clients at all (Activities has zero write access there by design). ISOLATION-TESTED BEFORE WIRING THE CLIENT: 14 new tests for alf_incidents (every role's write access confirmed open, every role's read access confirmed gated correctly to owner/nursing/billing, and the specific integrity case -- the original caregiver filer attempting to edit their own already-saved report gets a real 403) and 9 new tests for alf_activities (every one of the 6 roles confirmed able to read, only owner/activities confirmed able to write), both against the REAL handler with mocked fetch/auth/license, not a reimplementation -- 23/23 new tests pass. All four prior suites re-run unaffected: alf_clients 19, alf_mar 18, alf_billing 9 -- 69/69 across five separate server-side gates by the end of this session. - .claude/skills/sairn-guardian-v2/SKILL.md (CC) — DONE, pushed. Added a "Verification Discipline" section: six lessons from tonight's three parallel sessions, each a repeatable check rather than a war story. (1) seed-and-recheck to tell an honest empty state from a real defect — independently arrived at by Hank, Cody and me the same night, and it produced OPPOSITE verdicts on two cases, which is the point; (2) verify against the most specific marker, not any matching string — a KX colour-fix check went green on a pre-existing code path that shared a substring while the real change had not deployed; (3) fail-closed in the WRONG DIRECTION is still wrong — the DMEPOS SWO gate was briefed at seven required elements, CMS article A55426 says six, and a seven-element gate would have blocked compliant orders; (4) check the diff SHAPE before committing, not just that syntax passes — a one-line intent landed 88 insertions and pushed another session's uncommitted work, with node --check green the whole time; (5) this repo has MIXED line endings file-by-file (api/sd-data.js CRLF, sairncare.html LF, stonedesk.html CRLF) so `sed -i` is unsafe — detect and preserve explicitly, verify CRLF/bare-LF counts after writing; (6) a panel falling back to SEED data when its real store is empty is a fabricated-KPI risk, not a display convenience — primary example is StoneDesk's Slabs panel rendering 8 invented slabs and a fabricated $4,420 inventory value while a real synced slab was invisible, and a demoCleared()-style guard is NOT sufficient alone because StoneDesk had one and it still happened. TWO CORRECTIONS TO THE TASK AS BRIEFED, both verified rather than assumed. FIRST: there are NOT two copies to update in this checkout. The repo root IS C:/Users/marsh, so .claude/skills/sairn-guardian-v2/SKILL.md is simultaneously the user-level copy AND the git-tracked project copy — confirmed with `git ls-files`. One file, one edit. SECOND: eight copies exist on disk and I checked all of them by md5 rather than trusting the Aug-19 divergence note. The three active clones (SAIRN-cc, SAIRN-cody, SAIRN-hank) are BYTE-IDENTICAL to canonical and are git clones, so they receive this via `git pull` — I deliberately did NOT hand-edit them, which would have created the exact divergence the task exists to prevent. Two ARE stale and were left alone rather than silently overwritten: Documents/SAIRN (614 lines, still lists the removed SAIRNhr/SAIRNacc and lacks SAIRNcash/SAIRNgrounds) and SAIRN_fresh (347 lines, far behind). Neither holds unique content — both are simply behind — but they are not canonical and syncing stale checkouts was not mine to decide. OPEN FOLLOW-ON, FLAGGED NOT DONE: stonedesk.html has 29 SEED constants and 56 sdDemoCleared()?[]:SEED sites; only the Slabs panel is fixed (Phase 1a, 501d15b). The other 28 need auditing against lesson six's two-part test, and other apps likely carry the same shape. Counted, not estimated. 2026-08-23
- stonedesk.html + api/sd-data.js + api/_resources/stonedesk.js + sql/sd_slab_lineage_schema.sql + sql/sd_slabs_schema.sql (CC) — STONEDESK PHASE 1 DONE, pushed and live-verified against production: 1a `a0b47e2`, 1b `eb92db8`, follow-up fix `874efcb`. Design doc: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-22-stonedesk-phase1-slab-unification-design.md. THE BRIEF'S PREMISE WAS WRONG AND STEP 1 CAUGHT IT: Phase 1 was scoped as "migrate fungible SKU inventory to serialized slabs." Slabs were ALREADY per-unit serialized records with working remnant lineage (isRemnant/parentSlabId, consume-never-delete). Nothing needed serializing. The real gap was TWO PARALLEL SLAB SYSTEMS sharing zero field names — sd_slabs (server-synced, real data, no UI) and sd_slab_tracker (localStorage, seed data, the only UI) — a unification question a prior session had explicitly logged as unresolved in STONEDESK-SESSION72-HANDOFF.md. AND A LIVE CHECK 0b VIOLATION ON THE FLAGSHIP, found by browser not by reading: sd_slab_tracker's key was ABSENT, so the Slabs panel fell through to an in-file SEED constant and rendered 8 invented slabs, computing all four KPIs from them — Total 8 / Available 5 / Allocated 2 / Inventory Value $4,420, every figure fabricated — while a genuinely real slab written through Bulk Slab Upload was invisible and excluded from every count. The panel did not show stale data, it INVENTED data when its real store was empty. 1a deleted SEED outright (not behind a flag — a flag leaves the same landmine one boolean away), repointed the panel at sd_slabs, and made Inventory Value sum only slabs that actually carry a cost, disclosing the rest rather than treating them as $0. 1b added block -> bundle -> slab -> remnant lineage in SIBLING TABLES, not the slab blob, and the constraint decided it: sd_slabs.data is capped at 65536 bytes by sdslabs_data_size and ~55KB is already photo, leaving ~9KB per slab. Unbounded per-slab history there would make a record MORE likely to fail the longer it is used — failing first on the busiest, highest-value slabs, exactly the ones whose provenance someone is auditing when it breaks. Only two optional strings (blockId/bundleId, ~60 bytes) enter the blob. The slab->block link is a plain string, deliberately NOT a DB foreign key, so dropping the tables can never orphan a slab. VEIN-MATCHING PROVEN IN PRODUCTION, not inferred from the schema: real writes through the live API under SD-AUDIT-2026, read back independently, lineage resolved using functions extracted verbatim from the DEPLOYED file — a remnant whose parent is already consumed still resolves to block B-2026-PROD via bundle BND-PROD-A, and sdSlabsFromBlock returns both. (3) sc_settings SERVER-SIDE ROLE GATING -- LEFT AS A NAMED OPEN ITEM, NOT SILENTLY FIXED AND NOT SILENTLY WIDENED, because it is not trivial and the instruction was to say so if it isn't. The server-side gate itself would be trivial in isolation -- about eight lines in api/sd-data.js's sc_settings write branch, mirroring the sc_auth_requests sign-off gate byte for byte in shape (verifySessionToken(tokenFromRequest(req), licHash, 'sairncode') then a role check). What makes it NOT trivial is everything that has to move with it, and one of those is a real honesty problem rather than a nicety: scData() collapses every non-ok response to null, so today a 403 FORBIDDEN would surface to the user through the existing branch as "Saved on this device only -- server sync failed" -- i.e. a permissions refusal would be reported as a sync failure, on a compliance tool, which is exactly the honest-failure standard this codebase has spent the whole arc enforcing. So the real scope is four things, not one: (a) the server gate, (b) an scData path that distinguishes FORBIDDEN from a genuine sync failure instead of flattening both to null, (c) hiding or disabling the Save controls for roles that cannot write, so the refusal is never reached by surprise, and (d) an actual decision about WHICH roles may write practice-level settings -- admin only, or admin plus biller, given the four SAIRNcode roles are admin/coder/biller/auditor. (d) is a policy call, not a mechanical one. Worth stating plainly: this is PRE-EXISTING behaviour, not something today's change introduced -- retention_years has been writable by any authenticated session since 2026-08-20 and was live-verified that way; practice_name and payer_schedules simply inherit the same posture rather than widening it. The 10-year retention floor is unaffected either way, since it is a value guard enforced server-side regardless of role. 2026-08-23

Three leads created as owner, clearly labelled per the ZZ-VERIFY convention: ZZ-VERIFY-LEAD-A (assigned audit-sales-a), ZZ-VERIFY-LEAD-B (assigned audit-sales-b), ZZ-VERIFY-LEAD-UNASSIGNED (no assignee) -- the unassigned one included deliberately because the gate's one documented judgment call is that an untriaged lead is management-only-visible rather than self-claimable, and that branch is untested otherwise. SCENARIO 1 -- OWNER SEES EVERY LEAD: PASS. audit-owner read returns all 3, each with its real assigned_employee_id. SCENARIO 2 -- SALES SEES ONLY THEIR OWN: PASS, and tested as a real discrimination rather than an empty list. audit-sales-a sees exactly 1 (LEAD-A); audit-sales-b sees exactly 1 (LEAD-B); NEITHER sees the other's lead, and NEITHER sees the unassigned lead, confirming the management-only-visible call is genuinely enforced and not just documented. SCENARIO 3 -- REASSIGNMENT: PASS, five sub-cases, four refusals and two successes, each a distinct code path. (3a) sales-a reassigning its OWN lead to sales-b -> real 403 "Only management can assign or reassign a lead". (3b) sales-a writing sales-b's lead -> real 403 "This lead is not assigned to you". (3c) sales-a self-claiming the UNASSIGNED lead -> real 403 "This lead is not assigned to you" -- worth recording that the message differs from 3a's; the existing-row assignment check fires before the requested-assignee check, so a null assignment is refused as not-yours rather than as a reassignment attempt. Correct refusal, slightly different reason than predicted, noted rather than smoothed over. (3d) sales-a editing its OWN lead with the assignment unchanged -> ok:true, so the gate refuses reassignment WITHOUT breaking the legitimate day-to-day write, which is the failure mode a too-strict gate would have. (3e) MANAGEMENT REASSIGNMENT WORKS, proven for BOTH management roles, not just owner: audit-owner reassigned LEAD-A to sales-b successfully, and after creating audit-manager (role 'admin' -- the other half of CRM_MANAGEMENT_ROLES = {owner, admin}, which had never been exercised) that account also read all 3 leads and successfully reassigned LEAD-A back to sales-a. THE STRONGEST SINGLE PIECE OF EVIDENCE, because it rules out a cached client-side filter: immediately after the owner's reassignment, sales-a's own read dropped to 0 leads and sales-b's rose to 2 -- visibility genuinely followed the server-side assignment change, live, in the same session. THE REFUSALS WERE REAL REFUSALS, not optimistic-then-reverted: after all three 403s, an owner read confirmed LEAD-B still carried its original name (not "HIJACKED"), the unassigned lead was still unassigned (not "CLAIMED"), and LEAD-A was still assigned to sales-a. Nothing was written by any refused call. THREE ADDITIONAL CONTROLS: a garbage X-SD-Auth value returns 401 NO_SESSION rather than failing open; a deliberately wrong body app_id of 'sairnbiz' on the same sd_crm read had NO effect on scoping (sales-a still got exactly its own 1 lead) -- the branch calls verifySessionToken(token, licHash, 'stonedesk') with the app hardcoded, so the client-supplied app_id field is inert here, which is Check 28's whole point holding in practice; and no-session reads were already confirmed 401 in the prior entry. ACCOUNT CREATED THIS PASS, flagged rather than folded in silently: audit-manager (PIN 468024, role 'admin'), one real api/sd-auth 'setup' call under the owner session. Not in the brief -- the brief said owner "or a manager" -- but owner alone would only have proven half of CRM_MANAGEMENT_ROLES, and the admin path had never been tested on StoneDesk at all. FINAL STATE, verified: owner 3 leads / manager 3 / sales-a 1 / sales-b 1, with LEAD-A restored to its original assignee so the license is left coherent. DELETE LIMITATION STILL TRUE, re-verified live not assumed: an owner-session delete on sd_crm returns "action must be 'read' or 'write'" -- the delete verb is SC_RESOURCES-only, so the three ZZ-VERIFY leads CANNOT be removed through the API and need a direct Supabase delete if Michael wants them gone (they are labelled "ZZ VERIFY ARTIFACT - DELETE" in the name field for exactly that reason). Four employee credentials now on SD-AUDIT-2026 (audit-owner, audit-manager, audit-sales-a, audit-sales-b) -- these should STAY, they are the point of a dedicated test license and make any future StoneDesk RBAC check a single sitting. STILL HELD, NOT STARTED: sc_settings server-side role gating, awaiting Michael's policy call. 2026-08-23
- stonedesk.html saveShopSettings() DELETED (CC) -- approved dead-code removal, pushed, live-verified. ZERO-REFERENCE RE-CONFIRMED AT CURRENT HEAD BEFORE CUTTING, same discipline as the APP_CONFIG cleanup, and the raw grep count was MISLEADING in a way worth recording: `grep -c saveShopSettings` returned 2, not 0. The second hit was not a caller -- it was a SELF-reference inside the function's own body, `document.querySelector('[onclick="saveShopSettings()"]')`, looking for a button that does not exist. Confirmed no real caller three ways rather than trusting the count: zero `<button ... saveShopSettings` elements anywhere, and all four DOM fields it read (admin-shop-name / admin-shop-addr / admin-shop-phone / admin-shop-email) have ZERO id="..." occurrences in the file. It could never have run successfully even if something had called it -- the first line dereferences .value on a null element and would have thrown immediately. This is the same shape as the photoExport() removal in STONEDESK-SESSION77: an orphaned function, not a live mislabelled button. THE FINDING THAT MATTERS MORE THAN THE DELETION, surfaced by checking what the function actually wrote: those four lines were the ONLY writers of fab_shop_name / fab_shop_addr / fab_shop_phone / fab_shop_email anywhere in stonedesk.html, and fab_shop_name is READ in roughly a dozen places -- proposals, invoices, order text, revenue config, print headers. Because the writer was already unreachable, every one of those reads has ALWAYS fallen through to its own hardcoded default ('Your Stone Shop', 'Stone Fabrication Shop', 'SAIRN Technologies LLC', 'your shop'). So this deletion changes nothing behaviourally -- it makes an existing gap visible instead of leaving it hidden behind a function that looks like it saves shop settings. THE REAL GAP, FLAGGED NOT FIXED (out of scope for an approved deletion, and it is a product decision): StoneDesk has no working way to set its own shop name, address, phone or email. Those defaults are what a customer's proposals and invoices actually print today. Worth a decision rather than a silent patch, since four different hardcoded fallbacks are currently standing in for one real setting. NOT TOUCHED, deliberately: loadShopSettings() immediately below it is ALSO currently callerless (grep-confirmed, its only hit is its own definition), but it was not part of the approved change, and unlike saveShopSettings() it is defensively written -- it null-checks every element before touching it, so it cannot throw. Left in place and flagged rather than bundled in. Removal comment left in its place recording all of the above, matching the house style of the demo_pin and APP_CONFIG removals so the next session finds the reasoning rather than a gap. VERIFIED PRE-PUSH: checkblocks 128/128 script blocks pass node --check; div balance 4746/4746 DIFF:0 PASS; duplicate_global_check 742 distinct globals, 0 duplicates; panel_nesting_check 0 trapped panels across 62. TWO TOOL FINDINGS INVESTIGATED AGAINST HEAD RATHER THAN REPORTED AS NEW, per the standing false-positive discipline: sairn_dead_button_audit.py reports TOTAL FLAGGED 2, and the identical 2 appear at HEAD -- C1 notify() (a toast-only function with 79 real callers, i.e. a live button, not dead code) and D2 (37 names reused across unrelated scopes, which the tool itself labels informational, not a collision). missing_dom_target_check.py reports 168 missing targets; HEAD reports 168, and a diff of the two name sets is byte-identical, so this change neither added nor removed one. Both pre-existing, neither introduced here. Also noted in passing, contradicting an older claim I was handed earlier this session: stonedesk.html's div balance is now DIFF:0 PASS, not -4 -- verified at HEAD as well as post-change, so the -4 was fixed by someone at some point and that stale figure should not be carried forward. Diff shape checked before commit: 24 insertions / 15 deletions, one file, CRLF preserved (34975 CRLF, 0 bare LF). LIVE-VERIFIED AFTER PUSH against sairn.vercel.app/stonedesk, not assumed from the push succeeding. STILL HELD, NOT STARTED: sc_settings server-side role gating, awaiting Michael's policy call. 2026-08-23
- stonedesk.html REAL Shop Settings save/load BUILT and BROWSER-VERIFIED END TO END (CC) -- pushed (`c8e80ad`), tested with a real login on SD-AUDIT-2026, not asserted from source. THE GAP WAS NOT "no way to set the shop name" -- IT WAS TWO PARALLEL SYSTEMS, and the app looked like it worked. panel-admin's Company Profile card wrote sd_settings (a JSON blob) and reloaded from it, genuinely functioning. But a dozen other places read a COMPLETELY SEPARATE raw-string namespace -- fab_shop_name/_addr/_phone/_email -- each with its own hardcoded fallback, and NOTHING wrote it (its only writer was the unreachable saveShopSettings(), deleted in 66ff298). So a user could type a company name, hit Save, get "Company profile saved.", and every proposal and cut sheet still said "Your Stone Shop". That is worse than an absent feature: it is a save that reports success and changes nothing the customer ever sees. BUILT: sdAdminPublishShopIdentity() publishes the four form fields into fab_shop_* through stRaw(), this file's real honest-failure write path. CLEARING a field REMOVES the key rather than writing '' -- an empty string would pin every read site to blank permanently, which is NOT the same as "never configured" and would defeat all twelve fallbacks. sdAdminLoadProfile() populates the form from sd_settings, falling back to fab_shop_* so a value set before this bridge existed shows up instead of appearing blank while quietly driving every proposal; wired into sbNav('admin') so it re-reads on EVERY panel open, not once at parse time. sdAdminSave() now reports WHICH half failed, since the two keys fail independently. DELIBERATELY NOT a third set of admin-shop-* fields -- rebuilding those would have added a third parallel system, the exact bug class being fixed. A SEPARATE REAL DEFECT FIXED IN THE SAME PASS, because the main fix would have made it dangerous: all seven Company Profile inputs shipped with hardcoded values baked into the markup ("Pinnacle Stone & Design", "Jim Hartwell", "OH-STONE-29441", "info@pinnaclesd.com", a Cleveland address and phone). Before this change they were merely cosmetic, since nothing they fed was ever read. AFTER wiring the bridge, a user hitting Save without editing would have published a fabricated shop name and a fabricated address onto their real invoices and proposals. Now empty with real placeholders -- Check 0b discipline, same as the SAIRNcode Settings rebuild earlier today. REQUIREMENT 4 ANSWERED HONESTLY, loadShopSettings() IS NOT THE FUNCTION THIS NEEDED and is now doubly dead: it targets admin-shop-name/-addr/-phone/-email, four ids with ZERO occurrences in the file, so every one of its null-checks fails and the entire body is a no-op even if something called it. Wiring it up would have silently done nothing. Left in place, NOT deleted -- a second removal was not approved -- with a comment recording the finding so a future session does not wire the wrong one. Flagged as a deletion candidate on its own merits: zero callers, zero reachable DOM targets, fully superseded by sdAdminLoadProfile(). REQUIREMENT 3 ANSWERED PRECISELY RATHER THAN AS "all 15 now work" -- a mechanical check of every read site, asking whether the enclosing function actually USES the variable it computes, found that FOUR DO NOT. LIVE AND CONFIRMED WORKING (9): showWelcome, buildEstimate, printDrawCutSheet, showSOPs, vendorPlaceOrder (inline expression), sdAIQGenerate, esigSendDepositEmail (name and phone), esigPrintApproval, revGenerate. DEAD ASSIGNMENTS -- compute shopName and never reference it again (4): agPrint and agViewSigned, which is CORRECT BY DESIGN and should not be changed (both render the StoneDesk SERVICE AGREEMENT between SAIRN Technologies LLC and the customer -- SAIRN's own SaaS contract, so the hardcoded SAIRN name is right and the shopName variable is simply vestigial); plus esigApprove's shopName and esigSendDepositEmail's shopEmail, which look like genuine small gaps. FLAGGED, NOT FIXED -- changing customer-facing approval and deposit-email copy is a content judgment call, not part of an approved plumbing fix. ALSO WORTH KNOWING: fab_shop_addr has ZERO live readers -- its only reader was loadShopSettings(). It is still saved (it belongs to the profile and the blob) but nothing renders it today. BROWSER-VERIFIED END TO END on the live deployed site, real login (audit-owner on SD-AUDIT-2026), not curl and not asserted: baseline with everything cleared showed BLANK fields (no fabricated Pinnacle name) and the read site correctly returning its own default "Your Stone Shop". Typed a real profile (Marietta Stone Works / 77 Quarry Rd, Marietta GA 30060 / (770) 555-0142 / quotes@mariettastone.example / Dana Whitfield) and clicked the REAL "Save Company Profile" button, not the function -- both namespaces written correctly, sd_settings blob and all four fab_shop_* keys. PROPOSAL: ran the real buildEstimate() and read the real rendered DOM -- est-shop-name AND est-footer-shop both show "Marietta Stone Works" where they had shown "Stone Fabrication Shop". APPROVAL/INVOICE DOCUMENT: ran the real esigPrintApproval() with window.open stubbed to capture its document.write output -- 2,208 characters emitted, contains "Marietta Stone Works", contains ZERO instances of the old "Your Stone Shop" default. CUT SHEET: real printDrawCutSheet() captured the same way, contains the real name. CLEAR PATH: emptied Company Name, clicked the real Save again -- the key was genuinely REMOVED (null, not ''), the proposal correctly fell back to its own "Stone Fabrication Shop" default, and the other three fields were left untouched, proving per-field independence. PERSISTENCE ACROSS A REAL RELOAD: full page navigation, fresh JS realm, real re-login -- opened Shop Settings and all five fields repopulated correctly from storage, zero JS errors. ISOLATED-LOGIC TESTED 16/16 against the real functions extracted verbatim from the file before any of the above, covering trim, per-field independence, the removeItem-not-empty-string rule, honest-failure on a real storage failure, the sd_settings-wins-then-fab_shop-fallback load order, the migration case, the nothing-stored case, and a corrupt blob degrading instead of throwing. - THREE APPROVED ITEMS DONE (CC) -- loadShopSettings() deleted, both shop-identity copy gaps closed, sc_settings admin-only gate built and live-verified with a real non-admin session. Pushed (`2e71d9b`). (1) loadShopSettings() DELETED, zero-reference re-confirmed at HEAD first: 2 grep hits, both inside the removal comment block, no callers anywhere in the repo, and all four ids it targeted (admin-shop-name/-addr/-phone/-email) still at 0 occurrences -- so its body was a no-op even if something had called it. Removal comment left pointing at sdAdminLoadProfile() as the real one, so a future session does not rediscover the wrong function. (2) BOTH CUSTOMER-FACING COPY GAPS CLOSED, using the fab_shop_* bridge built earlier today rather than new reads. esigApprove computed shopName and never used it -- the customer's on-screen approval confirmation never said WHOSE project they had just approved; the real shop name now renders there. esigSendDepositEmail read shopEmail and never used it -- a customer receiving a deposit request had no address to reply to unless a phone happened to be set; there is now an "Or reply to us at ..." line, conditional exactly like the existing phone line so an unset field prints NOTHING rather than a dangling "reply to us at ." -- the same never-fabricate rule the rest of the shop identity follows. A mechanical re-audit of every fab_shop_* read site now shows every variable used, except two. THOSE TWO ARE agPrint AND agViewSigned AND THEY ARE CORRECT AS-IS -- both render the StoneDesk SERVICE AGREEMENT between SAIRN Technologies LLC (the vendor) and the customer shop, so every party/footer/signature name in them is SAIRN's own and correctly hardcoded; substituting the shop's name would misstate who the contract is with. Rather than leave two unexplained dead variables to be "fixed" wrongly by a future pass, both are now annotated in place with that reasoning. (3) sc_settings ROLE GATE BUILT TO THE DECIDED POLICY -- Compliance Admin only for writes, every other role read-only. (a) SERVER GATE in api/sd-data.js's sc_settings write branch. The role comes from the verified session token, never from the client, and the token carries an app claim so a valid session for a different SAIRN app cannot satisfy it (Check 28). Reads deliberately stay open to every authenticated role -- a coder or auditor SHOULD be able to see the retention policy that governs them. This knowingly NARROWS existing behaviour: retention_years has been writable by any authenticated role since 2026-08-20, and is now admin-only too. That is the intended consequence of the policy, not a side effect, and it is recorded as such in the code. (b) scData() HONEST-ERROR FIX, the part that mattered most. It collapsed every non-ok response to null, so a 403 reached the user as "saved on this device only -- server sync failed" -- a false statement in the most damaging direction, telling someone their edit is safe locally and will sync later when the server had in fact rejected it and never would. ~25 call sites depend on the null return, so the type was left alone and the real error is now recorded alongside it (scLastDataError()). scSaveSettings() returns `refused` separately from `synced`, and A REFUSED WRITE IS ROLLED BACK LOCALLY -- caching a value the server rejected would show a practice-level setting that is not the practice's, and the next login's scSyncOneResource() pull would silently replace it, so the user would watch their change stick and then vanish with no explanation. A genuine NETWORK failure still caches locally and still says "sync failed", because that one really is true. (c) UI GATING: every practice-level control disabled for non-admins with a plain note naming their actual role. Applied from the Privacy panel's render path as well as Settings', because the retention control lives in Privacy but writes the same admin-only row -- without that, a coder reaching Privacy directly would find a live Save Policy button the server would refuse. LIVE-VERIFIED WITH A REAL NON-ADMIN SESSION, which is what this whole arc has been blocked on before: created gate-test-coder (role 'coder') through the app's own real setup action under an admin session, logged in as them for real, token decoded and confirmed {app:sairncode, role:coder}. NINE SERVER CASES, all correct: coder practice_name write -> real 403 FORBIDDEN; coder payer_schedules write -> 403; coder retention_years write -> 403 (the previously-allowed path, now correctly closed); coder READ -> still 200 with real data, confirming read access was not collateral damage; no session at all -> NO_SESSION not FORBIDDEN, so the two failure modes stay distinguishable; admin read-back after all three refusals -> the row was BYTE-UNCHANGED, proving nothing leaked past the gate; admin write -> real success, so the gate is not a blanket block; admin write of retention_years:3 -> still RETENTION_BELOW_FLOOR, confirming the role check did not replace the value check (two different controls, both live); and a coder write to sc_scrubrules -> still succeeds, confirming the gate is scoped to sc_settings alone and did not silently narrow the other 25 SC resources. BROWSER-VERIFIED for the UI half: signed in as the real coder, all 7 controls disabled, the note visible and correctly reading "...disabled for your role (coder)". THEN DELIBERATELY BYPASSED THE UI THE WAY AN ATTACKER WOULD -- re-enabled the input in devtools, typed a value, forced the save -- and the SERVER still refused, with the toast and the status line both reading "Only a Compliance Admin can change practice-level settings. Your changes were not saved." and the local cache confirmed ROLLED BACK to its prior value. That is the proof that the UI is a courtesy and the server is the boundary. Same session logged back in as admin: all controls live, note hidden, a real practice-name save succeeded with the plain "Practice name saved." wording. ISOLATED-LOGIC TESTED 26/26 against the real functions extracted verbatim before any of the live work, covering both failure modes separately, the rollback, the no-leak-into-returned-record case, all four roles, and signed-out. Prior shop-bridge suite re-run 16/16, unaffected. GUARDIAN: checkblocks 2/2 (sairncode) and 128/128 (stonedesk); dead-button 0 flagged; div balance DIFF:0 on both; 0 duplicate ids; 342 distinct globals 0 duplicates; 0 missing DOM targets; 0 trapped panels; api/sd-data.js node --check plus a real require, both clean. One self-caught mistake worth recording: a python replace introduced 16 bare LFs into stonedesk.html (CRLF file) -- caught by the CRLF check before commit, not after, and repaired; final diff is 38/0 on api/sd-data.js, 122/28 on sairncode.html, 40/30 on stonedesk.html with CRLF intact in all three. ALL TEST ARTIFACTS CLEANED: the sc_scrubrules scope-check row deleted via a real admin delete and confirmed gone, and sc_settings restored to its exact pre-test state (retention_years:10, no practice_name) confirmed by a real server read-back. ONE ARTIFACT LEFT DELIBERATELY: the gate-test-coder credential on SC-PINNACLE-2026 -- the first real non-admin SAIRNcode session that has ever existed, and the thing that made this verification possible at all. Recommend keeping it for future RBAC checks; say the word and I will remove it. 2026-08-23
- gate-test-coder removal BLOCKED (no API path) + both SAIRNcode blockers re-checked live (CC) -- verification only, one new SQL file, no app code touched. (1) GATE-TEST-CODER NOT DELETED, AND I CANNOT DELETE IT -- reporting this rather than claiming it done. api/sc-auth.js exposes exactly four actions (bootstrap, login, setup, check_license); there is no delete and no deactivate. Confirmed by probing all four candidate verbs (delete/deactivate/remove/disable) against production with a real Compliance Admin session -- every one returned the action-allowlist error, NOT a permission error, so the capability is genuinely absent rather than merely gated. Re-confirmed in the same pass that the account is still live (a real login returned ok:true, role coder). 'setup' cannot substitute: it upserts with active:true and takes no active/enabled parameter, so re-running it can only rotate the PIN, never remove or disable the row. DELIBERATELY DID NOT rotate it to an unrecorded PIN as a workaround -- that would leave an orphaned account nobody can clean up later, which is worse than one intact and documented. Wrote sql/sairncode_gate_test_coder_cleanup.sql instead: a single scoped DELETE on sairncode_employee_auth for employee_id 'gate-test-coder' AND license_hash sha256('SC-PINNACLE-2026'), with both verification curls inline. NOT RUN -- no DB access. The file carries an explicit warning not to widen it: the Compliance Admin ('owner') lives in that same table, and dropping the employee_id predicate would lock SAIRNcode's demo license out with no recovery path -- precisely the failure that burned three StoneDesk keys and needed sql/stonedesk_audit_license_credential_reset.sql. STRUCTURAL NOTE, same root cause as the StoneDesk one: SAIRNcode has no credential lifecycle at all -- accounts can be created but never removed, disabled, or rotated-with-knowledge through the API. Flagged, not built. (2) BLOCKER RE-CHECK, STEDI -- STILL NOT CONFIGURED, unchanged since Phase 2 closed. Probed two independent ways with a real admin session rather than one: api/sc-credentials 'status' returns {stedi:{configured:false, last4:null, updated_at:null}}, and a real 270/271 'check' against production returns the honest 503 NOT_CONFIGURED with its full guidance text. No key has been connected. Phase 3 item 4 (payer-eligibility-informed suggestions) therefore remains correctly unbuilt -- its precondition is still unmet, verified not assumed. (3) BLOCKER RE-CHECK, MIGRATIONS -- BOTH STILL UNRUN. Probed the real provisioned flag per resource: sc_coded_items provisioned:false, sc_credential_scope provisioned:false. Both still degrade honestly to local-only sync. Controls run in the same pass so the probe method is trustworthy rather than assumed working: sc_settings and sc_scrubrules both returned provisioned:true with 1 real row each, so a false is a genuine absent table, not a broken probe. sql/sairncode_coded_items_schema.sql and sql/sairncode_credential_scope_schema.sql both still need Michael in Supabase. THREE ITEMS NOW QUEUED FOR SUPABASE, all mine to flag and none mine to run: those two migrations, plus the new gate-test-coder cleanup. 2026-08-23
