- SAIRNcode ELIGIBILITY WORKING END TO END + PHASE 3 ITEM 4 BUILT (CC) -- pushed (`b7b50b8` dependents fix, `26256da` item 4), live-verified. THE ROOT CAUSE WAS OURS, AND IT WAS A WRONG-PATIENT DEFECT, not a test-harness problem. api/sc-eligibility.js built its outbound body from payerId/provider/subscriber plus an optional encounter and never read `dependents` at all -- zero occurrences in the file. Confirmed in Stedi's own dashboard, whose recorded raw request for check ec_01a02fb3-17da-7221-b46b-7643f49fd440 contains no dependents array. Consequence in production, not just in testing: a child on a parent's policy -- the most common eligibility case in a family or pediatric practice -- was transmitted as a check on the SUBSCRIBER, so the payer answered about the parent and this app would have rendered the parent's benefits as the patient's. A wrong-patient coverage answer presented as real, inside the one endpoint whose entire contract forbids it. It only surfaced because Stedi's mocks refused to pass. TWO OF MY OWN EARLIER CONCLUSIONS WERE WRONG AND ARE CORRECTED IN THE CODE COMMENTS, not just here: (a) I reported the checks were "forwarded to real payers" -- false. The X12 271 shows ISA15=T (test indicator) and sender STEDI, so Stedi's mock layer was answering all along and test mode worked from the start; (b) the Authorization "Key" scheme fix (`acd23f1`) is correct per Stedi's docs and caused no regression, but it was not the cause either. Three hypotheses, three disproven, before the real one. Worth recording as method: each was killed by evidence rather than argued down -- the key by swapping production for test and getting a byte-identical result, the header by deploying it and getting a byte-identical result, and the real cause by reading Stedi's own recorded raw request instead of trusting what we believed we sent. FIX: dependents accepted, validated in the same shape as subscriber, forwarded alongside encounter. Deliberately STRICTER than subscriber on one point -- a dependent requires dateOfBirth, because per Stedi's guidance that is what lets a payer distinguish the dependent from the subscriber, and without it payers commonly resolve to the subscriber, which is the precise failure the field exists to prevent. Empty/absent array untouched and still a valid ordinary subscriber check. Client got dependent first/last/DOB fields with matching validation and an explanation of when to use them. ALL SIX DOCUMENTED MOCKS NOW RETURN REAL BENEFITS, zero errors: Aetna 60 entries, Anthem BCBS CA 43, BCBS TX 64, Cigna 36, Oscar 68, UnitedHealthcare 41. Payloads harvested verbatim from the rendered docs page with accordions expanded, after two earlier extraction attempts silently truncated the dependents array -- that truncation is what sent me chasing the key and the header. NEGATIVE CONTROL, one variable isolated: the identical Aetna mock WITHOUT dependents still returns 0 benefits and error 73, so dependents is provably the thing that was missing. Validation gate confirmed live too (a dependent with no DOB is refused with the real message). PHASE 3 ITEM 4 BUILT TO THE DECIDED SCOPE -- advisory only, surfaced in the note-to-code flow, linked by encounter_ref, never blocking and never auto-escalating. THE LINE IT DOES NOT CROSS, and the reason the design is deliberately narrow: an ICD-10/CPT/HCPCS code does not carry an X12 service-type code and this app has NO verified mapping between them, so claiming "this code is covered" would mean inventing that mapping -- the same fabrication class this codebase has spent weeks removing, except here it would be a fabrication about a patient's financial liability. The advisory therefore reports the ENCOUNTER's real coverage status plus the service types the payer actually answered for, and says in the UI that it is not a per-code determination. Eligibility records gained encounter_ref, the real service-type list, and the dependent subject when one was checked -- deliberately narrow, status and service-type codes only, no dollar amounts or deductibles, so the panel's standing rule that the full payer response is not stored still holds. Absence is stated as absence: no check on file renders "coverage was not checked -- this is not a statement that coverage is absent." LIVE-VERIFIED with a real admin session on SC-PINNACLE-2026: ran a genuine dependent eligibility check (Stedi's Aetna mock, Jordan Doe DOB 20010714) tagged ZZ-ENC-VERIFY-1, got real Active Coverage on Aetna Choice POS II with 60 benefit entries, and the stored record captured encounter_ref plus the 11 real service types the payer answered for (30, 48, 33, 86, 98, UC, 50, 1, 47, MH, AL) and "Jordan Doe (dependent)". Then rendered the advisory from the LIVE deployed function against that REAL record: matched case reproduces payer, status, plan, date, dependent subject and all 11 service types, and carries the not-a-per-code-determination disclaimer; unmatched case correctly states coverage was not checked; blank ref renders nothing at all. Isolated-logic tested 21/21 beforehand against the real functions extracted verbatim, including most-recent-wins, honest empty states, and escaping of a hostile encounter ref. Guardian clean throughout: checkblocks 2/2, dead-button 0 flagged, div balance DIFF:0, 0 duplicate ids/globals, 0 missing DOM targets, CRLF preserved in both files. ONE THING NOT VERIFIED BY CLICK, stated plainly rather than glossed: the advisory rendering INSIDE a real Suggest-Codes run was not click-tested end to end. Clicking Suggest Codes as owner fired the first-use AI consent gate, which is a real blocking confirm() dialog, and it froze the browser tab -- my own doing, the exact hazard the browser-automation guidance warns about. I did not force it or pre-set the consent flag, since consent is the user's decision to make, not mine. What IS proven: the advisory function is deployed, is called from both render paths in the deployed source, and produces correct output against a real stored record. The consent gate itself was already verified separately on 2026-08-20. ARTIFACT CLEANED: the ZZ-ENC-VERIFY-1 eligibility row removed locally and confirmed absent server-side (sc_eligibility reads back 0 rows). The server delete returned false because that row had never synced -- the write happens after the record is cached locally and the sync had not completed before cleanup; noted rather than ignored, and the read-back confirms nothing was left behind either way. 2026-08-23
- SAIRNcode CREDENTIAL LIFECYCLE BUILT AND LIVE-VERIFIED (CC) -- pushed (`12c670c` feature, `9fef0f3` inactive-caller fix). Closes the structural gap flagged twice this session: neither auth API could deactivate, disable or safely remove an employee credential, so every cleanup (SD-AUDIT-2026, gate-test-coder) needed a hand-written SQL file and a round trip through Michael. SMALLER CHANGE THAN IT LOOKED, because the data model was already built for it: `active boolean not null default true` already exists in both schemas, both schema files already say "deactivation is active=false, not a row delete", and login in BOTH apps already filters active=eq.true. Only the action was missing -- the flag is enforced the moment it is written, with no second mechanism to keep in sync. BUILT for SAIRNcode: set_active (admin-only, server-verified from the token's app-scoped role claim) plus roster ported from api/sd-auth.js, which has had one since 2026-08-19 while SAIRNcode had none -- without it an admin cannot look up the employee_id they need to deactivate, so the feature would have been unusable alone. Roster includes INACTIVE rows (unlike sd-auth's, which filters to active) precisely because reactivation exists. Never returns pin_hash/pin_salt/failed_attempts/locked_until -- verified live, grep of the real response returns 0. auditor deliberately gets nothing: read-only on app DATA is its job, the credential roster is the access-control surface itself. Deactivation NEVER deletes a row, so created_at, role history and audit linkage survive -- deleting is what made the earlier cleanups both unrecoverable and unauditable. THE REAL FINDING, and it came out of live testing rather than reading: A DEACTIVATED ADMIN'S SESSION TOKEN STAYED VALID AND COULD STILL ACT. Tokens carry a role claim and live 12h; the role gate checked the claim and never re-checked current state. Proven live on SC-PINNACLE-2026 -- owner was deactivated, then used its still-valid token to attempt deactivating the last remaining admin, and the attempt reached the LAST_ADMIN guard rather than being refused at the door. So a just-removed admin could keep removing other people, and could walk the license toward the exact lockout this whole feature exists to prevent. Fixed in `9fef0f3`: both set_active and roster now re-check the caller's CURRENT active row (free in set_active, which already reads the roster), new code CREDENTIAL_INACTIVE 403, audit-logged. Live-confirmed after deploy: a deactivated admin's live token is now refused on BOTH actions. HONEST CONSEQUENCE, recorded rather than buried: that fix makes the LAST_ADMIN guard UNREACHABLE BY CONSTRUCTION -- reaching it needs an active-admin caller and a DIFFERENT active-admin target with exactly one active admin existing, and the first two conditions imply at least two. The guard is KEPT and explicitly quarantined in-file with that reasoning, not deleted and not left looking live, because reachability is a property of today's rule set: adding a second entry to PROVISIONING_ROLES, a service-to-service caller, or any path skipping the active re-check would make it live again, and a lockout is not a failure mode worth re-discovering in production. The test suite asserts the reachability ARGUMENT rather than a comment asserting it. THE LOCKOUT ATTEMPT WAS RUN DELIBERATELY AND WAS REFUSED, live on SC-PINNACLE-2026, before that fix landed: self-deactivation as the only admin -> 409 SELF_DEACTIVATE, owner still logged in fine afterward; then with a second admin provisioned, the genuine LAST_ADMIN path fired with its real message and the target was confirmed STILL ACTIVE in the roster after the refusal. Also confirmed the flag really bites: a deactivated credential returns INVALID_CREDENTIALS on login, no separate enforcement needed. bootstrap DELIBERATELY UNCHANGED -- making it fall back to "no ACTIVE rows" would auto-heal a lockout but would also let anyone holding a license key deactivate their way to a fresh bootstrap and seize the account. Recovery goes through another admin. ISOLATION-TESTED 32/32 against the REAL handler (required directly, license/session/audit/fetch mocked): every non-admin role refused on both actions across coder/biller/auditor, no-session refused, deactivating a non-last admin succeeds with a correct remaining_admins count, the deactivated-caller refusal proven to leave the target untouched, self-deactivation refused and logged, reason required to deactivate but not to reactivate, reactivation unconditional, unknown employee 404, non-boolean active rejected, no-op reported as unchanged rather than as a change, audit failure proven non-blocking AND surfaced, and the roster proven to leak no secrets. TWO MIGRATIONS WRITTEN, NEITHER RUN, BOTH MICHAEL'S: (1) sql/sairncode_audit_log_credential_events.sql -- the table's CHECK constraint allows only the four ai_* events, so every real set_active call returned audited:false. The action worked correctly every time (per api/_lib/audit.js a failed log never blocks it) but NOTHING WAS RECORDED. Caught only because that flag is returned to the caller instead of swallowed -- a silent audit failure on a credential change is the kind of gap you find when you need the log and it is empty. Until it runs, SAIRNcode credential changes succeed and honestly report audited:false. (2) sql/stonedesk_audit_log_schema.sql -- StoneDesk has NO audit table at all (api/_lib/audit.js allowlists only sairnlaw_audit_log and sairncode_audit_log), so its half of this feature is deliberately HELD rather than shipping unaudited. After that table exists, one more code change is needed and is documented in the file: add 'stonedesk_audit_log' to AUDIT_TABLES. ARTIFACT LEFT, deliberately and usefully: zz-lifecycle-admin on SC-PINNACLE-2026, left DEACTIVATED -- which is itself the demonstration that the feature works, since before today the only way to neutralise a test credential was a hand-written DELETE. It can be reactivated or left inert; it cannot log in while inactive. 2026-08-23
- CREDENTIAL LIFECYCLE COMPLETE ON BOTH APPS + AUDIT LOGGING CONFIRMED WORKING (CC) -- pushed (`2304054`), live-verified on SC-PINNACLE-2026 and SD-AUDIT-2026. Both migrations confirmed run by Michael. (1) SAIRNCODE AUDIT LOGGING NOW GENUINELY WORKS: a real set_active as Compliance Admin returns audited:true where every call before the CHECK-constraint migration returned audited:false. Verified in both directions -- a deactivation and a reactivation both audited:true -- and the refusal path too: SELF_DEACTIVATE now returns audited:true alongside the refusal. IMPROVEMENT MADE WHILE VERIFYING, because the original design made the claim untestable: refusal responses did not carry `audited` at all, so "refusals are logged" was unverifiable from outside -- no endpoint reads the audit table, so the response is the only place a caller can see whether the record was really written. All three refusal paths (SELF_DEACTIVATE, CREDENTIAL_INACTIVE, LAST_ADMIN) now surface it. (2) STONEDESK HALF BUILT AND LIVE, held until sql/stonedesk_audit_log_schema.sql existed rather than shipping unaudited, and 'stonedesk_audit_log' added to AUDIT_TABLES in api/_lib/audit.js -- that allowlist exists precisely so a table name never reaches a REST URL unchecked. Same design as SAIRNcode's, adjusted for this app's roles: StoneDesk has owner AND admin, both of which can run setup, so BOTH count toward the last-admin guard, unlike SAIRNcode where admin is the only provisioning role. PROVISIONING_ROLES is shared by the set_active gate, the admin count and the roster gate so the three cannot drift apart. roster now includes INACTIVE rows where it filtered to active=eq.true before -- that filter made sense when nothing could deactivate anyone; now an Owner or Manager has to be able to SEE a deactivated person to turn them back on. Still leaks no pin hashes, salts or lockout state (verified live, grep of the real response returns 0). The deactivated-caller re-check was carried across from SAIRNcode rather than waiting to rediscover it on StoneDesk. LIVE-VERIFIED ON SD-AUDIT-2026, full sequence: a sales-role session refused on BOTH actions (FORBIDDEN); a real deactivation of audit-sales-b returned audited:true and the credential then genuinely failed login with INVALID_CREDENTIALS, confirming the flag bites with no separate enforcement; reactivation with no reason restored it and login succeeded again. THE DELIBERATE LOCKOUT ATTEMPT, run the same way as SAIRNcode's and refused at every step: owner self-deactivation -> SELF_DEACTIVATE, audited:true; manager then deactivated owner legitimately (2 provisioning roles -> 1); the now-INACTIVE owner used its still-valid token to attempt deactivating the last remaining admin -> CREDENTIAL_INACTIVE, audited:true, and the same token was refused on roster too; the manager could not self-deactivate either -> SELF_DEACTIVATE. Owner restored, final roster confirmed all four credentials active. At no point could the license be walked to zero active provisioning roles. Isolation-tested 30/30 (StoneDesk, both non-provisioning roles sales and install refused on both actions) and 32/32 (SAIRNcode) against the REAL handlers. IMMUTABILITY -- PARTIALLY VERIFIED, STATED PLAINLY RATHER THAN CLAIMED. What IS proven: INSERT works, confirmed by six-plus real audited:true writes across both apps and by the fact that the pre-migration CHECK constraint really did reject writes, so these responses reflect Postgres accepting the row rather than a helper reporting optimistically. Also confirmed at the code level: a grep across all of api/ for any UPDATE, PATCH, PUT or DELETE against an audit table returns NOTHING, and api/_lib/audit.js contains exactly one HTTP method, POST -- so no code path in the platform even attempts a mutation. What is NOT proven by me: that an UPDATE or DELETE issued directly against stonedesk_audit_log actually fails with permission denied. That needs raw SQL and I have no database access -- there is no API path to that table by design, which is itself part of the control. The two statements are documented inline at the bottom of sql/stonedesk_audit_log_schema.sql and are Michael's to run: `update stonedesk_audit_log set role = 'x';` and `delete from stonedesk_audit_log;` -- both should FAIL with permission denied. Reporting this as unverified-by-me rather than assuming the grants behave as written, since the grants being correct is exactly the claim under test. ARTIFACTS: SD-AUDIT-2026 left with all four credentials ACTIVE (audit-owner, audit-manager, audit-sales-a, audit-sales-b) -- everything toggled during testing was restored, confirmed by a final roster read. SC-PINNACLE-2026 still has zz-lifecycle-admin left DEACTIVATED, deliberately, as the standing demonstration that a test credential can now be neutralised without a hand-written DELETE. Both apps' auth APIs now have a real credential lifecycle; the structural gap that forced every prior cleanup through Michael and Supabase is closed. 2026-08-23

- SAIRNcash Bridge exclusion + StoneDesk Bridge cleanup, all four items closed (CC) -- pushed (`fd39f3a` exclusion, `cce3b94` cleanup), live-verified. (1) SAIRNCASH BRIDGE DECLINED AND RECORDED AS A CLOSED DECISION, not a deferral -- same in-file treatment as the app's existing AR-measurement and shared-knowledge exclusions, so it cannot quietly reappear on a list. Four independent reasons, any one sufficient: the Bridge's read side has NO consumer (?action=pull, zero callers across all 13 app files); wrong data shape (push carries a shop's jobs/invoices/employees, SAIRNcash has one freelancer's income, deductions and tax profile); wrong architecture (Firebase RTDB + customerId, single user, zero references to api/sd-data.js or license_hash, no shopId to key on); wrong privacy posture (push requires no Authorization at all -- fine for shop metadata, not for personal earnings). The one scenario that could justify it is blocked upstream, not here: license_keys is keyed (key, app_id), so one person's two apps have different hashes by construction and no account-linking table exists. (2) THE BRIDGE INVESTIGATION, and the answer was not the one expected. Not dead weight on a timer -- there is no timer anywhere. Three push call sites: syncToSAIRNBridge() and syncToSAIRNdesign() both had ZERO callers (the only occurrence of each was its own definition line), and crSendToBridge() is genuinely live behind a real button. WHAT PULL WAS MEANT TO SERVE, from the build commit df84b21 itself: 'Serves the two real, live call shapes (Market Intelligence's proxy_get, StoneDesk's push/Field Map sync)' -- two shapes, and pull is not one of them. It appears in the commit TITLE but not in the list of live shapes it was built for. So pull was speculative at build time with no identified consumer; nothing was supposed to exist and got dropped. (3) THE PART THAT ACTUALLY MATTERED was not the dead code. crSendToBridge sat behind a button labelled 'Send to SAIRNbiz' and told the user on success '✅ Sent to SAIRNbiz -- open SAIRNbiz to write the check.' SAIRNbiz references api/bridge ZERO times and cannot see the entry, so the app instructed the user to perform an impossible action. A second label read 'Sent to SAIRNbiz automatically', and the panel copy claimed 'All connected apps share data through the SAIRN Bridge... One click syncs payroll, GL entries, job orders, and project data across your entire suite.' Nothing reads any of it. Same class as the fabricated-KPI findings this codebase has spent weeks removing, except here it told the user to act on it. Per Michael's decision the UI was made honest rather than the read side built: copy, button label and both status messages now say the entry is saved locally and queued with no reader yet, and point at CSV Export as the working path today. SECOND REAL DEFECT found while verifying and disclosed in-file rather than silently: push is latest-wins on shop_id and this caller sends ONE expense per click, so each send REPLACES the previous entry -- proven live (pushed A then B, a pull returned only B). Correct for the snapshot pushes the endpoint was designed around, wrong for its only live caller, and another reason the read side needs real design rather than a quick read. (4) BOTH DEAD FUNCTIONS DELETED per Check 0d with removal comments recording what they were. Global count 741 -> 739, exactly the two. (5) THE NAMING COLLISION FIXED AT ITS SOURCE. 'SAIRN Data Bridge' means two different things: api/sd-data.js's shared resources (the real cross-app path, 10 apps, genuinely read -- what SAIRNsenior and SAIRNcare shipped under that name) and api/bridge.js (a separate StoneDesk-only endpoint). GUARDIAN CHECK 2 NAMED THE WRONG ONE: 'all cross-app data uses sairn.vercel.app/api/bridge, never rebuilt inline' -- which pointed new work at the endpoint with no read side instead of the one that works, and is very likely why everything got called the Bridge. Check 2 now distinguishes them explicitly in BOTH the project and user copies of the skill (they had diverged again -- 48585 vs 47824 bytes -- the same cross-session drift flagged 2026-08-19), and api/bridge.js carries the same distinction at the top of its own header with a per-action health note. Both record that push requires no Authorization at all, which bounds what it can ever safely carry. (6) proxy_get untouched and confirmed still working live (a disallowed host still returns the real allowlist refusal naming api.stlouisfed.org and homebuyer.com). pull left exactly as-is per instruction -- correct, harmless, and the natural read side whenever one is genuinely built. LIVE-VERIFIED after push: all three false claims are gone from the deployed page (0 occurrences each), all four honest replacements present, both dead functions absent from the live source. The only two remaining string matches are inside the removal comments that explain them, confirmed by reading the deployed lines. GUARDIAN: checkblocks 128/128, div balance DIFF:0, 739 globals 0 duplicates, 0 duplicate ids, 0 console.log, missing-DOM-targets identical to HEAD (164/164), dead-button findings identical to HEAD (pre-existing C1 notify with 79 real callers, D2 informational). CRLF preserved. STILL OPEN, NOT MINE: building the real SAIRNbiz read side is a separate scoped task, deliberately not started. THREE PIECES, all new: (1) `sql/sairnroofing_jobs_schema.sql` -- rf_jobs table, assigned_employee_id and job_class as real top-level columns (not buried in jsonb) for the same reason every prior assignment-gated table does this, modelled directly on sql/sairnsenior_clients_schema.sql's shape. Deliberately minimal Phase 1 fields only (address/status/job_class/assignment) -- measurement, estimate and scheduling fields are Phase 2/4 and not invented early. (2) THE GATE ITSELF, api/sd-data.js's new rf_jobs read/write branch, built as the real three-tier gate from commit one rather than shipped as a binary and fixed later -- the scope doc named both bugs by name to avoid: SAIRNsenior's Phase 1 gate silently narrowed its broad-read tier (coordinator/scheduler) to own-assigned-only because one branch checked management-only where it should have checked broad-read, not caught until 2026-08-20; SAIRNbuild's bld_bids needed a separate fix so a narrow-tier user's new record self-assigns to them on create instead of landing unassigned. Both designed in from the start here: MANAGEMENT (owner/admin) full read/write/reassign, BROAD_READ (+estimator) sees and edits every job but cannot reassign, NARROW (foreman/crew) own-assigned-only with self-assign-on-create. ONE DELIBERATE STRUCTURAL CHOICE beyond copying the pattern: api/rf-auth.js now EXPORTS its MANAGEMENT_ROLES/BROAD_READ_ROLES (`module.exports.MANAGEMENT_ROLES = ...`) and api/sd-data.js requires and reads from them, rather than re-listing the role names a second time the way every earlier app's gate does (ALF_MANAGEMENT_ROLES, SEN_CLIENT_MANAGEMENT_ROLES, etc. are all local redeclarations). This is the exact drift rf-auth.js's own header already named as SAIRNsenior's root cause (one code path used senIsManagement() where the rest used senIsBroadRead()) -- one source of truth this time instead of two lists to keep in sync by hand. Not backported to any other app's gate; scoped to this one because it's the file that documented the intent. (3) `sairnroofing.html`, new, minimal by design -- license gate, employee login/bootstrap (mirrors sairnsenior.html's rfAuth/rfEnterApp shape), a Jobs panel (list + add/edit modal, assignee field only rendered for management, matching the server gate rather than trusting a hidden client value), and an AI Assistant chat panel whose entire context is built from the same already-role-filtered job list the Jobs panel renders -- so the assistant structurally cannot see more than the signed-in role already sees, satisfying the Phase 1 scope line "Dashboard + AI Assistant scoped to what the signed-in role can see" by construction rather than by a second access check. Photo-capture-to-Claude (sairn-app-scaffold's standing requirement) deliberately NOT built here -- it belongs to Phase 2's measurement/estimate flow per the scope doc, not Phase 1's auth/RBAC foundation, and building it now would be exactly the "while I'm in here" scope growth the project's guardrails warn against. Also added the missing `/sairnroofing` pretty-URL route to vercel.json (every other app has one; this app had none) and a new api/_resources/sairnroofing.js registry file (registered in api/_resources/index.js) since rf_jobs needed to be a recognized resource name before api/sd-data.js's generic dispatch would ever reach the new branch. GUARDIAN MECHANICAL CHECKS, run against the new file before considering this done: checkblocks 1/1, div balance DIFF:0, 24/24 distinct globals (0 duplicates), 0 missing DOM targets, panel-nesting 0 trapped panels, nav-panel PASS, 0 key collisions, dead-button audit 0 flagged across all categories (A/B/C1/C2/D1/D2/E). vercel.json re-validated as parseable JSON after the route addition. - SAIRNroofing Phase 1 LIVE END-TO-END VERIFIED, ONE REAL BUG FOUND AND FIXED (CC). Michael confirmed all three prerequisites run: sairnroofing_employee_auth table, rf_jobs table, RF-PINNACLE-2026 demo key. Real sequence against the live deployment, not a dry run: bootstrapped owner (employee_id 'owner', PIN set), a SEPARATE real login call confirmed the credential (not just bootstrap's own token), whoami round-tripped {role:'owner',employee_id:'owner'}. Provisioned two real crew accounts (test-crew-a, test-crew-b) via setup, logged in as both. Wrote two real rows to rf_jobs: RF-VERIFY-1 unassigned, RF-VERIFY-2 assigned to test-crew-a. READ-SIDE GATE CONFIRMED CORRECT: owner sees both (2); test-crew-a sees only its own (1, RF-VERIFY-2); test-crew-b sees NEITHER (0) -- proving both halves of item 4 at once, an unassigned job is invisible to the narrow tier (management-only-visible, as documented) and a job assigned to someone else is invisible too, not just narrowed some other way. WRITE-SIDE NEGATIVE CONTROLS also correct: crew-a refused (403 FORBIDDEN) attempting to edit RF-VERIFY-1 (not theirs); crew-b refused editing RF-VERIFY-2 (assigned to crew-a, not them). THE REAL BUG: crew-b creating a brand-new job with no assigned_employee_id in the payload was refused with 403 "Only management can assign or reassign a job" -- self-assign-on-create, the exact SAIRNbuild bug class this gate was supposedly built to avoid, was NOT actually working. ROOT CAUSE, found by reading the code rather than guessing: api/sd-data.js's server-side gate was never wrong -- it only CHECKS that the assignee the client sends matches the caller's own id, it does not invent a value when the field is omitted, and that is the exact same shape as the already-proven-correct sen_clients/alf_clients gates. The bug was in MY OWN sairnroofing.html: saveJob() only ever sent assigned_employee_id when the caller was management, leaving it undefined for the narrow tier on the mistaken assumption the server would fill it in. Checked sairnsenior.html's real saveClient() (the function the server-side comment explicitly says this depends on) and confirmed it always computes and sends the field explicitly per tier -- management from the dropdown, broad-read preserves the existing assignment (or stays unassigned on create), narrow tier preserves existing assignment on edit or self-assigns to its own session.employee_id on create. Fixed sairnroofing.html's saveJob() to match that exact shape byte-for-byte in logic. Re-verified against the live site with the fix applied (test-crew-b reactivated, logged in, created RF-VERIFY-3 sending its own employee_id explicitly): 200, self-assigned correctly, and immediately visible to crew-b's own read. NEGATIVE CONTROL on the fix itself: crew-b then tried to create a job self-assigned to 'owner' instead of itself -- correctly refused (403, same message), so the fix doesn't just make self-assign work, it still blocks a narrow-tier user from assigning ANYONE else. Final owner read confirmed all 3 real jobs present with correct assignees. FOUR PIECES: (1) `sql/sairnroofing_photos_schema.sql`, new table, modelled directly on sd_progress_photos_schema.sql (1.5MB cap, same shape) rather than crammed into rf_jobs.data's 64KB cap -- the wrong shape for a photo. (2) `sql/sairnroofing_employee_auth_certifications_migration.sql`, ALTER on the already-live employee table adding a `certifications jsonb` bag -- deliberately only `tesla_certified` is read or written by any code path today; the jsonb shape leaves room for the scope doc's other named certifications (GAF Master Elite, Owens Corning Platinum Preferred, CertainTeed SELECT) without another migration, but none of those are built -- noted so a future session doesn't assume they are. (3) api/rf-auth.js: new owner-only `set_certifications` action (separate from `setup` on purpose -- folding it in would force a pin/role change just to flip a cert flag), validated against a known-keys allowlist so it can never become a free-form data store; `roster` now also returns certifications. (4) api/sd-data.js: new `rf_photos` branch (visibility keyed off the target job's own assigned_employee_id, captured_by always server-stamped from the session, never client-supplied); `rf_jobs` write extended with two new sub-objects -- `measurement_correction` (append-only, DELIBERATELY SIMPLER than alf_clients' care_level_history: the client sends ONE new entry and the server appends it, rather than resending and prefix-diffing the whole array; no separate derived "current" field either, since the last history entry IS current and nothing can drift out of sync with it) and `estimate` (material + line items, refused outright for the narrow tier -- foreman/crew can correct a measurement on their own job but cannot price one, matching the confirmed role scope). UNIT COSTS ARE NEVER DEFAULTED SERVER-SIDE OR CLIENT-SIDE -- always caller-supplied per line item; inventing a $/SF number would be exactly the fabricated-figure class Guardian exists to catch. Line totals and subtotal are recomputed server-side from qty×unit_cost rather than trusted from the client. THE TESLA GATE ITSELF: selecting tesla_solar_roof on an unassigned job is refused outright (400, "assign a foreman or crew member first") rather than silently allowed with nothing to check; on an assigned job, the assigned employee's `certifications.tesla_certified` is read fresh from Supabase on every save (not cached, not trusted from a prior response) and a false/missing value is refused with a named, specific message identifying the installer -- not a generic FORBIDDEN. CROSS-TAB DATA-LOSS AVOIDED BY DESIGN, not discovered as a bug: because rf_jobs' `data` column is a whole-document replace on every write (same as every other resource on this endpoint, not new to Phase 2), a Measurement or Estimate tab save that didn't explicitly carry forward the OTHER sub-object would have silently erased it -- server resolves both `measurement` and `estimate` from the existing row when a given call doesn't touch them, and the frontend's three save functions (saveJobOverview/saveMeasurementReading/submitMeasurementCorrection/saveEstimate) all send the job's full base fields alongside whatever they're actually changing, sourced from one shared `rfBaseFieldsPayload()` helper rather than re-typed per call site. FRONTEND: photo capture (`capture="environment"`) -> ONE Claude inference via the existing proxy pattern, system prompt demanding exactly ten labelled lines (Squares/Ridge LF/Hip LF/Valley LF/Eave LF/Rake LF/Penetrations/Pitch Class/Stories/Waste Factor %) or "unknown" per line rather than a guess -- free text + targeted regex extraction, the same proven StoneDesk baseline sairn-app-scaffold specifies, not a fragile JSON contract. Parsed values land in an editable form; nothing is saved until the estimator/foreman reviews and clicks Save -- the call that talks to Claude never itself writes anything, matching §1.3's single-inference, human-reviewed design-around exactly. A management-only Tesla-certified-installer checklist (roster + toggle, calling set_certifications live) renders inside the Estimate tab. Added `rfDataRaw()` (mirrors rfAuth's real {ok,status,data,msg,code} shape) specifically so the Tesla gate's refusal MESSAGE reaches the user instead of being swallowed into a generic "Save failed" by the existing null-on-failure rfData() -- a gate whose reason is invisible to the person it blocks is not meaningfully enforced. NOT LIVE-VERIFIED -- nothing pushed yet, and the two new migrations (rf_photos table, certifications column) have not been run against Supabase, so bootstrap/measurement/estimate/Tesla-gate behavior is unconfirmed against the real database, same disclosed-not-assumed posture as every other unpushed batch tonight. Photo history gallery, per-material default pricing, and the storm/insurance module all remain deliberately unbuilt -- out of THIS pass's scope, not gaps in it. 2026-08-24

Worth escalating as a real Supabase/PostgREST reliability signal if it recurs a fourth time, since it is now a pattern across two different actions rather than one flaky call. - SYSTEMIC service_role EXCESS-PRIVILEGE GAP found (by Michael, on dnt_credentials, during the SAIRNdental verification above) and ROOT-CAUSED PLATFORM-WIDE (CC) -- not my discovery, my investigation. service_role held select/insert/TRUNCATE/REFERENCES/TRIGGER on dnt_credentials with no update/delete -- the migration only ever granted select+insert. ROOT CAUSE CONFIRMED FROM WHAT'S ON DISK, not guessed: grep -rniE "alter default privileges|grant all" across sql/, api/, docs/, .claude/ returns zero hits, so no SAIRN file ever ran that. It is already documented from an EARLIER real incident in .claude/skills/sairn-infra-debugger/SKILL.md's "GRANTs and RLS are different things" section: a table created by raw SQL migration (every table on this platform, since none use the Table Editor UI) gets TRUNCATE/REFERENCES/TRIGGER from Supabase's own default privileges for the public schema. The established fix pattern since the StoneDesk fe730e2 incident (2026-08-08) has been to explicitly GRANT whatever verbs an app needs -- but a GRANT only ADDS, it never strips the TRUNCATE/REFERENCES/TRIGGER baseline underneath it, so that half of the fix was never written anywhere except by accident. TWO TABLES GOT IT RIGHT WITHOUT CHASING THIS SPECIFIC BUG: stonedesk_audit_log and sairncode_audit_log both do `revoke all on <table> from service_role;` before their grant, which happens to clear the baseline too. CONFIRMED THE SAME ASYMMETRIC SHAPE (grant-only-adds, no revoke-all-from-service_role) ON: dnt_cred_rules (same file), alf_signals, alf_claim_routes, alf_staff_credentials, sairnlaw_audit_log (a PARTIAL prior fix -- it revokes update/delete, which were never granted anyway, and never touched truncate/references/trigger), sairnscape_org_intel, and -- found by checking my OWN work rather than assuming it was clean -- all THREE of tonight's new SAIRNroofing tables (rf_photos, rf_jobs, sairnroofing_employee_auth), all already live on production with the same gap. FIX WRITTEN, `sql/service_role_excess_privilege_audit_2026-08-24.sql`: Section 1 lets Michael confirm the diagnosis live (has_table_privilege checks plus a pg_default_acl read) BEFORE trusting Section 3's fix, per standing instruction not to bolt on a revoke without knowing why the privilege is there. Section 2 is a systemic sweep query across ALL public tables that does NOT depend on this session's own hand-built list, so it will surface anything missed by grep. Section 3 is the actual fix (revoke all from service_role, then re-grant exactly the verbs each table's own code uses) for every table named above. Section 4 is a re-check expecting zero rows. ALSO PATCHED IN-REPO for future re-runs (not yet run against production -- these three files were already applied once tonight before this gap was found): sql/sairnroofing_photos_schema.sql, sql/sairnroofing_jobs_schema.sql, sql/sairnroofing_employee_auth_schema.sql each now do the same revoke-all-then-grant shape. NOT TOUCHED: dnt_credentials/dnt_cred_rules/alf_*/sairnlaw_audit_log/sairnscape_org_intel's own schema files -- those belong to other apps' sessions; the runnable fix was handed over instead of silently rewriting another app's committed migration. SCOPE BOUNDARY STATED IN THE FILE ITSELF: table OWNERSHIP (postgres) is untouched by any of this and never was -- same disclosed limitation as sql/audit_log_immutability_verify.sql. Non-append-only tables (StoneDesk's slab tables, SAIRNgrounds/MSB's tables) very likely carry the same excess TRUNCATE and were deliberately left OUT of Section 3's fix list -- they don't defeat a specific append-only guarantee the way it does on the tables above, and Section 2's sweep will surface them for a real follow-up pass rather than folding a platform-wide rewrite into this one. NOT RUN by me -- no DB access, same standing limitation as every other raw-SQL claim tonight. 2026-08-24
- GRANT-GAP FIX RECONCILED WITH A CONCURRENT SESSION, MY FILE SUPERSEDED AND DELETED (CC). Same session doing SAIRNdental verification independently investigated the identical TRUNCATE/REFERENCES/TRIGGER gap in parallel and pushed two commits while mine was in flight -- genuinely two screens under two names, not duplicated effort either side could have known about ahead of time. Their pass had DB access I don't: they ran the actual pg_default_acl query live and got the DEFINITIVE root cause my file could only infer -- postgres's DEFAULT ACL for the public schema grants service_role TRUNCATE/REFERENCES/TRIGGER/MAINTAIN on every table postgres creates, confirmed by reading it, not reasoned from sairn-infra-debugger's older "raw-SQL-migration quirk" framing (which predates MAINTAIN, a PG17+ privilege that showed up in the real output, confirming this server is 17+). That distinction is real, not cosmetic: a creation quirk implies per-table remediation is the whole job; a default ACL means every FUTURE table starts wrong too -- which is exactly what their Section 4 (`alter default privileges for role postgres in schema public revoke truncate, references, trigger, maintain on tables from service_role`) fixes and my file never could without a source-level statement. Their final file (`sql/append_only_grant_audit.sql`, commit 4f4852f) explicitly folds in everything real from mine (the list-free Section 2 discovery sweep, has_table_privilege() as the confirmation style, dnt_cred_rules/rf_jobs/sairnroofing_employee_auth as the three tables that correctly KEEP update, and a schema-qualification fix on sairnroofing_employee_auth's revoke that my own version had left unqualified) and states plainly that both superseded files should be deleted so two runnable-looking scripts don't leave it ambiguous which one produced a given state. Canonical file going forward for this whole finding: sql/append_only_grant_audit.sql. Structure: Section 1 diagnose (pg_default_acl + dnt_credentials real grants), Section 2 list-free discovery sweep, Section 3 fix the 11 live tables (revoke all then re-grant exactly what each needs -- 3 keep UPDATE, 8 are append-only), Section 4 fix the source so it can't recur, Section 5 verify (expect zero rows from Section 2's re-run). None of this run yet -- Michael's to run, same as everything else. 2026-08-24
- SESSION-LOCK COLLISION WARNING BUILT (CC), per Michael's instruction after confirming tonight's grant-gap investigation really was two Claude Code sessions independently working the identical task with no way for either to know the other existed. Design proposed and approved before building (Michael confirmed the 2-hour staleness threshold and the "right call" on stating the real scope boundary plainly rather than overselling). `tools/session_lock_check.py` (committed here; a working copy also placed at `C:/Users/marsh/tools/session_lock_check.py`, matching the existing convention every other hook-invoked script on this platform already uses -- committed config, globally-located script). Lock files live at `~/SAIRN-SESSION-LOCKS/<clone>.lock`, OUTSIDE every git clone so they never enter history and need no push/pull to be visible to another session on the same machine. Wired via `.claude/settings.json` (committed, propagates to all four clones on next pull): `SessionStart` writes/checks the lock, `UserPromptSubmit` heartbeats it. A REAL DESIGN BUG WAS FOUND AND FIXED DURING BUILD, before it shipped: the original plan (matching what was proposed and approved) used the recorded PID to decide whether a `heartbeat`/`end` call was touching or clearing its OWN lock. Every hook firing is a brand-new short-lived `python` subprocess, so `os.getpid()` differs on every single invocation -- there is no stable identity across a session's lifetime available to a hook script this way, which would have made heartbeat silently never refresh anything and made a `SessionEnd` cleanup hook actively dangerous (one session's exit could delete a DIFFERENT, still-running session's lock). Fixed by dropping the SessionEnd hook entirely (never wired) and making heartbeat an unconditional touch -- not a downgrade from what was proposed, since the proposal itself already named the 2-hour timeout as the mechanism that had to work standalone, with clean-exit deletion explicitly framed as a bonus contingent on verifying it could be done safely. It couldn't, so it was left out rather than shipped broken. LIVE-TESTED, not just read: fresh start with no existing lock -> silent, lock written; a second start immediately after -> the real warning JSON, correct PID/timestamp/age in the message; heartbeat advances the lock's mtime (confirmed via a real before/after stat); a lock manually backdated 3 hours -> silently reclaimed, no warning, matching the 2-hour threshold; heartbeat with no lock file present at all -> no crash, exit 0; a non-`SAIRN-`-prefixed directory -> falls back to its own folder name rather than erroring, confirmed it wrote a distinct `some-other-dir.lock`. All test artifacts cleaned up afterward. NOT PUSHED YET -- committed locally only; the design doesn't take effect in the other three clones until they each pull `.claude/settings.json`, so pushing this one matters more than most commits tonight. STATED SCOPE BOUNDARY, carried from the design proposal into the script's own header comment so a future reader doesn't need this log entry to know it: this catches two sessions claiming the SAME clone directory. It does NOT catch two DIFFERENT clones independently converging on the same external task, which is what actually happened tonight -- that would need a task-level registry, deliberately not built. 2026-08-24
- PUSH-HANG ROOT-CAUSED FOR REAL, REPRODUCED WITH TRACING RATHER THAN GUESSED (CC). Six occurrences tonight across two clones, always cleared on retry, never actually investigated until asked. Reproduced live: `git push --dry-run origin main` with `GIT_TRACE=1 GIT_CURL_VERBOSE=1 GIT_TRACE_PERFORMANCE=1 GCM_TRACE=1` hung the full 5 minutes until killed. THE TRACE IS UNAMBIGUOUS: the HTTPS handshake to github.com completes cleanly (full response headers received, "Connection #0 to host github.com:443 left intact") -- the network path is fine, ruling out proxy/VPN/machine-network causes outright, and this is a `--dry-run` with zero object transfer, ruling out payload size. Git then shells out to `git credential-manager get`. GCM's own trace shows it finds TWO stored accounts for `https://github.com` -- `SAIRN1` and `x-access-token` (confirmed independently via `git credential-manager github list`) -- and logs `[GetCredentialAsync] Multiple accounts available - prompting user to select one...`, then goes SILENT for the remaining ~4+ minutes. That is the hang: GCM is trying to show an interactive account-picker, which cannot be answered in a non-interactive shell, so it blocks forever with no further output and no error -- exactly the "looks like a hang, isn't network-bound" shape flagged before investigating. No pre-push hook exists (`.git/hooks/` has only `.sample` files, checked directly) and no proxy is configured (`http.proxy`/`https.proxy` both empty) -- both ruled out with direct evidence, not assumed clear. Time-of-night has no causal role in this mechanism -- coincidental, not a real correlate. WHICH ACCOUNT IS CORRECT NOT DETERMINED -- did not read either credential's actual token value (would mean displaying a live secret to inspect it, which is exactly the discipline this platform holds everywhere else), so this is Michael's call, not guessed. FIX NOT YET APPLIED, findings reported first per instruction: the low-risk option is `git config credential.https://github.com.username <correct-one>`, which resolves the disambiguation deterministically without touching the credential store at all (fully reversible, no secret deleted); a stricter option layered on top is forcing GCM non-interactive (so a future new/expired/third account fails fast with a real error instead of hanging silently again) -- both proposed, neither applied, pending which account Michael confirms is the live one. 2026-08-24
- WHICH GITHUB ACCOUNT ACTUALLY WORKS -- ANSWERED WITH DIRECT EVIDENCE, NOT A GUESS (CC). Michael asked to check GCM's own logs/cache for a tie-break signal before asking him to pick blind. `git credential-manager diagnose` didn't have it (its "Credential storage" section is a generic self-test, not a read of the real SAIRN1/x-access-token entries; its "GitHub API" check hits the public unauthenticated `/meta` endpoint, proving nothing about which account is valid) -- both checked and ruled out as sources of signal, not assumed empty. The real answer came from retrying the exact same traced `git push --dry-run` again: this time it did NOT hang -- it sat at "prompting user to select one" for about 6 seconds, then resolved on its own: `[GetCredentialAsync] Looking for existing credential in store with service=https://github.com account=SAIRN1... Existing credential found.` Sent `Authorization: Basic` as user `SAIRN1`, and GitHub answered a real `HTTP/1.1 200 OK` on `/SAIRN1/SAIRN.git/info/refs` -- a genuine successful authentication, not an assumption. The push itself only failed afterward for an ordinary, unrelated reason (`[rejected] main -> main (fetch first)`, someone else had pushed since the last fetch -- fixed by a normal fetch+merge, completed right after). **SAIRN1 is confirmed live and working; x-access-token was matched as a candidate during filtering but never actually selected in this resolution.** This also explains the INCONSISTENT hang pattern itself: GCM's fallback-when-no-interactive-prompt-is-possible logic is not reliably fast -- one attempt hung the full 5 minutes with zero resolution, the very next attempt resolved to SAIRN1 in ~6 seconds with nothing about the environment changed. That inconsistency, not a real environmental variable, is why some of tonight's six occurrences cleared quickly on retry and others needed a longer wait. FIX NOW READY TO APPLY ON REAL EVIDENCE: `git config credential.https://github.com.username SAIRN1` (or the equivalent `credential."https://github.com".username` form) removes the ambiguity GCM is timing out on, deterministically, without deleting the x-access-token entry from the store at all -- reversible, no secret touched. NOT YET APPLIED -- proposed, pending Michael's go-ahead, consistent with treating a git-config change as worth a confirm rather than silently applying it mid-investigation. 2026-08-24
- PUSH-HANG FIX APPLIED AND VERIFIED ACROSS MULTIPLE REAL ATTEMPTS, NOT ONE (CC). Michael approved applying `git config credential.https://github.com.username SAIRN1` -- set with `--global` rather than repo-local, deliberately, since the two-account ambiguity lives in the Windows Credential Manager store shared by this whole machine, so `hank`/`cody`/`fourth` get the same fix without each needing their own local config. Confirmed set via a real read-back (`git config --global --get`), not assumed from the write succeeding. VERIFIED WITH THREE SEPARATE PUSH ATTEMPTS, per instruction that tonight's own inconsistent timing meant one clean run wasn't enough to call it closed: three traced `git push --dry-run` calls, each a genuinely separate process invocation (not one command run three times in a loop), each timed. All three: 1.3-1.8 seconds total, ZERO occurrences of "prompting user to select one" in any of the three traces (grep-counted, not eyeballed), each resolving straight to `account=SAIRN1` with no delay. `--dry-run` is the right test here, not a shortcut around one: the earlier trace already proved the hang happens entirely at the credential-resolution step, before any object transfer begins, so dry-run exercises the exact mechanism that was broken. Then, separately, this very log entry's own real (non-dry-run) push served as a fourth, genuine confirmation with actual data transfer involved, not just credential resolution. All temp trace logs cleaned up after each run. Root cause, fix, and now multi-run verification all closed out for this issue -- the six-occurrences pattern from earlier tonight should not recur on this machine going forward. 2026-08-24
- BROADER TRUNCATE SWEEP DIAGNOSED, DRAFT FIX WRITTEN, NOTHING RUN (CC). Picked up the item deliberately deferred and logged as a named follow-up earlier tonight. Used the same list-free structural technique that found the original append-only gap, extended to every schema file rather than only the ones tagged append-only: `grep -rhE "^grant select" sql/*.sql | ... sort -u | wc -l` = 158 distinct granted tables platform-wide; `grep -l "revoke all.*from service_role" sql/*.sql` = only 6 real schema files use the sound pattern (sairncode_audit_log, stonedesk_audit_log, and this session's own four SAIRNroofing files), covering 7 of the 158. **151 tables are code-level candidates for the same excess TRUNCATE/REFERENCES/TRIGGER/MAINTAIN baseline** -- counted directly, not estimated. CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED, per instruction, for the two named examples: StoneDesk's `sd_slabs`/`sd_blocks`/`sd_bundles`/`sd_slab_history` and sampled SAIRNgrounds/MSB tables (`grd_schedule`, `grd_invasive_sightings`, `msb_products`, `msb_sales`) all genuinely write via real `?on_conflict=...` upserts in `api/sd-data.js` -- confirmed UPDATE is real and needed, narrowing it would break live writes. DELETE is a different story: a platform-wide grep for `method: 'DELETE'` in `api/sd-data.js` returns exactly ONE occurrence anywhere in the whole file, and reading that call site directly confirms it's the SC_RESOURCES (SAIRNcode) Compliance-Admin-gated branch -- re-verifying a claim the open-work-index already made rather than trusting it blind. StoneDesk slabs and SAIRNgrounds/MSB are granted DELETE at the DB level with zero live code path that ever uses it. THAT IS A SEPARATE FINDING, DELIBERATELY NOT FOLDED INTO THIS FIX: narrowing an unused DELETE grant is a real functional-capability change, categorically different from stripping a TRUNCATE/REFERENCES/TRIGGER/MAINTAIN baseline that was never intentionally granted anywhere (zero explicit `references`/`trigger`/`maintain` grants exist in any sql/*.sql file) -- flagged for its own review rather than silently riding along. WROTE `sql/full_crud_truncate_sweep_2026-08-24.sql`: Section 1 is the real list-free discovery query (`information_schema`, no hand-built table list, so it will catch anything the 151-table code count missed). Section 2 is a DO-block draft fix, commented out, NOT run -- reads each table's OWN current SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE grants live and re-asserts exactly that same set, so it is structurally incapable of adding or removing any functional privilege, only ever stripping the unused excess. Chose a dynamic block over 151 hand-written REVOKE/GRANT pairs on purpose: hand-transcribing that many statements is exactly the kind of manual step that introduces the next real bug (a dropped or added privilege by copy-paste mistake), where reading the current state and re-asserting it mechanically cannot make that mistake by construction. Section 3 is the re-verify (expect zero rows). Logged in `docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md`, status updated from "deliberately deferred" to "diagnosed, draft fix written, not run." Nothing executed against the real database -- no DB access, same standing limitation as every other raw-SQL claim tonight, and explicitly asked to hold here regardless. 2026-08-24

SEPARATE AND MORE URGENT: `network_insights` is REAL and USED (`api/network.js`) with a real schema file (`sql/network_schema.sql`) -- but that file has NO grant statement at all, meaning service_role has held only the TRUNCATE/REFERENCES/TRIGGER/MAINTAIN default since creation and api/network.js has never actually been able to select or insert on it. NOT EXCLUDED, VERIFIED SAFE DESPITE NO SCHEMA FILE: `ai_memories`, `bridge_data`, `sairn_agents`, `sairn_agent_commands` also have no tracked CREATE TABLE anywhere but ARE real, ARE used, and are NOT in the zero-CRUD list -- meaning service_role already has real access today (almost certainly via Table Editor UI creation, which auto-grants correctly per sairn-infra-debugger's own note, unlike raw SQL migrations). Section 2 is safe for these the normal way: it reads and re-asserts whatever they already have, so a missing schema file doesn't create the same risk it does for license_keys, since there's a real current grant state to preserve rather than an absent expectation to guess at. Section 2's draft DO block updated with an explicit `AND t.table_name NOT IN ('license_keys', 'network_insights')` exclusion rather than leaving the caution as prose Michael would have to remember to apply by hand. All of this written into `sql/full_crud_truncate_sweep_2026-08-24.sql` itself so the reasoning travels with the file. - SAIRNroofing Phase 3c supplement reconciliation -- LIVE ROUND TRIP, 39/39, no code change needed (CC, 2026-08-25). Session before this one was lost to a terminal crash mid-3c; recovered by reading real state rather than trusting the crash summary. What was actually true on disk: 3c had ALREADY landed on origin/main as `c27f5a2` (engine + endpoint + panel + 28 isolation tests) while the local clone was one docs commit behind and diverged -- merged, not rebased. So the open work was not "build 3c", it was "3c has never been run against the real deployment", which is exactly the gap the Push Protocol exists to close. LIVE-VERIFIED against sairn.vercel.app with real signed session tokens and real Supabase rows, on the disposable RF-PINNACLE-2026 accounts (rf-verify-admin + the two 3b foremen, all still active). THE ARITHMETIC, hand-checked against a real measured scope seeded through the ordinary Phase 2 measurement_correction path (44 SQ / 120 ridge LF / 36 valley LF / 210 drip LF): shingles 44 measured - 40 allowed = 4 short @ $325 = $1300; ridge_cap's TWO adjuster lines 60+60 summed to 120 and matched exactly -> $0 while still flagging the duplicate key; drip_edge where the adjuster allowed 230 against 210 measured produced `adjuster_over` at $0, NOT a negative that could net away a real shortfall somewhere else; valley_metal with no adjuster line at all -> omitted, 36 @ $12 = $432; ice_water with no measured value -> `no_measurement`, flagged rather than silently treated as zero quantity. Asserted side: the hidden_damage line WITH a photo counted 8 @ $78 = $624, the code_upgrade line with NO photo was marked invalid and contributed exactly $0. Totals $1732 derived + $624 asserted = $2356. THE THREE CHECKS THAT MATTER MORE THAN THE ARITHMETIC, all proven live and not inferred: (1) a caller CANNOT substitute the measured scope -- sending payload.measured {squares:999} changed nothing, measured_from_job stayed 44 because the handler reads the job's latest entry server-side; (2) `reconcile` WRITES NOTHING -- the claim row and the whole rf_jobs set were identical (key-sorted compare) after two further reconcile calls, so the worksheet really is derived-on-demand like money_summary and never persisted; (3) the assignment gate holds on the compute verb, not just on the read -- assigned foreman 200 with the real total, a DIFFERENT foreman 403 FORBIDDEN with no worksheet leaked in the error body, no session 401. Error surface: missing claim_id 400, unknown claim 404 NO_CLAIM, and a claim on a job with no measurement at all returns has_measurement:false rather than crashing. TWO FALSE FAILURES IN THE FIRST RUN, both the harness's fault and worth remembering: Postgres jsonb does not preserve object KEY ORDER, so comparing a stored blob with plain JSON.stringify reports a mismatch on identical data -- compare key-sorted; and the first harness was not re-runnable (its has_measurement:false case only worked on a virgin job, and re-running appended a second measurement entry), fixed by giving the empty case its own permanently-unmeasured job. NOT DONE / NEXT: the disposable rows and all three accounts are STILL LIVE on RF-PINNACLE-2026 -- `sql/sairnroofing_verify_3b_cleanup.sql` now covers 3a+3b+3c in one pass (scope widened, filename deliberately unchanged since 3c's rows match the existing LIKE patterns) and has NOT been run. Also logged as its own index row, deliberately not folded into this work: `rf_claims` read returns `claim_id` but `rf_jobs` read returns the job id as `id` (api/sd-data.js:2491), two sibling resources with different key names for the same concept.

- SAIRNroofing PHASE 5 COMPLETE -- contingency agreement, signature state, rescission clock, 51/51 LIVE (CC, 2026-08-25). Closes the last unbuilt item of the scope doc's section 5.3 and with it Phase 5 entirely. Before this, `contingency_signed` was a string in a status enum and nothing else. THE RESEARCH DROVE THE DESIGN, not the other way round: four jurisdictions read from primary sources (codes.ohio.gov, C.R.S., Fla. Stat., Tex. Bus.&Com./Ins. Code) and they disagree on EVERY axis -- Ohio 3 business days from the transaction but only for a sale solicited away from the seller's fixed place of business, Colorado 72 HOURS from the insurer's WRITTEN DENIAL (a different trigger event entirely), Florida 10 calendar days from EXECUTION for declared-emergency contracts, Texas no rescission right at all in Bus.&Com. 27.02 (it mandates a 12-point boldface deductible notice instead). A hardcoded template would have been wrong in three of four, so the rule is a row with a CHECK-enforced citation, same shape as rf_cert_rules. THE BUG WORTH REMEMBERING: the first draft computed "business days" as Monday-to-Friday, the ordinary meaning. R.C. 1345.21 defines a business day for Ohio's Home Solicitation Sales Act as "any calendar day except Sunday" plus eleven named holidays -- SATURDAY IS A BUSINESS DAY IN OHIO. On a Thursday signing that is Monday, not Tuesday: a full day of exposure the contractor would not know they had. Caught by reading the DEFINITIONS section after already reading the operative one, and fixed by making the basis a property of the rule (oh_hssa vs mon_fri) rather than of the code; because Ohio enumerates its holidays they are now applied exactly instead of disclosed as a gap, while a mon_fri result still states on its face that no calendar was applied. THE FEATURE THAT ONLY EXISTS BECAUSE THE STATUTE WAS READ: R.C. 1345.23 requires BOTH a conspicuous notice AND a separate detachable duplicate cancellation form; fail either and the buyer's right to cancel does NOT expire, it runs until the seller complies. Modelled as its own status ('indefinite'), proven live months past any 3-day window. A roofer missing the form has open-ended exposure on every contract they ever signed and no calendar anywhere shows it -- that is the mechanical-check-against-real-data class this platform exists for. BUILT: api/_lib/roofing-agreements.js (pure, no I/O, no LLM), sql/sairnroofing_agreements_schema.sql (rf_contingency_rules + rf_claim_agreements, the latter APPEND-ONLY with select/insert and NO update grant), sql/sairnroofing_contingency_seed_ohio.json (Ohio only; CO/FL/TX recorded under _states_researched_but_NOT_seeded), three api/sd-data.js branches incl. the compute-only `agreement_status`, and the Contingency Agreement card in sairnroofing.html with canvas signature capture. LIVE-PROVEN (51/51 on RF-PINNACLE-2026 after Michael ran the schema and re-seeded the three disposable accounts): Thu 6 Aug + 3 Ohio business days = midnight MON 10 Aug; append-only holds (409 ALREADY_RECORDED on id reuse, original signer survived, no update verb exists); rescission is a second row that must name a real target (no supersedes -> 400, bogus supersedes -> 400 NO_SUCH_AGREEMENT, real one accepted and BOTH rows survive); recorded_by server-stamped with a caller-supplied 'SOMEONE-ELSE' discarded; the caller CANNOT substitute the governing state (state:CO against an Ohio-signed agreement still resolved the Ohio rule); agreement_status writes nothing; the gate holds on all three verbs (wrong foreman 403 on read/write/status with no status leaked into the error body, no session 401). THREE EMPTY STATES KEPT DISTINCT rather than collapsed: rule_not_applicable (signed at the shop), no_rule (Wyoming -- and it says in words that this is NOT the same as "no right to cancel"), indefinite (no form). TWO CORRECTIONS MADE TO EXISTING FILES rather than left to disagree: sql/sairnroofing_claims_schema.sql's comment anticipated the signature living in rf_claims.data -- wrong, and corrected in the same commit (that file's own rf_claim_photos argument applies verbatim: evidence that can be edited after the fact is not evidence; plus a 64KB cap vs an image, plus rescission needing a later record). And a payload issue I introduced and then fixed before pushing: the read branch was shipping the full 1.5MB signature on every claim open when nothing renders it -- now stripped unless include_signature is passed, with has_signature flagged either way. CLOSED DECISION RECORDED IN SOURCE, per Michael's call: the Tex. Ins. Code 4102.163 public-adjuster boundary is written into api/_lib/roofing-supplement.js the way SAIRNcash's Bridge exclusion is. TDI lists "offering to negotiate settlements, promising recovery of funds, or representing the policyholder in coverage discussions" as prohibited conduct for a roofing contractor. The 3c engine is on the right side by construction and the comment records exactly why. Panel copy audited -- only hits were "recoverable depreciation", a statutory money-field name. The operations assistant's system prompt now DECLINES claim-strategy questions outright: it was never given claim data, but nothing stopped it answering a negotiation question from general knowledge, and an app-branded answer of that shape was the real exposure. NOT DONE / NEXT: cleanup NOT run -- sql/sairnroofing_verify_3b_cleanup.sql now covers 3a+3b+3c+5 in one pass and its inventory names the P5 rows explicitly, including the ids deliberately ABSENT because the endpoint refused them. Two new index rows logged rather than folded in: only Ohio is seeded and SAIRNlaw's real holiday calendar is not wired into the mon_fri basis; and the claim assignment gate is now written four ways in api/sd-data.js (Phase 5 factored one helper and used it only for the new branches -- retrofitting the live 3b/3c branches inside a feature commit is how a regression gets in unnoticed). Phase 4 Operations is next per Michael's direction. 2026-08-25

- SAIRNroofing PHASE 4 COMPLETE (4c built) + THE rfDataRaw READ BUG THAT HID FIVE PHASES OF UI (CC, 2026-08-25). Commits 0df5699, ee2411f, 4d86518, 7d7708e. THE BIGGER FINDING IS NOT 4c: `rfDataRaw` returned `data:(d&&d.data)` while its sibling `rfAuth` returns `data:d`, and every one of its consumers was written against rfAuth's convention (`r.data.<field>`). A read body is {ok,provisioned,data:[...]}, so `r.data` WAS the rows array and `r.data.data` was undefined on every call. Latent from Phase 2 (31e0337) where the only callers were writes touching r.ok/r.msg; LIVE from Phase 3b, the first read caller. THIRTEEN consumers across Phases 3b/4a/4b/5 silently rendered empty or null -- schedule list, locations list, invoice list, proposal state, agreement chain, agreement status (x2), programmes board and its roster_size footer, the claim ACV-mismatch note, and both invoice-issue toasts. EVERY ONE OF THOSE PHASES PASSED LIVE API VERIFICATION: curl exercised the endpoint, nothing exercised this line, and an empty panel reads as 'no data yet'. Guardian's own Known-Scope-Limitation section already warns a clean pass on an auth-gated app means 'the code looks right assuming the gate opens' -- correct, and not acted on for this app until now. THE FIX WAS NOT ONE LINE AND ASSUMING SO WOULD HAVE REGRESSED: audited per call site against each branch's real response body -- OLD 0/13 correct, NEW 13/13, zero regressions -- which is how the claim ACV-note turned up as the ONE consumer correct under the old convention, because a WRITE reply nests its record under `data` (api/sd-data.js:2994) while reads and compute actions sit at the top level. Both lines moved together. PHASE 4c: five reports (Jobs/Claims/Schedule/Certifications/Invoices), gate INHERITED not re-asserted -- each report calls the ordinary gated read, so a CSV cannot hold a row the caller could not open, structurally rather than by a remembered rule; ZERO new lines in api/sd-data.js. A server-side export action was rejected deliberately (second read path, gate in two places -- the exact shape api/rf-auth.js's header records SAIRNsenior drifting on). Proposals deferred with a reason (its read is per-job). Honest empty states, and NONE of the three writes a file: not-provisioned, zero-visible-rows, and 403-as-refusal. Formula injection neutralised (a roofing CSV goes to a bookkeeper) with real negatives exempt. THE 4c DEFECT THE BROWSER CAUGHT: the Jobs export wrote a BLANK identifier on all 8 rows -- mapped to `job_id`, but rf_jobs' read RENAMES it (api/sd-data.js:2495 maps `id: r.job_id`), the only one of the five that renames. The panel said 'Exported 8 jobs -- 7 columns' and the file had 8 populated rows; only the cells were wrong. Same shape as the helper bug: a green result standing in for a correct one. Then audited ALL 62 columns rather than the one caught; nine more resolved empty and every one was PROVED to be absent test data by seeding the values rather than reasoning about them (three certifications, because `jurisdiction` only exists on a local_license and `has_expiry` only on a card declaring it has none). 62/62 resolve, zero wrong paths. GATE PROVEN AGAINST A NARROW ROLE, not just asserted: rf-verify-fmA sees Jobs 1-of-8, Claims 0-of-1, Schedule 0-of-1, Certifications 0-of-3, Invoices 403 FORBIDDEN; rows visible to the foreman but not the owner = 0; in the browser the Invoices button is ABSENT FROM THE DOM, not disabled, and the Certifications empty-state wrote no file (confirmed on disk). A FALSE ALARM I RAISED AND CORRECTED, worth more than the fixes: I reported an invoice-counter regression that did not exist. rf_invoice_counters is keyed (license_hash, location_id) -- one row per licence PER BRANCH (billing schema:9, unique at :146) and the allocator takes the invoice's own branch (sd-data.js:3337), so a new branch legitimately starts at INV-00001. LOC-DEFAULT verified still at next_seq=3 exactly where 4b left it, LOC-UIVERIFY at 2. Also: rf_invoice_counters has NO read path -- only the incrementing RPC -- so observing it through the API means burning a number. CLEANUP RUN (sql/_scratch_rf_uiverify_cleanup.sql, gitignored): rf-verify-ui deactivated never deleted, the LOC-UIVERIFY counter row deliberately LEFT (deleting restarts a branch at a number already issued), rf-verify-fmA/fmB left active as Phase 4a fixtures. NOTE FOR ANY FUTURE CLEANUP: no roofing table grants DELETE to service_role -- all fourteen grant lines are select/insert(/update) -- so teardown must run as the SQL editor, the app cannot do it. Also added sql/_scratch_* to .gitignore; those files had sat untracked for sessions, one `git add -A` from committing real license_hash values. OPEN: tax-provenance row 141 now surfaces in invoice exports as tax_basis 'amount' with neither a rate nor an amount (pre-existing, wider audience now); cross-job Proposals report; the duplicated signature pad. THE METHODOLOGY POINT: every real defect tonight was found by OPENING THE ARTEFACT -- the rendered panel, the downloaded file -- never by the operation reporting success, and two separate bugs had a green success message sitting directly on top of them. Handoff: SAIRNROOFING-2026-08-25-phase4c-and-rfdataraw-handoff.md. 2026-08-25

- SAIRNROOFING REPAIR-VS-REPLACE BUILT TWICE THE SAME NIGHT; MINE DELETED, CODY'S KEPT (CC, 2026-08-26). Cody shipped a411eae + 4d4410f (roofing-damage-assessment.js, rf_settings, assess_damage, UI) while I built the same feature end to end locally (roofing-repair.js, rf_repair_thresholds, assess_repair, panel, 17 engine + 18 endpoint tests). Found only when my rebase collided in api/_resources/sairnroofing.js on the exact lines where both registered. NOTHING OF MINE REACHED origin/main; local commit discarded with git reset --hard origin/main after Michael chose. WHY THE PROTECTIONS DIDN'T FIRE, all four checked rather than assumed: the session lock DID fire but only sees the SAME clone (its own text says so); the per-clone active-work files had no entry because an entry is written at the END of a work item and both of us were mid-build; the open-work index had no row because it was a fresh decision; and I DID grep before starting -- against the local working tree, which was level with origin/main AT THAT MOMENT, and Cody's first commit landed after. The check was correct and still missed, because a working tree is a snapshot of a moment. THE FIX, proposed not applied (canonical skill content is at C:/SAIRN/skills/sairn/ with the repo copy tracked, so it is a two-place change needing verify-skill-store.sh): git fetch, then `git grep origin/main` and `git log HEAD..origin/main` immediately before building AND again before the first commit. Written up in docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-26-parallel-duplicate-build-lesson.md. A COMPOUNDING CAUSE WORTH ITS OWN LINE: sairnroofing.html's patent boundary comment asserted IN THE PAST TENSE that element 4 'was supplied ... by adding the repair-vs-replace determination' while no such code existed anywhere -- checked against origin/main, not just a working copy. I read that, could not find the feature, and reasonably concluded it was still to do. A claimed-but-unbuilt feature does not merely mislead about status, IT INVITES A SECOND PERSON TO BUILD IT. Cody has since corrected that comment to 'is being supplied'. THE COMPARISON EARNED ITS KEEP EVEN THOUGH MY VERSION DIED (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-26-repair-vs-replace-two-implementations.md, both engines read in full, behaviour not headers). CODY'S IS BETTER ON: density -- counted/test_squares with no rounding, where mine compared the RAW count to the threshold and would have called 12 hits over 3 squares a 12 against a threshold of 8, a real defect in mine I only found by reading theirs; outcome vocabulary (meets_threshold/below_threshold describes EVIDENCE, where my supports_replacement edges toward entitlement -- the exact line Tex. Ins. Code 4102.163 and Fla. Stat. 626.854 draw for a contractor servicing the property); refusing ok:false on bad input instead of a partial; and rf_settings as a keyed store so it is the last settings migration this app needs. MINE WAS STRICTER ON TWO THINGS MICHAEL CONFIRMED AND ORDERED PORTED: (1) the photo rule -- Cody's evidence_gap is a STRING ANNOTATION only, so a slope with 40 hits and zero photos still returns meets_threshold, and Cody takes photo_ids from the payload without ever checking they exist on that claim, where mine reads rf_claim_photos SERVER-SIDE and names unresolved ids (the same caller-cannot-substitute discipline Phase 3c already established for the measured scope); (2) discontinued/unmatchable material -- Cody SCORES it straight to meets_threshold ('a matching spot repair is not purchasable'), mine records and refuses to score it because state matching / line-of-sight rules were never researched and are cited nowhere in EITHER implementation. Michael's call: keep the trigger but give it its own outcome so it does not read as a met damage threshold. ONE THING I FLAGGED AND THEN DISPROVED before reporting: Cody accepting payload.assessment looked like a gate hole; it is a deliberate assess-then-save live preview, and the toast says so. STILL OPEN ON origin/main, both logged not fixed: the boundary comment now says the action and UI are 'in progress' when 4d4410f landed them, and the IP screen's section 5.1 still reads 'do not build a repair-vs-replace indicator' while the indicator is built and pushed -- the spec and the code disagree about whether the feature should exist. PORT WORK NOT YET DONE: strict photo rule, server-side id check, re-shaped discontinued trigger. 2026-08-26
