docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-27-sairnmechanical-platform-spec.md (NEW) + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md + SAIRN-BACKLOG.md -- SAIRNMECHANICAL OPERATIVE SPEC LANDED, AND IT IS ADDRESSED TO CODY'S GUARDIAN PASS. **CODY, THIS ONE IS FOR YOU** -- the spec exists specifically so your Guardian pass on the recovered sairnmechanical.html has something to check the old file's agreement/billing model against, and section 6 is written for exactly that. Read section 6 first, then 1.8 and 2.4; the rest is reference. THE THREE CHECKS THAT MATTER, IN ORDER: (1) Section 1.8 -- the agreement/billing model. BINDING requirement: one agreement spans multiple trades with per-trade line items, and trade lives on the LINE ITEM, never on the agreement header. This is the one that fails SILENTLY -- a header-level trade field looks correct for every single-trade customer and every test written against a single-trade fixture passes; it only surfaces on the first multi-trade renewal, by which point it is a data migration and not a schema change. If the recovered file puts trade on the contract header, or holds one agreement row per trade, that is the single most valuable thing your pass can surface. (2) Section 2.4 -- is there any ENTITLEMENT concept at all, or only visibility filtering? "No entitlement model present" is a legitimate finding, not a null result. The market contrast is the useful frame: business units gate VISIBILITY, and one-tenant-per-acquisition gates SEPARATION BETWEEN COMPANIES; neither is entitlement, and both are easy to mistake for it. (3) Section 3, Tier A vs Tier C -- is anything in Tier A implemented PER TRADE (three credential tables, three asset tables, three agreement models)? That is the shape the one-platform decision rejects. TREAT EVERY MISMATCH AS A FINDING TO REPORT, NOT A DEFECT TO FIX -- the old file predates all of these requirements, so mismatches are expected; the finding is WHERE and HOW EXPENSIVE. ONE THING TO CONFIRM BEFORE YOU TRUST ANY FINDING ABOUT IT: as of this commit sairnmechanical.html is on NEITHER this clone's working tree NOR origin/main -- `git cat-file -e origin/main:sairnmechanical.html` returns "path does not exist" -- while sairn-guardian-v2's own App File Map asserts the file and api/claude.js allowlists the sairnmechanical app_id. So whatever you recovered is local to your clone and invisible to every other clone until you push it. Please push it, or say explicitly that it is local-only, before anyone reasons about your findings -- that exact failure mode bit this session today and is now its own backlog row. WHAT THE SPEC IS AND IS NOT: operative, not research -- requirements and verdicts, deliberately carrying zero citations. Every piece of evidence lives in docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-27-sairnmechanical-shared-platform-competitive-research.md and the spec points into it by section, so the sourcing is not duplicated and cannot drift. If the two ever disagree, the research doc holds the evidence and the spec holds the decision -- reconcile explicitly rather than silently picking one. CONTENTS: the binding multi-trade agreement/billing requirement; the trade-entitlement requirement (unlock in place, no migration, no second login, no new tenant, and it MUST be in the data model from the first table because retrofitting entitlement rewrites the authorisation surface); the full A1-A7 / B1-B8 / Tier C pattern table with a shared-vs-trade-gated verdict each; all 21 gap rows with trade and region frequency; and a prioritised CAPABILITY list. THE CAPABILITY LIST IS NOT A BUILD ORDER -- trade build order stays deferred, and the list carries its own caveat that gap frequency measures where competitors are weak rather than what customers need first, which is why table-stakes items are ranked in explicitly despite scoring low. GUARDIAN SCOPE, STATED HONESTLY: markdown only, no HTML and no JS, so Check 0a script extraction, 0d dormant code, and checks 1-30 (proxy/bridge/app_id/is_demo, JS safety, design/color, nav-panel, localStorage, escHtml, console.log, dead-button, env-var drift) have no target and were NOT run. Run and applicable: secret scan (every hit read individually per Check 1's corrected rule -- all are `stonedesk-`/`StoneDesk-specific` word fragments, zero real secrets), markdown table column consistency (12 blocks across three files, 0 mismatched rows, backtick-wrapped pipes excluded), and a spec-vs-research parity check confirming the spec reproduces all 21 gap IDs and all 15 pattern IDs identically rather than paraphrasing them. 2026-08-27
- CORRECTION TO HANK'S rf_contingency_rules FINDING, plus a handoff claim that was not true (CC, 2026-08-29). Hank's row in SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-hank.md (line 111) states `rf_contingency_rules` holds 0 rows on RF-PINNACLE-2026 while the seed has 2, and commit 1478dd4's message carries the same claim. **THAT IS NO LONGER TRUE, verified live, not assumed.** `POST /api/reference-fingerprint` with `Bearer RF-PINNACLE-2026` returns both rows -- `RFCON-OH-HSSA-CANCEL` (hash e599b1c284ef9025) and `RFCON-OH-HSSA-SCOPE` (hash 02b6686358a74771) -- and `python tools/sairn_load_state_check.py --app sairnroofing` reports `seed expects 2 | licence holds 2, MISSING 0 STALE 0 EXTRA 0` on rf_contingency_rules and the same on rf_cert_rules (3/3). - COMPLETENESS CHECK ON THE RE-LOADED OHIO CONTINGENCY RULES -- clean, and verified two independent ways (CC, 2026-08-29). A hash match on two rows could in principle be a partial load that happens to agree on the fields checked, so this was not left at `STALE 0`. (1) `tools/sairn_load_state_check.py --app sairnroofing` reports MISSING 0 / STALE 0 / EXTRA 0 -- EXTRA 0 is the part that rules out a partial load with debris, MISSING 0 rules out a short load. (2) Recomputed both hashes from `sql/sairnroofing_contingency_seed_ohio.json` with a SEPARATE implementation written for this check rather than by importing the tool, so a bug in its hashing could not agree with itself: RFCON-OH-HSSA-CANCEL -> e599b1c284ef9025 and RFCON-OH-HSSA-SCOPE -> 02b6686358a74771, both matching live. The hash covers all 8 storable fields including the whole `data` jsonb -- CANCEL's 10 subkeys (authority, url, read_on, verbatim, verbatim_source, notice_required, form_required, indefinite_if_noncompliant, applies_only_when_solicited, notes) and SCOPE's 5 -- so the citation, the verbatim statutory quote, the eleven-holiday note and the `business_day_basis: oh_hssa` that Saturday-counts correctness depends on are all confirmed present and byte-correct, not merely the row count. **ZERO seed keys that no column can store**, which was the other way a load could look complete while dropping content. Also re-ran the sibling licences while the question was open: sairncare alf_compliance_rules 16/16 and alf_payer_rules 6/6, sairndental dnt_cred_rules 6/6, sairnroofing rf_cert_rules 3/3 -- every reference table with a seed in the repo now matches. Nothing left to reload anywhere.
- THE BEARER-KEY-vs-SESSION-TOKEN ASYMMETRY IS NOT ROOFING-SPECIFIC, AND ROOFING IS THE STRICT END OF IT (CC, 2026-08-29, first pass). Hank flagged that `rf_contingency_rules` writes need a management session token where a bearer licence key suffices to read. That is correct and it is the RIGHT shape -- but surveying every reference-content table on the platform shows roofing is not the outlier, it is the good example. **Three tiers exist, on the same endpoint, behind the same `Authorization: Bearer <licence>` header:** (A) SESSION + MANAGEMENT ROLE -- `rf_cert_rules` and `rf_contingency_rules` (`api/sd-data.js:2929`, `:4043`, roles `{owner, admin}` at `api/rf-auth.js:95`), `alf_compliance_rules` and `alf_payer_rules` (`:5047`, `:4832`, ALF_MANAGEMENT_ROLES). (B) SESSION, NO ROLE CHECK -- `dnt_cred_rules` (`:5923`) goes through `dntGate` (`:5497`), which verifies a session and returns it WITHOUT consulting `MANAGEMENT_ROLES`, which does exist in `api/dnt-auth.js:144` and is used elsewhere in that file. So any signed-in dental employee, provider or front desk included, can rewrite a state credentialing requirement. (C) **BEARER LICENCE KEY ALONE, NO SESSION AT ALL** -- `law_deadline_rules` and `law_holidays` via `api/legal-deadlines.js` `add_rule`/`add_holidays` (`:698`, `:717`; the endpoint DOES compute `caller` at `:542` but uses it only for `verified_by` attribution and the audit line -- it is never enforced, so a write with no session stores `verified_by: null` and succeeds), `grd_boq_rates` (`api/sd-data.js:1128`, no gate in the branch; the only sairngrounds session check nearby at `:840` is scoped to the `qc_status` approve/reject branch of `grd_progress_photos`), and `sc_anesthesia_base_units` via the generic SC_RESOURCES write path (session checks there are scoped to `sc_settings` write, `sc_auth_requests` sign-off, and delete -- a generic reference write passes through ungated). **LIVE-PROVEN SIDE BY SIDE, same header, opposite answers, writing nothing:** `POST /api/sd-data {action:write, resource:rf_contingency_rules, payload:{}}` with `Bearer RF-PINNACLE-2026` -> **401 NO_SESSION**; the identical shape with `resource:grd_boq_rates` and `Bearer GRD-DEMO-2026` -> **400 "BOQ rate payload.id is required"**, i.e. it cleared authorisation and died in payload validation. And `POST /api/legal-deadlines {action:add_rule, rule:{}}` with `Bearer LAW-PINNACLE-2026` -> **400 INVALID_RULE "Missing required field: rule_id"**, not 401 -- no session was sent. Probes used deliberately invalid payloads so the validator refuses before any row is written. **`sc_anesthesia_base_units` IS STATIC-READ ONLY, NOT PROBED, and the reason matters:** that branch has no id validation either (`entry_id: String(payload.id)` at `:6349`), so the safe-probe trick does not exist there -- an empty payload would CREATE a row keyed `"undefined"`. Verifying it live requires accepting a write, so it is reported from source and labelled as such. **WHY THIS IS WORTH KNOWING AND NOT JUST TIDYING:** these are the tables whose whole design premise is a CHECK-enforced citation -- `rfcon_authority_check`, the `NO_AUTHORITY` refusals -- so that a number on a contractor's or a lawyer's screen traces to a statute somebody read. Tier C means a legal deadline rule can be overwritten by anything holding the licence key, with the `verified_by` provenance column recording null, and the load-state gate would then report the licence as STALE against the repo without being able to say who changed it. NOT A PROPOSAL TO FIX ANY OF IT TONIGHT -- SAIRNlaw's tier-C path is exactly what `tools/load_deadline_seed.py` depends on to run from any clone, so closing it is a real tradeoff with the load-gate design and needs Michael's call, not a unilateral patch. Raised, scoped, and evidenced. 2026-08-29

- AUDIT TOOL: the D1 duplicate check could not tell a CLOBBER from a DECORATOR, and one of the two is not a bug (CC, 2026-08-30). `window.NAME = function(){...}` has two meanings and the check only saw one. sairnbuild.html:3593/7532 was reported as "later silently wins" when the truth is the opposite -- `var _origRBids = window.rBids;` captures the original and the wrapper CALLS it, so the earlier definition is the one that still runs. Reporting that as a silent overwrite is precisely backwards. **THE FIX REQUIRES BOTH HALVES, and either alone is not enough:** a capture of the existing value (`<alias> = window.NAME` or `= NAME`) AND a call through it in the new body (`<alias>(`, `.call`, `.apply`, `.bind`). A genuine clobber has neither. **A capture with no call-through is still reported** -- that is a saved reference nobody uses, which really does lose the original at runtime, and waiving it would have been the false negative worth fearing. PROVEN ON A FIVE-SHAPE PROBE before touching real files: direct-call decorator waived, `.apply` decorator waived, bare `window.foo = foo` alias waived (pre-existing behaviour, unchanged), **real clobber still D1**, **capture-without-call-through still D1**. FULL SWEEP, 18 app files, diffed against the pre-fix tool: exactly two deltas, both correct -- sairnbuild D1 1 -> 0, and stonedesk's D2 informational count 37 -> 33. **I did not accept the second one on the strength of the first.** 24 waived sites in stonedesk were enumerated and read: `_origFetch`, `_origXHR`, `_origSP` (showPanel, x5), `_origAddMsg` via `.apply` (x8), `_origSendMessage`, `orig.apply(this,arguments)` on renderSchedule, and `origOpen` on openDocModal -- every one a real call-through wrapper, six distinct shapes read directly rather than pattern-matched and assumed. Section A is unchanged at 0 across all 18 apps, which is the check that would have broken if a waive had removed a name's only definition. **D1 is now zero platform-wide, and that is a real zero rather than a quiet one.**
- LOGGED, NOT FIXED, LOW PRIORITY: the audit tool's Section E phrase list false-positives on real UI copy. `sairnbuild.html:4029` renders `'Unpriced work in progress'` -- a genuine label for unpriced change-order work -- and the list matches the substring `work in progress`. Left alone deliberately on Michael's call: the D1 fix is a structural distinction that can be proven, whereas separating placeholder prose from business prose is a judgment the phrase list cannot make without a per-phrase context rule, which is how a scanner starts accumulating an allowlist -- the exact anti-pattern the string-literal fix earlier tonight was written to avoid. E stays 6-true/1-false and is documented as needing a human read before action, so this costs a glance, not a defect. 2026-08-30
- STONEDESK'S SIX AI FEATURES: CLAIM CONFIRMED, not a second SAIRNgrounds (CC, 2026-08-30). Conversation-history archaeology surfaced a June 2026 grep-audit claiming StoneDesk had Vein Matching AI, Seam Placement AI, Sintered/Porcelain Mode, an Executive Suite, a Field Map route optimiser and a Stone Hub portal. That is the same SHAPE of claim as the "70-panel SAIRNgrounds build", which was reported complete across multiple sessions and which an exhaustive check of every branch, every commit and the whole filesystem found **never existed anywhere**. So it was verified to that standard rather than accepted. **ALL SIX ARE LIVE.** `panel-veinmatch`/`sb-veinmatch`, `panel-seamai`/`sb-seamai`, `panel-sintered`/`sb-sintered`, `panel-executive`/`sb-executive`, `panel-fieldmap`/`sb-fieldmap`, `panel-stonehub`/`sb-stonehub` — every panel present, every sidebar button present and carrying a real `onclick`, nav via `sbNav`/`showPanel`. **AND THEY ARE NOT STATIC SHELLS**, which is the check that distinguishes an id from a feature: 27 inline handlers across the six, `sdVeinAnalyze` / `sdSeamAnalyze` / `sdSintAI` / `sdExecAI` / `shAsk` / `fmOptimizeDay` and the rest — **every single one defined, zero missing**. Five of six call the Claude proxy; `fmOptimizeDay` is local computation, which is correct for a route optimiser rather than a gap. **KPI cells are zero-initialised (`0%`, `$0`, `0 mi`), not fabricated** — the pattern Guardian 0b actually asks for — and commit `5571e5a` *"remove the fabricated Material Savings figure on Vein Match"* shows these panels are actively maintained, not abandoned. No `git log -S` archaeology was needed because nothing is missing. **THE METHODOLOGY TRAP THIS RUN HIT, and it is worth more than the finding:** the first check ran against a local clone that was **BEHIND origin**, while `raw.githubusercontent.com` was **AHEAD** of it — the opposite of the staleness direction anyone expects, and it was only caught by diffing the git blob at HEAD against the raw fetch and finding 42 lines of newer content in the *remote*. A confident "verified against the current file" from a stale clone would have been exactly the failure this task existed to avoid. **Fetch, then compare blob-to-raw, then report.** Logged to memory as `stonedesk-six-ai-features-verified-live` and `claims-about-shipped-work-need-a-live-read`, the latter recording that the failure runs in BOTH directions: believing SAIRNgrounds cost a session rebuilding a phantom, and disbelieving StoneDesk would have cost a rebuild of six working features. 2026-08-30

A checker that over-reports trains people to ignore it.

The one "hardcoded KPI" hit was the string Pro, a plan label, not a number.
  **BUT THE AUG 24 COMMIT FIXED A BUG THE AUG 18 LIVE VERIFICATION DID NOT CATCH, AND THAT IS THE FINDING.** Commit 147b308 records that showPage('app') never called initApp(), and the load handler only called it when the app page was already active -- which never happens on a fresh load. So initApp() ran on exactly two paths: a Stripe return URL, and immediately after startTrial(). **reverifyTrial() existed and worked the whole time; nothing ever called it on the returning-user path.** A user with a genuine, unexpired, server-verifiable trial who closed the tab and came back hit the paywall with no way past. **The Aug 18 verification signed in, reloaded, and passed -- because the path it exercised was one of the two that DID call initApp().** The mechanism was verified; its reachability on the real user path was not. **A passing live verification proves the path you took works, not that the path a customer takes exists.** That belongs beside the "84/84 unit tests green while every California row was unstorable" lesson -- the same failure at a different layer.
  Also confirmed the previously-orphaned getElementById('subBtn') is resolved: one real element with that id now exists, and the second textual match is a comment explaining the historical TypeError. - THE SAIRNCASH GAP CLASS SWEPT ACROSS ALL 16 APPS -- one real instance found, and it is the same shape exactly (CC, 2026-08-30). The class: a mechanism is defined, correct, and commented as necessary, and nothing calls it on the path a returning customer takes. Built `tools/sairn_reachability_probe.py` to trace restoration/gate-shaped functions back to page-load entry points.
  **THE REAL FINDING -- `mechRestore` in sairnmechanical.html has ZERO CALLERS.** It reads the stored session from localStorage, validates the token's app claim and expiry, sets `mechSession`, and fires a non-blocking `whoami`. Its own preceding comment says why it matters: *"Session restore, then a NON-BLOCKING whoami. Without the round-trip a revoked account renders as logged in until the token expires."* **Nothing anywhere calls it** -- 1 definition, 1 total reference, 0 markup onclick, 0 window attachment. So two things are true today: a returning SAIRNmechanical user with a valid stored session is **not restored and must log in again every visit**, and **the revoked-account check that comment describes never runs at all**. SAIRNmechanical was recovered from an unmerged branch on 2026-08-28 with "real auth first", so this is most likely a wiring gap from that recovery rather than a regression -- but it is the SAIRNcash pattern precisely, and it was invisible to every check that asks whether a function works rather than whether anything reaches it.
  **A SECOND ZERO-CALLER FUNCTION, smaller and a different category.** `itaCheckLicense` in stonedesk.html is 111 bytes: a `showToast('License active')` plus an `itaAudit(...)` write. Zero callers. **This is NOT a miss by the dead-button audit's C2 check** -- I went and read that classifier rather than assuming. C2 requires *every* statement in the body to be a toast call or a `return`, and `itaAudit(...)` is neither, so the function falls outside C2's definition by design. It is dead code (Guardian 0d), not an orphan toast. Correctly excluded by a precise tool; found by a different question.
  **THE PROBE OVER-REPORTS, AND THAT IS RECORDED IN ITS OWN HEADER.** It traces only from `DOMContentLoaded`/`load`/`window.onload` -- and **for a licence-gated app that is the wrong entry point**, since the real one is the gate button. `checkTrialGate` flagged on six apps and is fine in all six, reached via `gateSubmit`, `sbApplyLoggedIn` and `grdApplyLoggedIn`; verified by hand before reporting. **The signal that needs no entry-point tracing is CALLERS=0**, which is why both real findings came from that column. The tool prints its own caveat instead of a verdict. **This is the fourth over-reporting checker written today** -- after the `e-?sign` regex matching "design", the heading regex matching "0.1", and `git ls-files` split on whitespace shredding filenames with spaces. Four in one day is a pattern worth naming: **a checker's first draft over-reports, and shipping it without hand-verifying its own output is how a check gets switched off.** 2026-08-30
- tools/sairn_dead_function_sweep.py (NEW) + docs/2026-08-31-dead-function-sweep.{md,txt} (CC, 2026-08-31) -- THE CROSS-APP REACHABILITY SWEEP, WIDENED FROM A NAME WHITELIST TO EVERY FUNCTION. `sairn_reachability_probe.py` ran on 2026-08-30 and reported "one real instance found across all 16 apps". That number is correct for its question and is not a statement about the platform: its source reads `targets = [n for n in F if RESTORE.match(n)]`, a fifteen-name whitelist, so every function whose NAME did not match was never examined. **The gap was demonstrated, not assumed** -- working sairnmechanical.html by hand today turned up EIGHT zero-caller functions in the one file that probe had already flagged, none of them matching RESTORE. **RESULT: 100 zero-reference functions across 4257, in 5 of 19 apps** -- stonedesk 88, sairnmechanical 5, sairnscape 5, sairnbuild 1, sairnvet 1, and fourteen other apps genuinely clean. 14 of the 100 hand-verified against the raw files by independent grep, zero false positives in that sample; the other 86 are NOT read and must be before anything is deleted.
  **THE FINDING THAT MATTERS -- TEN OF STONEDESK'S NUMBERED SECURITY LAYERS ARE DEFINED AND NEVER CALLED.** The file carries `// LAYER 1` through `// LAYER 30`. Fourteen are implemented as a named function; ten of those fourteen have exactly one occurrence in the file, the definition: **Layer 2 XSS Output Sanitizer (`safeHTML`), Layer 6 Brute-Force Protection on PIN (`saTrackLoginAttempt`), Layer 16 Rate Limiting, Layer 17 Response Sanitization, Layer 19 Request Signing, Layer 20 Prompt Injection on Every Claude Call, Layer 21 Anomaly Detection, Layer 24 Data Minimization, Layer 27 Right to Delete, Layer 30 Security Status Dashboard.** Alive: Layer 1 fetch interceptor, Layer 7 saCleanPrompt, Layer 8 saScrubPII, Layer 9 XHR patch -- so some prompt-injection defence does run even though Layer 20's wrapper does not. **SCOPE, so this is not over-read: the other 16 layers are meta tags, self-installing IIFEs or listeners, are not covered by a reference count, and are NEITHER CLEARED NOR CONDEMNED here.** This is the fabrication class pointed at security -- present, named, numbered and commented, so a reader concludes the control exists, and it provides no protection. A missing control and a present-but-unreachable one are identical in effect, except the second also reads as evidence the work was done. **NOTHING CHANGED. Wiring ten sanitizers into live paths is a decision about what this product claims, not a mechanical edit** -- Michael's call, sairn-decision-gate before any of it is described as implemented.

- tools/sairn_sql_preflight.py + sql/schema_snapshot_query.sql + tests/sql_preflight/ (ALL NEW) (CC, 2026-08-31) -- **TOOL 2 BUILT, PROVEN AGAINST PLANTED DEFECTS, PUSHED `8e2f9fd`. NOT wired into the push gate -- that is a decision for Michael and is the only open item.** A SQL file here is written in a clone, reviewed as text, pasted into the Supabase editor by hand, and nothing between those steps checks that what it names is real. A typo'd column in an INSERT fails loudly; **a typo'd column in the WHERE of an UPDATE or DELETE matches nothing and reports success -- "0 rows" is indistinguishable from "nothing needed changing"**. Same shape as every expensive failure in this repo.
  **TWO SCHEMA SOURCES AND WITHOUT THE LIVE ONE IT PASSES NOTHING.** `--live` takes a snapshot from the new read-only `sql/schema_snapshot_query.sql` (run in the editor, save the JSON). Without it the tool compares against every CREATE TABLE / ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN in `sql/`, prints a five-line NOT-A-PASS banner, and **exits 2 -- a distinct code, never 0**, matching the push gate's rule that "could not tell" must never read as silence. **No clone has generic information_schema access** and adding an endpoint that returns the whole schema shape to any licence-key holder is a worse trade than pasting a query, so this follows the existing `introspect_undeclared_tables` pattern. It also **refuses to guess**: bare column names in a SELECT list or multi-table WHERE are counted and reported as unattributed rather than assigned to a table.
  **PROVEN AGAINST DEFECTS, NOT JUST A CLEAN SWEEP.** Across all 174 files in `sql/` the only finding is `license_keys` x17 -- real, and a genuine DECLARED limitation: no tracked CREATE TABLE exists for it, which two seed files already say in their own comments. **A checker that has only returned clean is unproven**, so `tests/sql_preflight/` carries a defect probe and a control: 4 planted defects (bad column in an INSERT list, bad table, bad column in an UPDATE SET, bad column behind a table ALIAS) all caught, control clean, 7 exit codes asserted. Re-runnable: `python tests/sql_preflight/run_probe.py`.
  **SEVEN FALSE POSITIVES AND ONE MISS, ALL FOUND BY HAND-CHECKING THE TOOL'S OWN OUTPUT rather than by reading the tool.** The worst: a multi-column `ALTER TABLE` was parsed only to its FIRST `add column`, so the tool reported **four real dnt_appointments columns as missing in a live dental app's demo seed**. Also `revoke ... from anon` -> missing tables `anon`/`service_role`; `on all tables in schema public` -> a table called `schema`; `grant execute on function` -> `function`; `with declared(table_name) as` -> `declared`; `x is distinct from y` -> `tst`/`test_data`; unqualified `pg_class`/`pg_namespace`; four `create temp table X as select` scratch tables. The MISS: alias-qualified refs like `a.last_login_at` went unchecked because the alias looked like a CTE name. **All eight shapes are now in the control probe so a future change that reintroduces one fails loudly.** Sweep went 84 findings -> 17, and the 17 are all one explicable cause.
  **OPEN, AND HELD FOR MICHAEL:** whether this becomes a third check in `tools/sairn_push_gate_hook.py`. It would block any push touching a SQL file until a current snapshot exists (DECLARED mode exits 2), which is a real workflow cost and a real decision -- not one the tool should make for itself. 2026-08-31

That is the version with **zero snapshot-freshness friction** -- no live schema needed, so an ordinary push costs nothing and there is nothing to keep current. Live-snapshot blocking remains a separate later decision.
  **BLOCKS ON `MISSING_COLUMN` ONLY.** In declared mode `UNDECLARED_TABLE` fires on every real table with no tracked CREATE TABLE -- `license_keys` alone is 17 occurrences of correct code -- so blocking on it would stop every licence-seed push on day one. Those print as a note and are allowed. **The split is encoded in an EXIT CODE, not in prose**: the new `--gate` mode exits 1 only on a blocking finding, so the hook keys on the code the way check 2 does. Had the hook parsed report text, a later format change could have silently disarmed it -- the same silent-failure shape the gate exists to catch.

That accidental block is incidentally live proof the hook is registered and running.
  `run_probe.py` now covers the gate contract too: blocks a phantom column, silent on clean input, and **does not block any existing file in sql/** -- the regression that would matter most. Full suite: 4 defects caught, control clean, 7 exit codes, 3 gate cases.

In LIVE mode **`MISSING_TABLE` now blocks** alongside `MISSING_COLUMN` -- the labels are not synonyms, `UNDECLARED_TABLE` is a fact about the repo and `MISSING_TABLE` is a fact about the database. The hook denies on 4, on findings, **and on the checker failing to launch, which used to be `p = None` and a silent allow**. Snapshot path `db/schema_snapshot.json`, override `SAIRN_SCHEMA_SNAPSHOT`.

That copy was the **2026-08-29 two-check version** (11129 bytes, no check 3), and `sairn_sql_preflight.py` **was not there at all**. Last night's "verified by running the hook" ran the REPO copy directly, which was true of the file and false of the installed hook. Both files are now copied to `C:/SAIRN/tools/` and hash-match the repo. **This is the committed-but-never-loaded shape the whole gate family exists to prevent, occurring inside the gate family.** OPEN FOR MICHAEL: a copy will drift again -- worth replacing the installed file with a thin shim that resolves the clone's tools directory from CWD, but that is a design change and not tonight's ask.
  **COST OF FAIL-CLOSED, STATED PLAINLY: `db/schema_snapshot.json` DOES NOT EXIST YET**, so every push touching a `.sql` file under `sql/` -- in any clone, any session -- is now DENIED until someone runs `sql/schema_snapshot_query.sql` in the Supabase editor and saves the JSON. That is the intended behaviour and the deny message says exactly that. Pushes that touch no `.sql` file are unaffected (hank's `sql/*.json` deadline seeds included).
  **(2) INNERHTML AUDIT DONE, NOTHING WIRED**, as instructed -- `docs/2026-09-01-stonedesk-innerhtml-audit.md`. **470 innerHTML assignment sites: 175 pure literals, 144 already escaped, 151 interpolating something unescaped.** The sharp subset is **40 raw interpolations landing inside an HTML attribute** (attribute position needs only a double quote to break out; element-text position needs a left angle bracket). **The headline recommendation is not to wire safeHTML at all:** `escHtml` (L1766) already exists and is already the escaper at those 144 sites, and safeHTML's extra slash-escaping and `on\w+=` stripping are worse for this job, not better.
  **Two claims I nearly got wrong and checked instead:** `m.name`/`e.name` interpolated into an input's value attribute looked like stored XSS because they are passed to `dbSaveBizData()` -- **that function is an empty stub**, so it is localStorage-only and self-XSS. And `c.vendorWebsite` into an `href` comes from the in-file `VENDORS` constant, not user input (the author escaped the vendor NAME on the same line and not the href -- wrong shape, safe data). **Separately: three of the four markdown renderers do not escape their input** (`formatMessage` L10162, `window.renderMarkdown` L18696, `sdMarkdown` L34112; only the function-scoped `renderMarkdown` L24716 does), and Layer 17's `saSanitizeResponse` strips script tags and `javascript:` but **not event-handler attributes** -- so an `onerror=` payload in a model response survives Layer 17 and reaches the DOM. safeHTML must NOT be applied there; the fix goes inside the renderers.
  **(3) SESSION LOCK ROUTED THROUGH REAL PER-EMPLOYEE AUTH, HARDCODED PINS GONE**, pushed `569fdec` then `a6bc568`. `saUnlock` now calls `api/sd-auth.js` `login` with the session's `employee_id`, inheriting per-credential hashing, the 5-strike/15-minute `locked_until` lockout and the generic bad-id/bad-PIN error. No session or no licence key **refuses** rather than falling back. `CODES` (the `{sales, admin}` pair holding the two literal PINs) and its **already-dead** writer `saveCodes()` removed -- verified dead first: neither `sales-code-input` nor `admin-code-input` has an `id=` anywhere in the file.
  **THREE MORE DEFECTS IN THE SAME LAYER, ALL FIXED, BECAUSE WITHOUT THEM THE PIN WORK WAS DECORATIVE.** (a) **`saLockScreen()` HAD NEVER RENDERED.** Its guard was "if an element with this id exists, return", and line ~14855 ships a static display:none stub carrying that exact id -- so it returned on the first call and every call after. `sairn_reachability_check.py` had been printing this as R1 "stub placeholder squats an id the JS also creates" and it read as cosmetic. (b) **`saResetTimer()`, registered on click/keydown/MOUSEMOVE/scroll/touchstart, set `sessionLocked = false` and removed the overlay** -- moving the mouse dismissed the lock, as did the first keystroke typed into its own PIN box. (c) the PIN box was `maxlength=4` while real PINs are six to eight digits server-side, so wiring it to the server without that would have **locked out every employee in the shop**.
  **AND A FOURTH, THE WORST, FOUND ONLY BY DRIVING THE REAL LOCK SCREEN IN A BROWSER**, pushed separately as `a6bc568`. Line 15532 held a one-line `saUnlock` that looked the overlay up and set its display to none -- a later plain function declaration silently overwriting the security layer's `window.saUnlock =`. The Unlock button ran THAT: hides the overlay, checks nothing. **Not a weak PIN check -- no PIN check.** Anyone at an idle machine clicked Unlock and was in, whatever the PIN comparison said. **The comment directly above it in the same file documents this exact bug class** for `saAcceptPrivacy`/`saDeclinePrivacy`; a previous session fixed those two and left this third one on the very next line. It was invisible to reading and to `duplicate_global_check` (the real one lives inside an IIFE, so only one depth-0 name exists) -- what exposed it was the overlay vanishing while `data-sa-locked` was still set and the error line still empty, a shape the real handler cannot produce.
  **LIVE-VERIFIED ON THE DEPLOYED FILE, BOTH ORIGINS AND BOTH FOR A REASON.** The lock screen cannot be triggered on the live origin (`SAIRN_SEC` is IIFE-private and the timeout is 30 minutes), so the deployed HTML was fetched and served on 127.0.0.1:8731 with **only two timing constants patched** (the session timeout and the 60s interval; delta -12 bytes): lock screen **renders** -- it never had before -- `maxlength` 8, `data-sa-locked` set; five mousemoves, a click, a keydown and a scroll leave it **still locked**; the old hardcoded PIN with no session refuses with "Cannot verify -- sign in again" and the button becomes "Sign in again". Then **on the real `sairn.vercel.app` origin**, where the fetch is same-origin and hits the real API: `window.saUnlock` is the real one (its source contains `sd-auth`), the old hardcoded PIN returns **"Unknown license key" from the server**, stays locked, PIN cleared, button restored. Probe keys removed from the browser afterwards, verified 0 remaining; local server stopped.
  Checks after every edit: 128/128 script blocks parse, div balance 4770/4770, no duplicate globals, panel and nav checks pass, missing-DOM-target findings drop by exactly the two dead ids `saveCodes` owned and gain none. **`div_balance_check` failed at -1 again on my first draft, for the same reason as last night** -- a comment containing one literal div tag; rewritten without it. The Layer 2 note in this file warns about exactly that and I still did it.

- stonedesk.html + docs/2026-09-01-stonedesk-innerhtml-audit.md (CC, 2026-09-01) -- **ESCHTML SWEEP DONE, THE THREE RENDERERS FIXED, SD_PINS GONE. Pushed `7af8cb3` then `3dc048a`, both live-verified in a browser against the deployed file.** Michael's call was escHtml, not safeHTML, and the audit's own recommendation agreed: safeHTML additionally escapes `/` and strips `on\w+=`, which mangles element-text output for no gain. **Layer 2 stays unwired and should stay that way.**
  **40 ATTRIBUTE INTERPOLATIONS CLOSED, escaped bucket 144 -> 154 of 470 innerHTML sites.** The two the scanner still names are inside the parked renderer's own body (one of two in-function literal style strings, and a scheme-validated href). **LIVE-VERIFIED ON THE DEPLOYED PAGE against the real `renderMachines()`** with a hostile equipment name: 3 inputs rendered, injected `onmouseover` attribute is `null`, the attribute set is exactly `onchange,placeholder,value`, and **the input's value is the payload VERBATIM** -- which proves the fix in both directions, no double-escaping.
  **TWO SITES NEEDED SOMETHING OTHER THAN escHtml, and saying so is the point** -- a sweep that applies one helper everywhere and calls it done is how false comfort gets shipped. (1) `sairnEmptyState`'s `btnFn` lands in an `onclick`: **the HTML parser decodes entities BEFORE the JS parser runs**, so `&#39;` becomes a real quote and still breaks out. It now takes a bare identifier or emits no handler at all. (2) `renderMarkdown`'s `[text](url)` had no scheme check and escHtml leaves `:` alone, so `[click](javascript:...)` in a model response would still have produced a live href; non-http(s)/mailto/relative now render as text.
  **THREE OF FOUR MARKDOWN RENDERERS DID NOT ESCAPE.** `formatMessage` (the assistant chat bubble) and `sdMarkdown` (Stone Hub / pricing panels) were unescaped AND reachable -- those were the real exposure and both now escape first. Layer 17 was never cover: `saSanitizeResponse` strips script tags and `javascript:` and **nothing else**, so an `onerror=` in a Claude response reached the DOM. **The escape pass would have silently broken blockquotes** (`^> ` never matches once `>` is `&gt;`) -- caught before pushing and the regex now takes both forms.
  **AND THE THIRD SHADOWING STUB OF THE NIGHT.** `window.renderMarkdown` (~L18897) is overwritten by the top-level `function renderMarkdown(txt)` in a LATER script block (~L24938), so **all four call sites have always resolved to the simpler renderer**. The comment above it said "expose the renderer globally so streaming can use it." Streaming never used it. So my first audit was WRONG on one point and the doc now carries the correction: that renderer was unescaped and **unreachable**, not a live path. **Found by running the deployed page** -- `window.renderMarkdown('[ok](https://example.com)')` returned its input unchanged and `# H` produced a div, which is the other function's behaviour. **My own node probe missed it because it evaluated each renderer in isolation, and isolation is exactly what erases a name collision.** Renamed to `renderMarkdownRich`, call sites deliberately NOT repointed -- switching them would start rendering tables, fenced code and links in the AI panels, which is a visible product change and Michael's call. `sairn_reachability_check.py` now correctly reports it unused, which is the truth the collision had been hiding. Its escaping is kept so it cannot be adopted later in an unsafe state.
  **VERIFIED BY EXECUTING THE REAL FUNCTIONS, twice over:** extracted from the repo file and again from the file the CDN actually serves, 25 assertions -- no img/script node survives an injection fixture in any of the four, the payload shows as text, and bold/italic/code/headers/lists/blockquote/table/fenced-code/paragraph breaks all still render; `javascript:` link refused, relative link allowed, apostrophes round-trip. Then re-asserted in the browser on `sairn.vercel.app` against the live globals.
  **SD_PINS REMOVED** -- a four-entry default-PIN table in a publicly served file with ZERO readers, re-checked at HEAD before cutting. Its three dead siblings (`SD_ROLE_LABELS`, the depth-0 `sdCurrentRole`, `sdSelRole`, all also zero-reader) are left in place and named in a comment; removing them was not the ask.
  **db/schema_snapshot.json IS THE ONE ITEM THAT GENUINELY NEEDS MICHAEL'S HANDS, and I checked rather than assumed:** no Supabase key in this session's environment, no `psql`, no `supabase` CLI, no `.pgpass`, and **no api/ endpoint reads `information_schema`** -- which is deliberate, `sql/schema_snapshot_query.sql`'s own header explains that exposing schema shape to any licence-key holder was judged a worse trade than pasting a query. So: run that file in the Supabase SQL editor, save the single JSON cell as `db/schema_snapshot.json`. Until then every push touching a `.sql` file is denied platform-wide, which is the fail-closed behaviour working as intended.
  **STILL OPEN, QUANTIFIED AND NOT FIXED: 115 interpolations land inside an `on*=` event-handler attribute** -- a distinct class ~3x the size of the one closed tonight, and **escHtml does not fix it** for the entity-decoding reason above. Most carry internally-generated ids (`'JC'+Date.now()`), which is why it has not bitten. The free-text ones are worth looking at first: `u.name` (~L34759), and `vk` / `sku` in the vendor panels (~L22833, ~L23001), which come from an imported CSV. Two sites already hand-escape for JS, so the shape was understood in places and never systematised. Not started -- Michael's call whether it becomes the next sweep.
  Checks after every edit: 128/128 script blocks parse, div balance 4770/4770, no duplicate globals, panel and nav checks pass. No double-escaping introduced -- verified mechanically: no escHtml inside a renderer call, none on a renderer's output, no nested escHtml, no escHtml/subEsc pairing. **A verification harness was wrong twice tonight and the code was right both times** -- once when `window.machines = [...]` silently did nothing because `let machines` at top level is not a window property, and once when a regex "confirming" the escape matched my own comment text rather than the code. Same shape as the 5-vs-20 login-threshold probe and the one-row-CSV checker. 2026-09-01
- stonedesk.html + docs/2026-09-01-stonedesk-innerhtml-audit.md (CC, 2026-09-01) -- **ALL 115 EVENT-HANDLER SITES CLOSED AND THE MARKDOWN CALL SITES REPOINTED. Pushed `201a3db`, `b1a183e`, `f3d2b5b`, all live-verified in a browser against the deployed file.** Michael's instruction was to check each site's real parser context rather than apply escHtml blindly, and that was the whole job: **two of the three fixes needed are not escHtml, and one entire class turned out to be a LIVE BUG rather than a latent injection.**
  **NEW HELPERS, AND THE ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING.** `jsStr` (JS-string-literal escaping) and `escAttrJs = escHtml(jsStr(v))`, beside `escHtml`. **The browser decodes HTML entities in an attribute BEFORE the JS parser runs**, so escHtml's `&#39;` turns back into a real quote and closes the JS string exactly as an unescaped one would -- escHtml is not weak there, it solves the wrong layer. jsStr first, escHtml second; reversed, the source *looks* escaped and is not. Backslash before quote inside jsStr, or a trailing backslash swallows the escape. All of that is written at the definition, not left to be re-derived.
  **THE 115 DECOMPOSE AS: 76 quoted-string arguments wrapped in escAttrJs; 23 emitted as BARE JS TOKENS, now quoted AND escaped; 5 carrying the wrong escaper, hand-fixed; 28 left bare on purpose** (loop indices used as array subscripts -- quoting breaks `machines[i]` -- booleans, and the identifier `sairnEmptyState` already validates against a regex). The 76 were done by a per-line, context-checked pass that **refuses to write if it meets an escaper it does not recognise**; the tool aborted twice and both aborts were correct.
  **THE 23 BARE-TOKEN SITES WERE ALREADY BROKEN, AND THAT IS THE bigger half.** Every id in this file is `'C'+Date.now()` -- a string beginning with a letter -- so `onclick="custOpenDetail(' + c.id + ')"` emitted `custOpenDetail(C1788268235253)` and threw `ReferenceError`. **Proven in the browser on the deployed build BEFORE touching it**: rendered a probe customer, read the emitted attribute, evaluated it, got the ReferenceError. **Customer cards, vendor discount save, override remove, comms pin/reply/delete, safety training delete, nesting load and finance job delete were all dead controls.** Every callee compares `===` against a `.id` or uses it as an object key, so a quoted string is what they always wanted. **escAttrJs alone could NOT have fixed these** -- a bare token needs no quote character to break out of, which is exactly why "apply the helper everywhere" would have shipped a clean-looking no-op.
  **AND THE QUOTING BROKE ONE THING, WHICH IS THE ARGUMENT FOR CHECKING EACH SITE.** **Quote history is the only numeric-id data set in the file** (seed rows `id:1..8`, new rows `id:Date.now()`). `sdHistoryView('1')` met `q.id === id` as `1 === '1'`, found nothing, and the View button went dead -- **the exact shape the sweep was removing, reintroduced by the sweep**. Caught by reading the emitted attribute on the live page, not by the checks. Fixed by making the three quote-history lookups `String()===String()` rather than by dropping the quote: **the quote is what closes the injection.** Every other callee's id generator was then read rather than assumed -- comms threads `'MSG'/'ANN'+`, safety training, nesting `'NEST'+`, finance `'FJ'+`, customers `'C'+`, vendor keys, SKUs, product ids `'job-t-'` -- all string.
  **THE 5 WRONG-ESCAPER SITES, worth naming individually:** three used `escHtml` in a JS-string argument (wrong layer); one hand-rolled `.replace(/'/g,...)` that handled the quote and **not the backslash**; and one used `subEsc(...).replace(/\\x27/g,'')` which **DELETED apostrophes** -- a sub-portal job at "O'Brien Rd" was being rendered as "OBrien Rd" in the button that opens its photo capture.
  **LIVE-VERIFIED AFTER DEPLOY:** 35 such controls rendered on the page, **35 with a quoted first argument and 0 bare**; `custOpenDetail` clicked with a normal id opens the detail with no error; clicked with `z'); window.__H1=1; ('` it **does not execute**, resolves the payload as data, and raises no parse error; `sdHistoryView('1')` populates the detail again ("Marcus Webb -- $4,850 -- Approved"). `jsStr`/`escAttrJs` also unit-tested by extracting them from the deployed file and running them: 9 jsStr cases plus 5 attack strings, each asserted to survive HTML-entity decoding as ONE well-formed JS string literal that round-trips to the original, with no side effect executed.
  **MARKDOWN REPOINT DONE -- AND IT CHANGES NOTHING YET, which is the part that needed saying rather than claiming a win.** The four AI-chat-area call sites now name `renderMarkdownRich`. Verifying on the live page showed **all four are unreachable**: the three streaming sites live inside `installStreamingHook()`, which has **exactly one occurrence in the file, its own definition** -- nothing calls it, `window.sendMessage` is assigned four times over and the live one references no markdown renderer at all; and the `addMsg` interception is guarded on `typeof window.addMsg === 'function'`, which is false, so `installMarkdownHook()` bails and its polling retry never fires. Markdown in the AI chat still goes through `formatMessage()`, which now escapes. **A comment claiming "tables and links render in the chat area for the first time" was written and then corrected before pushing** -- it would have been false.
  **Correcting my own earlier count: there are FIVE renderMarkdown call sites, not four.** The fifth is the Executive Suite chat, **deliberately left** on the simple renderer: `.exec-ai-msg.ai` is `background:var(--surface-2);color:var(--text)` and the rich renderer hardcodes `#F0F0FF`. Same reason the hooks should not simply be switched on -- `.msg-bubble` is `background:#FFFFFF;color:#1A1410`, so wiring the rich renderer as written **paints near-white text on white**. Its colours need to move to theme variables first: a design change, not a security one.
  The rich renderer itself is verified safe and full-featured for that day: against a hostile fixture in the live runtime it renders h1, table, ul, blockquote, pre, code and a safe link, creates **no img or script node**, refuses a `javascript:` href, and shows the payload as text.
  Checks after every edit: 128/128 script blocks parse, div balance 4770/4770, no duplicate globals, panel and nav checks pass. **Two self-inflicted errors recorded rather than quietly repaired.** (1) Running the positional rewriter AFTER a hand fix on the same line double-wrapped one site (`escAttrJs(escAttrJs(sku))`) -- the quote pass has no already-escaped test, unlike the wrap pass; caught by grep, repaired, asserted to zero. (2) I declared one REFUSED site "element text, a regex artefact" on the strength of a 260-character truncation of a 496-character line; the handler was past the cut and it was a real site. **A truncated read is indistinguishable from a complete one**, which is the standing rule and I broke it anyway. Also worth keeping: `eval()` is blocked by this page's CSP, so handler verification had to move to real `.click()` -- which is the better test, and one live click on a Save button hung the renderer because it persists to a real tenant. **Do not click write-path buttons on the live origin;** read the emitted attribute instead.
  **STILL OPEN:** (1) ~25 innerHTML sites assign a single variable built elsewhere -- the risk is wherever it was assembled, each needs following by hand; (2) whether to switch on the streaming/addMsg hooks, which needs the rich renderer restyled first; (3) `db/schema_snapshot.json` still needs Michael -- unchanged from earlier tonight. 2026-09-01
- stonedesk.html + api/sd-sub-data.js + sql/sd_subs_compliance_2026-09-01.sql + tools/sairn_push_gate_hook.py + .githooks/ + .gitattributes (CC, 2026-09-01) -- **THREE TASKS DONE. And the middle one accidentally proved that the push gate built earlier the same night had TWO bypasses and then a third that the fix for the first one caused.** Pushed `bfcb365`, `c6dcb69`, `ca7c2e5`, `4f1c0f9`, `ca07b41`, `25738ea`.
  **(1) renderMarkdownRich NOW INHERITS COLOUR** instead of hardcoding a dark panel -- `bfcb365`. **The fix is `inherit`, NOT a swap to `var(--text)`**, and that distinction is the whole point: this file has exactly one `:root` palette and no dark-theme override, so `var(--text)` is a single dark value -- right on the white `.msg-bubble` and wrong on the dark AI panels that already exist. Only inheriting is right on both, and it stays right if a dark theme is ever added. Surface tints moved to neutral grey at low alpha (`rgba(127,127,127,a)` darkens on light and lightens on dark, which the original white-alpha values only ever achieved on dark); rules to `var(--border)`; de-emphasis to `opacity`. **LIVE-VERIFIED ON BOTH SURFACES by reading COMPUTED colour off the deployed page**, not by eye: rendered into a white container (`#1A1410` text) and a dark one (`#12162a` / near-white), every one of h1/strong/code/li/blockquote resolved to its own container's colour, blockquote at opacity .8 in both. A side-by-side screenshot was attempted three times and the extension's capture timed out each time; the computed-colour readout is the stronger evidence anyway and is what this rests on. All 25 renderer assertions still pass.
  **(2) THE STONEDESK COMPETITIVE GAP, CLOSED.** The findings doc is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-27-sairnmechanical-shared-platform-competitive-research.md` §5 -- **it contains exactly ONE StoneDesk row**, and the other two competitive docs mention StoneDesk once and zero times. So "highest-value gap" had one candidate, not a ranking: **the Subcontractor Portal had no compliance layer at all** -- no COI, insurance, licence, W-9 or expiry on `sd_subs`. **Re-verified against the live schema and api/sd-sub-data.js before building rather than trusted from the 08-27 doc** (the doc is five days old and this project's own rule is that a status claim is a claim until re-checked): still absent.
  **SAIRNbuild, in this same repo, is the reference implementation, and the lesson taken from it is that TRACKING WITHOUT A GATE IS A REPORT NOBODY READS** -- its award path is hard-blocked for a non-compliant sub. So the gate here is **server-side** in `api/sd-sub-data.js`'s jobs-write path, and the panel's badges are UX on top of it, not the enforcement. Expired COI or licence -> **409 SUB_NOT_COMPLIANT**, naming the sub and what lapsed. **New assignments only**: marking an existing job paid must not become impossible because a certificate lapsed after the work was done, or the office routes around the gate and it stops meaning anything.
  **THREE STATES, NEVER TWO.** valid / expiring / expired, **plus UNTRACKED, reported as untracked and never as a green tick**. A roster of green ticks meaning "nobody filled this in" would be worse than no feature. Expiring-within-30-days is surfaced and does NOT block -- a hard block on a certificate still valid today trains people to ignore it. **Untracked does not block either, and that is deliberate and arguable**: every existing roster row is untracked the moment the migration runs, so blocking on null would take every shop's assignment path down on deploy day. One line to change if Michael wants it, but it needs a roster-migration plan first.
  **IT DOES NOT LIE WHEN UNMIGRATED**, which mattered because the SQL could not ship in the same push. The roster read asks for the compliance columns, falls back to the base list on PostgREST's 400, and returns `compliance_provisioned:false`; the panel then shows a banner saying expiry is NOT being checked. The roster write retries without the compliance fields and returns a warning the client surfaces, so "saved" can never quietly mean "your COI expiry was dropped". A failed read is a **third** state (null) reported as "not checked", not as provisioned. `complianceStatus` unit-tested by extracting the real function from the real file: 17 cases including the today / day-30 / day-31 boundaries, year rollover, and a timestamptz-shaped value.
  **(3) THE SHADOWING GREP -- one real finding, and three false alarms I checked instead of reporting.** 11 names carry more than one top-level definition. `fetch`, `stRaw`, `showPanel`, `openDocModal`, `renderSchedule` are all **legitimate wrapper chains** -- each captures the previous and calls it. I nearly reported Layer 1 as dead because the live `window.fetch` mentions none of the security-layer identifiers; **a behavioural probe settled it**: `fetch('https://example.com/')` rejects with `SAIRN Security: blocked outbound to example.com`, so Layer 1 is alive and the outer wrapper chains correctly. `showPanel` looked like a 73-character stub winning over five wrappers until I found the sixth definition my regex missed -- a one-line IIFE at ~L33837 that does call `orig(id)`.
  **THE REAL ONE: `printSOP`, `printAllSOPs` and `vendorPlaceOrder` inline-print wrappers NEVER INSTALL.** All three are guarded by `typeof X === 'function' ? X : null` in **script block 37**, while the real functions are declared in blocks 43, 46 and 69. Function declarations hoist within their own block only, so the guard is false every time and all three interceptors bail. Verified live: none of the three is the wrapper. Effect is functional, not security -- those three print via a `window.open` popup instead of the inline modal. **Not fixed; task 3 was scoped as a quick grep.**
  **── AND THEN THE GATE. THREE BYPASSES IN ONE NIGHT, IN THE GATE I BUILT TONIGHT. ──**
  **(a) COMMIT AND PUSH IN ONE BASH CALL BYPASSED IT ENTIRELY.** It is a PreToolUse hook: it runs before a single character of the command executes. `git add sql/x.sql && git commit && git push` is therefore invisible -- at hook time the commit does not exist, the outgoing list is empty, and it allows. **`sql/sd_subs_compliance_2026-09-01.sql` reached origin/main through exactly this shape**, hours after I made the gate fail-closed and watched it deny correctly on a separate push. The gate was working; it was looking at a moment before the thing it guards existed. Fixed by refusing the narrow case -- combined commit+push while SQL is uncommitted -- rather than blocking every routine combined workflow, which is how a gate gets switched off.
  **(b) EVERY `sairn_claim.py` PUSH SKIPPED ALL THREE CHECKS**, because the hook keyed on Bash command TEXT matching `git push` and that tool pushes from a Python subprocess. A gate on how a push was spelled is not a gate on the push. Fixed by also running the checks as a git **pre-push** hook, which git invokes for every push from any caller.
  **(c) AND THE FIX FOR (a) SILENTLY DISABLED THE FIX FOR (b).** The combined-commit check referenced `cmd`, which is only assigned in pretooluse mode; in pre-push mode that raised `UnboundLocalError`, the **fail-open** handler swallowed it, and the pre-push hook allowed everything it had just been added to catch. **Two bypass fixes landed minutes apart and the second broke the first. Neither test would have caught it alone** -- I only found it by instrumenting the shell hook to write a trace file, proving git really was invoking it, and then reproducing with git's actual new-branch stdin.
  **Plus: the hook file was written CRLF, so the shebang named an interpreter ending in a carriage return and git skipped it without a word.** `.gitattributes` now pins `.githooks/*` to LF. And a push that only DELETES refs is now exempt -- `git push origin --delete` was being refused while HEAD sat on a branch with SQL outgoing, a pure false positive.
  **Final state verified three ways against the COMMITTED code**: new-branch content push denies (exit 1), deletion push allows (exit 0), and a **real `subprocess.run(['git','push',...])` is blocked** with the deny text on stderr. Installed copy at `C:/SAIRN/tools` hash-matches the repo. All probe branches deleted locally and from origin; two stray remote branches created during testing were removed and confirmed gone.
  **OPEN FOR MICHAEL, unchanged and now blocking real work:** `db/schema_snapshot.json`. Until it exists every `sql/*.sql` push is denied platform-wide -- including, now, any correction to the compliance migration. The app and API ship in a state that says out loud it is not provisioned, so nothing is silently broken, but the feature is inert until that migration runs. 2026-09-01
- tools/install_git_hooks.py + all four clones (CC, 2026-09-01) -- **RECONCILED THE PUSH-GATE FIX AGAINST CODY'S. Both intact, compatible, 11/11 on a two-entry-point matrix. AND THE COMBINED FIX HAD NEVER RUN IN ANY CLONE, INCLUDING CODY'S.**
  **FIRST, A CORRECTION TO MY OWN PREVIOUS ENTRY.** I wrote up bypass (b) -- "every `sairn_claim.py` push skipped all three checks, because the hook keyed on Bash command TEXT" -- as though I had found and fixed it. **I had not. Cody shipped it at 14:47 in `922265b`**, with `--pre-push` mode, `.githooks/pre-push` and `tools/install_git_hooks.py`. It arrived in my tree through a routine `git pull --rebase` and I mistook the resulting file change for a linter touch. My two commits (`4f1c0f9` 15:05, `25738ea` 15:26) sit on top of his. The entry stands as a description of the defect; it was wrong about who found it.
  **NEITHER FIX OVERWROTE THE OTHER, verified line by line, not by reading the merge result.** `4f1c0f9` is +46/-0 on the gate file -- pure addition, so nothing of Cody's could have been dropped. `25738ea` is +42/-8, and **all eight deleted lines are accounted for**: six are the body of Cody's `prepush_base()`, which I EXTENDED to return `(base, delete_only)` rather than removed; one is its call site, changed to unpack the tuple; one is my own line from `4f1c0f9`. `prepush_base` has exactly one caller, so the signature change is contained. Every marker from Cody's commit is still present in HEAD (`MODE = 'prepush'`, `def prepush_base`, four `--pre-push` references, the stderr deny path) and so is every marker from mine.
  **COMPATIBILITY MATRIX, 11/11, run against the COMMITTED code through BOTH entry points.** Cody's: existing-branch content push denies, new-branch content push denies, `SAIRN_SEED_GATE=off` still honoured. Mine: push-only with SQL outgoing denies, non-push ignored, combined commit+push with pending SQL denies and names the reason, delete-only exempt. **Cross-check that mattered most: the combined-commit guard must NOT leak into prepush mode** -- delete-only still allowed with pending SQL, and no `UnboundLocalError` leaks. One matrix case asserted the right answer for the wrong reason (a "combined commit+push, no pending SQL" case that denied because the test branch had outgoing SQL, i.e. check 3 fired, not the guard); re-asserted cleanly on main with nothing outgoing -> correctly allowed.
  **THE REAL FINDING: CODY'S HOOK HAD NEVER EXECUTED, IN ANY OF THE FOUR CLONES.** `.githooks/pre-push` is stored LF in the object database, this repo runs `core.autocrlf=true`, and until my `.gitattributes` rule there was no attribute governing it -- so git wrote **CRLF into every working tree on checkout**, the shebang named an interpreter whose name ends in a carriage return, and **git skipped the hook silently on every push**. Measured, not assumed: hank CRLF, cody CRLF, fourth CRLF, cc CRLF. **hank and cody had both already run the installer and had `core.hooksPath` set, so both were reporting themselves protected while the hook had never once run.** SAIRN-fourth did not even have `hooksPath` set.
  **So the two fixes were not redundant -- they were each half of one working gate.** Cody's supplied the entry point that no caller can spell its way around; mine supplied the `.gitattributes` rule without which that entry point was inert everywhere. Neither alone protected anything against a `sairn_claim.py` push.
  **WHY CODY'S OWN VERIFY DID NOT CATCH IT, which is the transferable part.** `install_git_hooks.py` ended with "Gate script runs. Installed." -- and that was TRUE. It ran the PYTHON directly and it worked. What it never checked was whether **git can execute the shell wrapper**, which is where the failure lived. A verify that proves the inner component works while the outer one is dead is the same shape as everything else found tonight.
  **INSTALLER NOW REPAIRS AND RE-VERIFIES.** It byte-checks `.githooks/pre-push` for CRLF and rewrites it, and separately runs the wrapper. **Honest scope, measured rather than claimed: the wrapper run does NOT catch the CRLF case** -- `sh <file>` reads the file as a script and never consults the shebang, so a CRLF copy and an LF copy both exit 0. I tested both before writing the comment. The byte check is the authoritative one; the wrapper run is a second, narrower net for a broken interpreter or shell syntax, and is labelled as such rather than left to look stronger than it is.
  **ALL FOUR CLONES NOW: `hooksPath=.githooks`, endings LF.** Repaired hank/cody/fourth in place and ran the installer in fourth, which had no `hooksPath` at all. **PROVEN, NOT INFERRED, IN THE CLONE THAT WAS COMPLETELY UNPROTECTED:** instrumented SAIRN-fourth's wrapper to write a trace file, ran `git push --dry-run`, got the trace, restored the file byte-for-byte and confirmed `--dry-run` created no remote branch.
  **ONE COSMETIC SIDE EFFECT, stated so nobody chases it:** hank, cody and fourth will show ` M .githooks/pre-push` until they pull `.gitattributes`. **`git diff --numstat` on it is EMPTY** -- zero lines changed -- so it cannot be committed as a change and clears itself on the next pull. Checked rather than assumed.
  **STILL THE ONE THING NEEDING MICHAEL'S HANDS: `db/schema_snapshot.json`.** Every `sql/*.sql` push is denied platform-wide until it exists -- and as of tonight that is denied through BOTH entry points in all four clones, which is the intended state and also means there is no longer a path around it. Run `sql/schema_snapshot_query.sql` in the Supabase SQL editor, save the single JSON result cell to `db/schema_snapshot.json`. Until then the StoneDesk subcontractor compliance migration cannot ship or be corrected. 2026-09-01
- db/schema_snapshot.json (NEW) + tools/sairn_sql_preflight.py (CC, 2026-09-02) -- **THE SNAPSHOT IS IN. The push gate now checks SQL against what the database actually has, and it is verified working in both directions.** Pushed `c121dd5` / `6d92cb9`. Michael ran `sql/schema_snapshot_query.sql` and supplied the JSON: **256 tables, 2145 columns**, `_generated_at` 2026-09-02 08:50:14+00.
  **FIRST LIVE RUN FOUND TWO FALSE POSITIVES AND THEY WERE FIXED, NOT DOCUMENTED AWAY.** `platform_demo_licence_provenance_audit_2026-08-28.sql` and `platform_reference_rules_divergence_2026-08-28.sql` each bind `k` twice -- `from public.license_keys k` (table alias) and `join lateral unnest(con.conkey) as k(attnum)` (column-alias list). **The alias map is per FILE, not per statement**, so `k.attnum` was attributed to license_keys and reported as `MISSING_COLUMN license_keys.attnum`. Both wrong, and **both would have blocked a push touching those files on the gate's first day live** -- precisely the cry-wolf failure the tool's own docstring warns gets a checker switched off. An identifier also bound as `as name(cols)` is now excluded from the alias map. Per-statement scoping is the general answer, carries its own regression risk, and was deliberately not attempted.
  **VERIFIED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, because removing a false positive can buy a false negative.** The probe suite's **D4 case -- a genuine alias-qualified missing column -- is still caught.** Full suite green: 4 defects caught, control clean, 7 exit codes, 3 gate cases, 6 `--require-live` cases. Whole `sql/` tree against the real snapshot: **0 findings**, down from 2.

  **This is not a new judgement, it is StoneDesk's, applied.** `stonedesk_recovery_admin_seed.sql`: *"A published PIN cannot safely persist on SD-PINNACLE-2026 ... THE FIX IS THE LICENCE, NOT THE PIN."* Roofing never got that treatment.

  **(3) THE NEXUS PER-USER STYLE PROFILE -- BUILT, and the gap was exactly as described.** `buildSDSystemPrompt()` concatenates a base prompt, the SHOP's profile, the SHOP's memories, and **one style word set by hand on the shop record**. So two people at the same shop got byte-identical prompts. `sd_employee_profiles` is a manager's declared intent (its own header says so) and `sd_shared_knowledge` is company-wide aggregate; **neither observes an individual**. There was no structured persistent profile object at all.
  **SCOPED BEFORE BUILDING** -- `docs/2026-09-02-nexus-style-profile-design.md`, three decisions: ONE shared table for all apps (not eleven divergent copies of an analyser); observed signals only, all derivable without a model call; applied as directives, not statistics, behind a confidence floor.
  **THE HONEST LIMIT IS IN THE SCHEMA, not just the doc.** Keyed `(license_hash, employee_id)` because that is all a session token carries; `app_id` is recorded but deliberately NOT in the key so cross-app rollup is a later `union` rather than a migration. **But license_hash is per-app-LICENCE, so the same human in two apps has two profiles today.** "One shared table" is not "one profile per person across the platform" and the file says so rather than letting a reader assume it. The only cross-app anchor is `employees.customer_email`, which is not in the token.
  **WHAT IT ACTUALLY CAPTURES:** message length and its distribution, sentence complexity, question/imperative ratios, bullet/numbered/markdown habits, CAPS emphasis, hedging, courtesy, trade-abbreviation density, and the top content words the user actually uses. All counts, so a profile merges incrementally without re-reading history.
  **NO RAW TEXT IS STORED, AND IT IS ASSERTED RATHER THAN CLAIMED.** The client analyses locally and posts the COUNTS; the message never reaches the endpoint. A test feeds a sentence of deliberately rare words and checks no phrase and **no word ORDER** survives -- while asserting that individual terms DO, because that is the vocabulary signal. Stating the distinction rather than overclaiming the property.
  **A CONFIDENCE FLOOR, because this is the failure mode that matters.** Under five observed messages the block is omitted **entirely** and the prompt is byte-identical to before. Two terse messages must not convince a model the user wants telegrams forever. **And declared intent outranks observation**: the shop's hand-set style is passed in as an override and the prompt says the manager's value wins, rather than silently picking one.
  **THE TESTS FOUND TWO REAL DEFECTS IN MY OWN CODE.** (a) The term tally evicted at 60 on every merge, so **which vocabulary survived depended on the ORDER messages arrived** -- two identical users got different profiles. Cap raised to 400, where a real corpus never evicts, with the residual trade written down rather than hidden. (b) The abbreviation threshold was 1.5 per message, so the terse fabricator corpus -- **the exact user this feature is for** -- scored 0.33 and was told nothing.
  **28 assertions in style-profile.test.js**, the important one being that two genuinely different writers produce **different** directives with word targets more than 2x apart, because a personalization feature that emits the same block for everybody is the token implementation this was built to avoid. **Plus 19 in tests/style_profile_parity.js**, which extracts the client copy out of the real HTML and asserts it agrees with the server module message by message -- the duplication is deliberate and now cannot diverge silently.
  **AND DRIVING THE LIVE ENDPOINT FOUND A THIRD DEFECT, IN THE CODE WRITTEN TO PREVENT EXACTLY IT.** A `write` against the not-yet-created table returned **`{ok:true, provisioned:false}` at 200** -- a silent success for a request that stored nothing -- because the not-provisioned branch answered identically for read and write. **Reading the code would not have caught it; calling it did.** A read genuinely has nothing to report and still returns 200; a write now returns **503 NOT_PROVISIONED** naming the schema file. Validation also moved ahead of the database call so a malformed observation is rejected regardless of provisioning.
  **LIVE-VERIFIED ON THE DEPLOYED API** (SD-AUDIT-2026, real employee session): write against a missing table -> **503 NOT_PROVISIONED**; a 12-sample observation -> **400 ONE_AT_A_TIME**; missing observation -> **400**; read -> **200 provisioned:false**; no session -> **403 FORBIDDEN**. 128/128 script blocks parse, div balance 4770/4770, no duplicate globals, preflight clean.
  **STILL NEEDING THE SUPABASE EDITOR, three files, in this order:** `sairnroofing_audit_license_seed.sql`, `sairnroofing_verify_admin_removal_2026-09-02.sql`, `sairn_style_profiles_schema.sql`. Until the third runs the style feature is inert **and says so** -- provisioned:false, 503 on write, and a prompt byte-identical to before. Nothing is silently half-working. 2026-09-02

  **(1) [0072] WAS NOT TAKEN ON TRUST -- the previous session died before logging it, so every claim was re-checked.** `node --check` clean on all three JS files; **24/24 assertions pass** in `job-risk.test.js`; both commits confirmed present on `origin/main` by `git log origin/main`, not by the transcript. **And live-verified on the deployed API**: `supplier_lead_times` is in the deployed resource whitelist (a bogus resource name returns the whitelist and it is there) and the branch gates correctly to `403 FORBIDDEN` without an employee session. Nothing was assumed from "Pushed to main" appearing in a summary.
  **THE ONE THING THAT IS NOT DONE: `sql/sd_supplier_lead_times_schema.sql` HAS NEVER BEEN RUN.** SQL preflight is clean but preflight explicitly says so itself -- **no `--live` snapshot was given, so it compared against the repo's own CREATE TABLE statements, which is exactly the state where "a schema file written but never run looks PRESENT."** That is not a pass and is not being reported as one. No credential rows, so no recoverability guard applies. **It now joins THREE other SQL files already waiting on the Supabase editor** (`sairnroofing_audit_license_seed.sql`, `sairnroofing_verify_admin_removal_2026-09-02.sql`, `sairn_style_profiles_schema.sql`). Until it runs the risk engine reads an empty table and **correctly returns `unknown` for every job** rather than inventing a number -- inert and honest, not silently half-working.
  **(2) THE COMPETITIVE AUDIT -- 22 products, seven categories, three continents, every claim read from the VENDOR'S OWN SITE.** Aggregator "best software 2026" pages were used only to find vendors and then discarded: **SlabWise, SlabOS and Stone Project all publish comparison content about their own rivals**, so their claims about competitors are marketing. Four fetches failed and are recorded as failures rather than filled in (`marmoia.com.br` 403, `stoneproject.app` 403, `easystoneshop.com` TLS, and `actionflow.com` returns a bare LiteSpeed directory listing -- the real site is `actionflow.net`).
  **THE MARKET IS NOT WHERE PRIOR ASSUMPTIONS PUT IT.** The strongest single product found is **Italian, not American**: `iBlocky` out of the Carrara district, EUR 299-399/mo flat, with an AI Render Studio that turns a real slab photo into a photorealistic kitchen *including digital bookmatch*, and a **branded public catalogue the customer opens with no login**. Brazil has a whole tier of its own (`Sistrom`, `Marmu`, `Gransoft`, `Marmo IA + ERPedra` -- the last with native AI). Europe has `DDL`, whose Slab Digitization does **AI contour recognition from a smartphone**.
  **EIGHT GAPS, EVERY ONE CONFIRMED BY GREPPING THE FILE, NOT INFERRED.** The largest is structural: **there is no customer-facing portal at all.** `panel-client` is a print/PDF estimate view rendered *inside the shop's own app* (`stonedesk.html:5422`, "Back to quote", `printEstimate()`), and there is no StoneDesk portal endpoint in `api/` (`sen-portal.js` is SAIRNsenior's). **iBlocky, DDL, ActionFlow, SlabWise and Slabsmith all have one** -- five strong products in three regions converging independently.
  **SECOND: NESTING PRODUCES NO MACHINE OUTPUT.** `panel-nesting` has fourteen functions and **not one export** -- `nestExport|exportNest|downloadDxf|toDxf|sawTicket|cutTicket|pickTicket` all return zero, and `g-code`/`gcode` appear **zero times in the entire file**. DXF is **inbound only** (template upload from LT-2D3D/Proliner/Flexijet/Leica). SlabWise's whole pitch is "every templater in, **every machine out**"; StoneDesk is half that sentence. **And the AI system prompt claims "DXF export" as expertise** -- defensible as domain knowledge, but a shop owner will read it as a product claim.
  **THIRD: `barcode` APPEARS ZERO TIMES.** The only trace is `externalBarcode:''` at `:32793` -- an empty placeholder wired to nothing. ActionFlow, Stone Profits, Slabsmith, DDL (with a handheld scanner app) and Sistrom all have it, because **it is the mechanism the industry uses to stop the double-sale** -- the error iBlocky puts SECOND on its own homepage. Also: no slab-scanner integration (`slabsmith` = zero occurrences), which matters because **Slabsmith's Perfect Match matches COLOUR as well as vein, across remnants, from calibrated scanner imagery** -- higher-fidelity input than Vein Match's photos.
  **FOURTH, AND THE SUBTLE ONE: e-signature EXISTS but points the wrong way.** `ag.signedAt`/`ag.signerName`/executed-agreement banner are real (`:31306`, `:31566-31602`) -- but only on **SAIRN's own service agreements in `panel-agreements`, i.e. SAIRN selling StoneDesk to a shop.** The shop cannot get its own customer to sign a quote. A naive grep for "esign" returns 105 hits and **every one of them is the word "Design"**; the feature had to be checked by function name, not keyword.
  **PRICING, NOW VERIFIED RATHER THAN ASSUMED:** a 5-user Moraware shop (CounterGo $100/user + Systemize $120/user 3-min + Inventory $50/user) pays roughly **$1,350/mo**. Stonify **$500/mo base**. iBlocky **EUR 299-399 flat**. SlabWise **$99/$299/$799**. StoneDesk's own tiers are **$199/$299/$599** -- **at or below the market floor while carrying more surface area than anything above it.** Raised as a decision, not a recommendation.
  **HONEST POSITIONING FINDING: StoneDesk is not competing where it thinks it is.** Moraware/ActionFlow/Stonify do ONE workflow deeply (template -> draw -> nest -> schedule -> machine). StoneDesk does that workflow **more shallowly** and the surrounding business (compliance, workforce, reputation, risk, six real AI tools) **far more deeply**. Pitching it on Moraware's axis loses on gaps 2-4 in the first ten minutes of a demo.
  **(3) TWO FINDINGS FOUND WHILE LOOKING, NEITHER FIXED, BOTH ORTHOGONAL TO A RESEARCH GATE.** (a) **`panel-executive` is a plain sidebar button with a SELF-SELECTED role picker -- not gated on employee role** -- and the CFO advisor's context string carries **SAIRN Technologies' own chart of accounts and StoneDesk's own price book ("Starter $199/mo... It now carries an explicit status block in §3 and a matching line in §7 so it reads as **a competitive disadvantage being carried knowingly**, never as an oversight or a finding that went missing. §7 item 1 also records §4.1 as closed with its residual named.
  **(3) [0039] -- THE PREMISE FAILED, AND THAT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT PART OF IT.** It was held a full day for real production traffic to supply real assembled prompt sizes. **There are none, and there never were.** `api/claude.js` destructures `system` and `messages`, **receives Anthropic's own `usage` block and forwards it to the client without ever reading it**, and `sairn_ai_rate_limit_log` stores only `(app_id, requested_at)`. Vercel confirms **97 `/api/claude` calls in 36h across all nineteen apps with no size recorded on any of them.** Waiting another day would have produced exactly the same nothing.
  **MEASURED FROM THE FILE INSTEAD**, because the structure can be measured even when the traffic cannot. Fixed per request: **base system prompt 2,086 chars + guardrail 383 + style note <=49**, plus **466 bytes of tool-definition JSON (exactly one tool is registered)**. Bounded-but-variable: style directives (~1k ceiling), profile block. **UNBOUNDED: the memory block, and `history` -- the dominant term and the actual bug.** `var history=[]` is appended at **four** sites and cleared at **exactly one**, the user pressing Clear, and it was posted **whole** on every turn at both fetches.
  **THE CORRECTNESS IS IN THE DROP POLICY, AND IT IS NOT "DROP THE OLDEST N".** Anthropic rejects a `tool_result` whose `tool_use` is missing, so messages are **grouped first and dropped in whole units**. The newest group is **never** dropped -- it carries the message being answered. When even the irreducible request is over budget that is returned as **`over:true` rather than sent anyway**. `history` is **not mutated**, so the on-screen conversation stays whole and only the request is bounded, and **the user is told when context was left out** -- silently dropping it changes the answer with nobody knowing why.
  **THE TOOL RESULT IS THE ONE THING THAT CAN EXCEED THE WHOLE BUDGET ALONE** (`get_job_profitability` returns every financial record there is) **and the group drop cannot help, because that group is the last one.** So it is capped at half the budget and **the model is TOLD it was capped**, in the same terms this file uses everywhere else: do not total, count or average across the full set from a partial output. An ERROR result is deliberately never truncated.
  **`SD_PROMPT_BUDGET_TOKENS = 20000` IS PROVISIONAL AND SAYS SO IN THE CODE** -- ~10% of the window, above any ordinary conversation, biting on runaway tool-result accumulation. Token counts are chars/4 **estimates, labelled as estimates**; there is nothing to calibrate against yet.

It now sweeps 47 budgets instead of trying one. It was the weakest assertion in the file while reading as the strongest.

Recording it means touching `api/claude.js`, **shared by all nineteen apps** -- console-log to Vercel (cheap, short retention), a new column on the rate-limit table (needs the Supabase editor, already a bottleneck), or a new table.
