  **(1) THE EXECUTIVE SUITE'S INTERNAL DATA IS OUT OF THE HTML ENTIRELY.** The earlier `showPanel()` gate closed the UI path and **said at the time that it did not close View Source.** It now actually is closed: the CEO/CFO/CTO prompts live in `api/_lib/exec-context.js` and reach a browser only after `api/sd-data.js` verifies a real employee session on the licence **and** that its role is owner/admin -- the same check, moved to where it cannot be edited out of the page.
  **RELOCATION VERIFIED BYTE-IDENTICAL FOR ALL THREE ROLES BEFORE THE LITERALS WERE DELETED** (1604/1661/2004 chars, compared by evaluating the old object out of the file against the new module). **NO LOCAL FALLBACK:** a refusal makes the assistant say so and send nothing -- a fallback literal would restore the exact exposure.   **The `cto` string was carried across VERBATIM including its known defect** (it describes Fabricor's Railway/React/Drizzle stack and claims a QuickBooks UI that does not exist -- audit finding 4.2, still open). Correcting text inside a commit that moves text would make the diff unreviewable as a relocation. It is **named in the new file's header** rather than carried quietly.
  **LIVE-VERIFIED:** `Chart of Accounts: Assets 1000s`, `Stripe price IDs on file`, `May 21 2027`, `Drizzle ORM` and `EAI_SYSTEMS` all return **zero occurrences** on the served page; the endpoint is in the whitelist and returns `403 FORBIDDEN` without a session.
  **(2) AI CALLS NOW RECORD THEIR SIZE -- and the reason [0039] had no data is now fixed rather than just reported.** `api/claude.js` received Anthropic's `usage` block on every successful call and **forwarded it to the client without ever reading it**; the log table stored only `(app_id, requested_at)`. `sql/sairn_ai_usage_columns_2026-09-02.sql` adds two nullable columns to **the table that already gets a row per call** -- no parallel logging system. **NULL means "not recorded", which is a real state; a zero would be a measurement.**
  **NO `grant update` APPEARS IN THAT FILE.** service_role deliberately holds only SELECT and INSERT there, and `sairn_ai_rate_limit_consume_fn.sql`'s own header already argued that one EXECUTE grant is narrower than widening a table grant. The recorder is the **one SECURITY DEFINER thing** in that schema and is fenced to match: one row by id, two columns only, **refuses a row whose tokens are already set** (fill in once, never rewrite) and **refuses anything older than an hour**.
  **`consume` now returns the row it inserted**, so a measurement attaches to **THIS** call rather than to whichever row is newest -- guessing under concurrency would file one app's tokens against another app's request. The write happens **after `res.json()`, unawaited**: a statistic must never delay or fail a reply, and it stays inert (404, quiet) until the migration runs. **HONEST SCOPE, stated in the SQL, the JS and the tests: only `is_demo` calls write a log row, so this covers most platform traffic and not all of it.**
  **(3) NEXT GAP FROM THE AUDIT -- AND IT WAS NOT THE ONE THE AUDIT RANKED CHEAPEST.** Investigating GAP 3 (no barcode) turned up **the thing the barcode exists to prevent, unprevented.** Three places did `rs.status='reserved'; rs.reservedFor=customer;` **with no check of what was already there** -- quote builder, mobile POS, remakes -- and `write` is a blind upsert. **The second salesperson to save silently took a slab already promised to someone else and DESTROYED `reservedFor`, the only record of who had it.** Both quotes then showed the same physical slab as theirs.
  **By this project's own prioritisation principle that outranks every other item on the gap list** -- silent corruption of live data, not a missing feature -- **and unlike GAP 1 and GAP 2 it needed no decision from anyone.**
  **WORSE ON THE POS PATH: the reservation ran AFTER `sd_invoices.unshift(inv)` and `invSaveStore()`.** Money was taken, with a deposit, against a slab that might be another customer's. **Reserving now happens BEFORE the invoice exists** -- a refusal before the sale is a salesperson picking another slab, a refusal after it is a refund conversation.
  **A CLIENT CHECK COULD NOT HAVE FIXED IT:** `sdSlabs` is a localStorage array loaded once per session, so the templater's tablet is invisible to the salesperson's laptop. New `reserve` action: read, then write with a WHERE that re-asserts the row is unchanged. **Offline is a REFUSAL, not a local reservation** -- an unreachable server cannot tell you a slab is free. **The picker chip said "Reserved" at PICK time, when nothing was reserved** -- during exactly the window someone else could still take it. It now says "Selected".
  **(4) AND THEN THE FIX SHIPPED BROKEN, WHICH IS THE MOST USEFUL THING IN THIS ENTRY.** `6993e6a` passed **twelve assertions** and **refused every legitimate reservation.** Driven against the deployed API right after the push: seed an in-stock slab, reserve it, no concurrency at all -> **`RESERVATION_RACE`**. The predicate `data->>status=eq."in-stock"` never matched a row, so the PATCH always touched zero rows. **The double-sale refusal worked and the normal path did not, which is a worse shape than the bug it replaced.**
  **THE TESTS COULD NOT HAVE CAUGHT IT, AND THAT WAS THE REAL DEFECT.** The stub returned a row from PATCH **regardless of the URL** -- it proved a conditional write was ISSUED, never that the condition was one PostgREST agrees with. **A stub that ignores predicates cannot test a predicate.** It now evaluates the filter against the stored row the way the database would, and `mutateBefore` changes that row between read and write to simulate a real lost race instead of hard-coding an empty response. **Proof it now bites: restoring the original guard fails three assertions. Before the change it failed none.**
  **The guard is now keyed on `updated_at`** -- a plain timestamptz column, so no JSON path, no identifier case-folding, no filter-value quoting to get wrong -- and it is **strictly stronger**, because every writer stamps it, so it catches changes to fields the old predicate never looked at. The `pgq()` escaping helper was **deleted rather than fixed**: the predicate no longer embeds a customer name at all.
  **LIVE-DRIVEN END TO END after the correction:** reserve an in-stock slab -> **200**; a second salesperson -> **409 `ALREADY_RESERVED`, naming "Ruiz kitchen"**; the same customer again -> **200, not a false race.**
  **(5) TWO THINGS FOUND ON THE WAY, NEITHER ABSORBED SILENTLY.** (a) The resource registry **REFUSED** my first attempt to declare `reserve` in `stonedesk.js`, because `shared.js` owns `slabs` -- that guard working is why the verb is declared in the right place. (b) **`api/_resources/extra-actions.test.js` HAS BEEN RED SINCE 2026-08-27**: SAIRNsenior's `readiness` verb (`f1d2b57`) shipped without being added to the canonical list. **A tripwire that is already failing catches nothing** -- the next person reads the red and assumes it is the known one, which is very nearly what happened here. Declared late, after reading its handler to confirm it is session-gated and genuinely persists nothing.
  **ALSO PRE-EXISTING AND NOT MINE, verified under `git stash`:** `api/sd-data-complaints-readonly.test.js` and `api/sd-data-appointments-photos.test.js` both fail with 401 on main already.
  **PROBE ROWS:** `SLAB-RESERVE-PROBE-20260902`, `-PROBE2-`, `-PROBE3-` were written to **SD-AUDIT-2026** driving this. `sd_slabs` has no DELETE grant platform-wide, deliberately, so they stay. That is what the audit licence is for and they touch no customer data.
  **ALL SUITES GREEN:** 128/128 script blocks, div balance 4728/4728, no duplicate globals, seam check 34 clean / 0 could-not-tell, and exec-gate 16, prompt-budget 21, slab-reserve-client 13, style-parity 19, job-risk 24, ai-usage 15, exec-context 16, slab-reserve 12, extra-actions 24. 2026-09-02
- api/_resources/extra-actions.test.js + stonedesk.html + tests/nesting_saw_ticket.js + docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-02-stonedesk-worldwide-competitive-gap-audit.md (CC, 2026-09-02) -- **TWO TASKS. Pushed `80b6abc`, `59f6b0b`. And the second one required correcting my OWN audit, which had been wrong in a way that hid a live defect.**
  **(1) THE UNDECLARED-VERB TRIPWIRE WENT RED TWICE IN ONE DAY AND HID IT BOTH TIMES.** It was **one `deepStrictEqual` over the whole verb list**, red since 2026-08-27 (SAIRNsenior's `readiness`, `f1d2b57`, shipped undeclared). **A tripwire that is already failing catches nothing** -- the next person reads the red, recognises it, and moves on. That nearly happened when I added `reserve`, and then **it happened again the same day**: SAIRNroofing's `crew_load` landed while I was fixing the file and the suite went straight back to red.
  **Declaring the two missing verbs makes it green. It does not make it work**, which was the actual ask. So: **two assertions instead of one.** "No UNDECLARED verb" names the offending verb **and the resources granting it**, and tells the reader to go read the handler branch before adding it. "No STALE declaration" catches the other direction. **Split, an already-known failure in one direction can no longer hide a new one in the other** -- which is exactly how this failed.
  Both verbs declared **after reading their handlers, not on trust**: `crew_load` (rf_schedule) is session-gated, management/broad-read only -- the branch itself says letting a narrow-tier role through would be a way around the schedule read's own filter -- and persists nothing; `readiness` (sen_visits) is session-gated to owner/billing/coordinator/scheduler, one select, no write. **Verified both directions fire independently** by injecting `zzz_probe_verb` (fails only the undeclared assertion, printing the verb and its resource) and by removing a granted verb (fails only the stale assertion). **No registry file was touched** -- SAIRNroofing's is another session's active work and its declaration was already correct.
  **(2) CLAIM GATE BLOCKED ME LEXICALLY AND I RECORDED IT RATHER THAN REWORDING.** `sairn_claim.py` refused `stonedesk / saw and pick tickets from nesting gap 2` against cody's `sairnroofing / crew field labour scheduling depth gap A2` -- **overlap on the single word "gap"**. Different app, different files, no real subject overlap: the known matcher defect in CLAUDE.md. Claim written **by hand with a note saying exactly that**, per the standing rule that rewording to slip past the matcher is how the gate gets hollowed out.
  **(3) GAP 2 -- AND MY OWN AUDIT WAS WRONG IN THE SPECIFIC, IN A WAY THAT MATTERED.** The audit said `panel-nesting` had **"no export function of any kind."** **Two existed**, both wired to real buttons: `nestingExport()` (Export CSV) and `nestingPrint()` (Print). **The grep missed them because it searched for the names a reader would EXPECT** -- `nestExport`, `sawTicket`, `pickTicket` -- **rather than reading the panel's own markup for what its buttons actually call. A grep for the name you assume is not a search for the thing.**
  **AND THE TRUTH WAS WORSE THAN THE ABSENCE I HAD REPORTED.** `nestingExport()` wrote a CSV of **exactly TWO ROWS** -- a header and one row of slab width, height, material and cost -- and **exported NOT ONE CUTOUT**. `nestingPrint()` printed material, slab size, yield, waste cost, an **empty** AI line and a signature line: **no pieces, no positions, no dimensions, not even the canvas.** A cover sheet for a layout it did not contain. **A fabricator printed it, carried it to the saw, and had nothing to cut from -- with nothing on the page saying so.** Same failure class as a fabricated KPI: a plausible artefact that is quietly empty.
  **BOTH NOW CARRY THE REAL CUT LIST** -- seq, label, W, H, sq ft, X, Y, kerf -- **in SAW ORDER (Y then X, across then down, which is NOT the order pieces were entered)**, with **the origin corner named on the sheet**, because an X/Y with no stated reference is a number the operator has to guess at. The print **embeds the layout canvas, or says plainly that the diagram is missing** rather than looking complete without it. **An empty ticket is refused outright: that was the bug.**
  **EVERY FIGURE IS COMPUTED FROM THE CUTOUT DATA, NEVER READ BACK OUT OF A RENDERED KPI ELEMENT.** A ticket that echoes the screen is one DOM change away from being wrong on paper while looking right -- **a test sets the KPIs to `999%` / `$12345.00` and asserts none of it reaches the ticket.** And an **unentered cost prints "not entered", not "$0.00"**, because an unentered cost is not a measured zero; a cost genuinely entered as 0 still prints $0.00.
  **SCOPE STATED IN THE CODE, THE TEST AND THE COMMIT: this is the ticket half, not the machine half.** No DXF or G-code out to a specific CNC -- a per-machine integration with no generic answer. `g-code` still appears **zero times** in the file and the audit still says so.
  **16 assertions in `tests/nesting_saw_ticket.js`, mutation-tested** (removing the cut-order sort fails 2). **The audit doc carries the correction inline at GAP 2**, stating that the corrected finding is worse than the original one, rather than quietly editing the sentence.
  **LIVE-VERIFIED** on `sairn.vercel.app/stonedesk`: `nestCutOrder`, the CUT LIST origin header, the `saw-ticket-` filename and the missing-diagram notice all present on the served page. **ALL SUITES GREEN:** 128/128 script blocks, div balance 4728/4728, no duplicate globals, exec-gate 16, prompt-budget 21, slab-reserve-client 13, saw-ticket 16, style-parity 19, job-risk 24, ai-usage 15, exec-context 16, slab-reserve 12, extra-actions 25. 2026-09-02
- stonedesk.html + tests/slab_scan_labels.js (CC, 2026-09-02) -- **GAP 3 CLOSED: slabs can be labelled and scanned, and a scan says who the stone is promised to.** Pushed `05d2252`, live-verified. Five SQL files sent to Michael for the Supabase editor in run order.
  **WHY THIS GAP AND NOT ANOTHER.** GAP 1 (customer portal) and GAP 8 (remnant publishing) both need the same public-surface architecture decision the audit flagged as blocked; GAP 4 needs scanner hardware; GAP 7 (multi-location) is a schema change. GAP 3 was **third in the audit's own premortem** ("we scan every slab in") and needed **no decision from anyone**. It also compounds the morning's work directly.
  **THE ARGUMENT FOR IT, STATED PLAINLY: the compare-and-swap shipped earlier today stops the double-sale IN THE SYSTEM. It cannot stop someone walking into the yard and cutting a slab that is already promised, because nothing on the slab says so.** Scan a slab now and the reservation is the loudest thing on the card -- **"RESERVED FOR Ruiz kitchen -- do not cut this for another job"** -- not a field halfway down a list. A consumed slab says **ALREADY CONSUMED** rather than merely "not available".
  **NO NEW DEPENDENCY, AND A REAL ONE RECOVERED.** `qrcodejs` was **already being downloaded on every page load** (`stonedesk.html:25450`) and **`new QRCode(` appeared ZERO times in the file** -- a library every user paid for and nothing consumed. It is used now.
  **THE ASSERTION THAT MATTERS: AMBIGUITY IS REPORTED, NEVER RESOLVED.** Supplier barcodes repeat across bundles. A resolver that silently returns the first match would be **the same shape of mistake as the blind reservation write it follows -- a confident answer about the wrong physical stone.** Two slabs sharing a code produce **no match and BOTH ids**, so the yard checks the physical labels. Duplicate slab IDs are handled identically, not first-wins. And a code carrying our own `SDSLAB:` prefix **never falls through to a supplier barcode**: the prefix means "the payload is a slab id", and falling through would answer a question nobody asked.
  **STATUS IS DELIBERATELY NOT PRINTED ON THE LABEL, and the sheet says so on the printed page rather than only in a code comment.** A label reading "available" outlives the fact by months and would still be read as current. Status is what the scan is for.
  **BINDING A SUPPLIER'S OWN LABEL REFUSES ANYTHING THAT WOULD MAKE ONE CODE MEAN TWO STONES** -- a code already bound elsewhere (**naming the slab that holds it**), a code that is already a slab ID, or one of our own labels. **Its concurrency limit is stated rather than implied:** the bind writes through the ordinary blind upsert, so two devices binding the same code to different slabs in the same moment would both succeed. That hole is real, and smaller than the one it declines to copy, **because the resolver then reports the ambiguity at the next scan instead of quietly identifying the wrong stone.**
  **A MISSING QR LIBRARY IS ANNOUNCED ON THE PRINTED SHEET**, not just in a toast -- it comes from a CDN, so a shop printing offline gets IDs it can type by hand plus a loud note, never a silent pile of blank labels.
  **27 assertions in `tests/slab_scan_labels.js`, mutation-tested** (making the resolver first-wins on a duplicate barcode fails the ambiguity assertion). **Live-verified** on the served page: scan input, resolver, `new QRCode(`, the RESERVED FOR block and the label-sheet note all present.
  **AND I CHECKED THE PANEL'S EXISTING HANDLERS BEFORE ADDING TO IT, because of the GAP 2 lesson from an hour earlier** -- `sdSlabsAdd/AI/Export/Print`, `sdAddBlockPrompt`, `sdAddBundlePrompt`, `sdLineageReport`, `bsuSaveAll/ClearQueue` all defined, and `sdSlabsExport`/`sdSlabsPrint` read to confirm they emit the real slab rows rather than a header. **They do.** No second empty-artefact bug in this panel.
  **ALL SUITES GREEN:** 128/128 script blocks, div balance 4728/4728, no duplicate globals, slab-scan 27, saw-ticket 16, exec-gate 16, prompt-budget 21, slab-reserve-client 13, style-parity 19. 2026-09-02
- stonedesk.html + api/sd-data.js + api/_resources/stonedesk.js + sql/sd_approvals_schema.sql + api/sd-data-approvals.test.js + tests/approval_persistence.js (CC, 2026-09-02) -- **GAP 5, AND THE AUDIT WAS WRONG AGAIN -- THE SECOND TIME TODAY, THE SAME WAY.** Pushed `c1ec9da`, live-verified. **A SIXTH SQL FILE now waits on the Supabase editor: `sql/sd_approvals_schema.sql`.**
  **THE CORRECTION FIRST.** GAP 5 said *"the shop cannot get its own customer to e-sign a quote."* **It can, and always could.** `esigApprove()` captures a typed name, a canvas **checked for being genuinely blank**, the agreed total and the 50% deposit. **I concluded otherwise from grepping for the names I had already found in `panel-agreements` instead of reading the estimate panel's own markup** -- exactly the GAP 2 mistake, repeated within the hour. **And exactly as with GAP 2, what the wrong finding hid was worse than what it claimed.**
  **THREE DEFECTS IN ONE FLOW.**
  **(1) THE SIGNATURE WENT TO `localStorage['sd_approvals']` AND NOWHERE ELSE -- read back from NOWHERE in the entire file.** The document proving a customer agreed to a price lived on **one machine**, invisible to every other device and to everyone in the shop, and **one cache clear destroyed it**. Nobody could answer *"did the Smith quote get signed, and for how much?"* anywhere in the app.
  **(2) A CUSTOMER COULD SIGN A $0 AGREEMENT.** The total was parsed out of `#est-total.textContent`. **With no quote loaded that element renders an em-dash**, which strips to `''`, parses to `NaN`, and `||0` made it **ZERO** -- so a customer could draw a signature and be recorded as approving a $0 project with a $0 deposit, **saved without a word**. It now comes from `lastCalc.total` (the value that element is rendered FROM) and a non-positive total **refuses outright**: *"Nothing was recorded, and nothing was signed."*
  **(3) THE APP TOLD THE CUSTOMER SOMETHING IT HAD NOT DONE.** The confirmation said **"Your project is reserved"** while **nothing was reserved** -- no slab, no schedule slot, no install date; approving sets a customer record to `approved` and that is all. The heading said **"Approve & Reserve Your Project"**. Both now say what actually happened and what the deposit **will** do, in the tense that is true.
  **APPEND-ONLY, ENFORCED IN THREE PLACES, because it is the property that makes the record worth keeping:** a plain INSERT (**never** merge-duplicates), `unique (license_hash, approval_id)`, and **no UPDATE grant at all**. A duplicate id is **409 "sign a new approval instead"**. No DELETE grant either. **`signed_by` comes from the verified session, never the body** -- who was present when a customer signed is not a field the client asserts.
  **THE SYNC IS DELIBERATELY NOT AWAITED** (the customer is standing there and the local write already succeeded) **but a failure is SAID OUT LOUD**, quoting the server's own reason, and the row is badged **THIS DEVICE ONLY** in the new Signed Approvals list until it lands. `ALREADY_RECORDED` counts as synced -- the row **is** on the server, which is what was being checked.

  **One real gap surfaced by turning it on:** SAIRNcare described its three verbs collectively and named only `'route'`, so a reader grepping for `derive_charges` found the grant and no reasoning. Named individually now.

  **EPA 608 SECTIONS ARE EQUIPMENT, NOT RANKS -- that is most of the design.** Type I small appliances, Type II high-pressure, Type III low-pressure, Universal all three. **A Type I technician is not “less certified” than a Type II one.** Dispatching on “has an EPA card” is **how somebody is sent to a chiller they may not legally open**, so the section is stored and matched: Universal covers everything, nothing else covers anything else, and an unknown section in a requirement is **refused**, not ignored.
  **`has_expiry` IS STATED, NEVER DEFAULTED.** EPA 608 is for life (40 CFR 82.161); NATE renews on two years.   **ELIGIBILITY REFUSES, IT DOES NOT GUESS.** Missing -> not eligible. Expired -> not eligible. **Unknown expiry -> NOT eligible, and the reason says “unknown”, never “expired” and never “ok” -- those lead to different phone calls.** A job with no stated requirements returns **NO_REQUIREMENTS**, not “anyone may go”. Expiring still dispatches (valid today) but is warned about.

Done as **its own task**, not folded into either app's feature work.
  **READING THE THREE SIDE BY SIDE IS THE WHOLE VALUE, because they disagree in a way a blanket extraction would have silently flattened.** `dental` takes a DATE and returns `unknown|expired|expiring|ok` -- **dentistry has no lifetime credential, so no ‘current’ at all**. `roofing` returns **FIVE** states where **‘current’ means “valid and does NOT expire”** and ‘ok’ means “valid, expires, not near”. `mechanical` returns **FOUR**, where **‘current’ covers BOTH of roofing’s**.

  **THE SHARED PRIMITIVE THEREFORE ANSWERS ‘valid’ -- deliberately nobody’s word** -- and each engine maps it onto its own. **A shared classifier returning ‘current’ or ‘ok’ would have handed one app the other’s meaning without a single test failing.** There is an assertion for exactly that.
  **`latestByKey` is PARAMETERISED, not unified**, for the same reason: roofing keys on `(employee_id, type, credential|jurisdiction)`, ranks by `recorded_at` then `entry_id`, and **DROPS unknown record types**; mechanical keys on `(technician_id, type, epa_section|jurisdiction)` and ranks by `issued_on`. **`keyOf` returning null preserves roofing’s drop behaviour** rather than grouping strays under an empty key.

Pushed `c9c54a2`, live-verified. An EIGHTH SQL file now waits on the Supabase editor: `sql/mech_site_assets_schema.sql`.**
  **THE PART WITH LEGAL WEIGHT IS THE REFRIGERANT CHARGE.** EPA keys its leak-repair provisions to appliances whose **full charge is at or above 50 lb (40 CFR 82.157)**. So a charge nobody has recorded is **`unknown_charge` and NEVER `below`**. Telling a shop a machine is under threshold when **nobody ever weighed it** is a compliance claim with no evidence -- the same shape as the EPA 608 section mistake: **an answer that reads as clearance.**
  **THE TEST SUITE CAUGHT A REAL BUG DOING EXACTLY THAT.** `Number('')` is **0** in JavaScript, and the first draft ran the charge through `Number()` directly -- so an **EMPTY charge field became a measured zero** and classified the unit as below threshold. Fixed with a helper treating empty/whitespace/null as null, and the endpoint now refuses a non-numeric charge with a message that says **in as many words not to type 0 to mean unknown**.
  **AND THE ENGINE ISSUES NO COMPLIANCE VERDICT.** Leak-rate percentages differ by appliance category and the HFC picture has moved (2016 rule, the 2020 partial rollback, AIM Act rulemaking since) -- **encoding one would be this app asserting current federal law from a hardcoded number**. It answers one narrow, checkable question and **returns the threshold and its citation alongside** so the answer can be checked. **A test asserts “must”, any leak-rate percentage and any inspection schedule are all absent.**
  **NOT APPEND-ONLY, UNLIKE mech_credentials, AND DELIBERATELY SO.** A credential is **evidence** -- a renewal must not overwrite what somebody held on a given day, hence no UPDATE grant there. **An asset is a description of a physical thing**: a serial gets corrected, a unit gets relocated, a nameplate is re-read. Copying append-only by reflex would force a new row per typo and make “which row is the unit” ambiguous. So this one **upserts and carries an UPDATE grant as a considered exception** to the platform's grant-narrowing default -- **and still no DELETE**.
  **WRITE IS NOT MANAGEMENT-ONLY, also deliberately.** Credentials are, because nobody should record their own licence; **that reasoning does not transfer to describing a machine**, and copying it would have blocked the primary workflow -- a technician recording the unit in front of them.
  **Warranty is THREE states, not two:** true, false, and unstated. **`false` means “checked, and it is out”; `null` means nobody checked.** They store differently because they must not render the same.
  **THE PAGE IS RENAMED Equipment -> Site Assets**, because the research's shape is customer -> site -> asset and **the SITE was the missing part**: a unit with no site cannot be dispatched to, scheduled against, or put on an agreement. **“Last Service” and “Next PM” columns were deliberately NOT carried over** -- the service-history chain is genuinely append-only and is not built yet, and **columns that would always read “—” are how a panel starts looking populated when it is not.**
  **FIRST NEW CONSUMER OF THIS MORNING'S EXTRACTION:** warranty classification reuses `api/_lib/credential-expiry.js` and **maps the primitive's ‘valid’ onto its own ‘in_warranty’** rather than inheriting another app's vocabulary -- the property the extraction was designed around, exercised by a fourth caller.
  **43 assertions (23 engine, 20 endpoint). Mutation-tested: disabling the unknown-charge guard fails 4.** Seam check **53 clean**. **LIVE-VERIFIED:** `mech_site_assets` in the deployed whitelist, the served page carries `mechAssetRefresh` and the “blank if never weighed” placeholder, endpoint returns **401 NO_SESSION**. 2026-09-02

  **THE ONLY GENUINELY OPEN SAIRNROOFING ITEM on Hank's re-derived list.** Re-verified before building rather than taken from the doc: **`supplier` and `vendor` each appear ZERO times in sairnroofing.html**, and the single `receiving` hit is inside a patent-claim comment.
  **THIS IS NOT EDI TRANSPORT, AND THE PANEL SAYS SO ON SCREEN.** An X12 850/856/810 exchange needs a **trading-partner agreement** with ABC Supply / Beacon / SRS, an AS2 or VAN connection, and a **per-partner certification cycle**. That is an integration, not a screen -- **the same refusal as B3's certified payroll, for the same reason.** What IS built is the reconciliation those documents exist to enable, **identical whether the paperwork arrives by EDI, PDF or off a paper packing slip.**
  **AN UNSCANNED DELIVERY IS `receipt_unknown`, NEVER `short_received`.** Nobody writing down what arrived is a different fact from the delivery not arriving, **and only one of them is the supplier's problem** -- reporting the first as the second sends a contractor to argue about a truck that did arrive. **The mirror holds: a recorded ZERO received IS short, because somebody checked.** An empty quantity string stays empty through the client, the endpoint AND the engine -- **`Number('')` is 0** and coercing anywhere in that chain would turn every unscanned delivery into a short shipment. (Second time tonight that exact trap appeared; caught by design this time rather than by test.)
  **DISCREPANCIES ARE NEVER NETTED.** Over-invoiced on one line and short on another is **two problems, not zero** -- a single “variance” is how a ,000 over-bill hides behind a ,000 short delivery. **There is no net_variance field and a test asserts its absence.**
  **PRICE IS COMPARED PER UNIT**, because extended totals hide a price rise behind a quantity change -- there is a test where a **25% unit increase makes the invoice total go DOWN**. **Lines match on item code, never on position**: a supplier does not invoice in the order you ordered, and matching by index compares shingles to fasteners.
  **IT ISSUES NO PAYMENT VERDICT.** Same boundary as the refrigerant scope and the fall-protection board. **Tolerances default to EXACT -- no silent forgiveness -- and whatever is used is echoed back in the result.**
  **APPEND-ONLY, and here it is not a style choice:** these rows are what a contractor argues from when an invoice is wrong, and **a receipt edited after the fact is worth nothing in that argument.** No UPDATE grant, no DELETE grant; a corrected invoice is a NEW document and the match shows both. **Same reasoning as mech_credentials, opposite of mech_site_assets -- the difference is whether the row is evidence or a description.** Read is management/broad-read (an estimator needs material cost); **write is management only, because these rows decide whether an invoice gets paid.**
  **42 assertions (24 engine, 18 endpoint). Mutation-tested. Seam check 53 clean.** **Hank's status list updated in the same commit, per its own maintenance rule** -- B6 is now **PARTIAL** with the refused half named, and the roofing summary now reads “nothing open that is not vendor-gated.”
  **NOT CURL-VERIFIED:** `sairn.vercel.app` is still returning the Vercel Security Checkpoint challenge to automated requests. Reported as pushed, not as live-verified. 2026-09-02

No object, no field any caller could inspect, **memory selection was `_sdMemories.slice(0, 10)` -- insertion order and nothing else** -- and **no bound of any kind on the result**, while the `[0039]` message budget shipped earlier the same day measured its own fixed cost against it.
  **FOUR THINGS ADDED AND NOTHING ELSE.** (1) **Named fields** -- profile data is `{key, label, value, pos, drop, tokens}`, not eight `+` operators. (2) **Scored ranking** -- relevance to the message actually being answered (term overlap, normalised by **sqrt of the query's term count** so a long memory is not punished for containing more than was asked, and a one-word question does not score 1.0 off one incidental hit) at **0.65**, recency (exponential, **30-day half-life**) at **0.35**. (3) **A 3,000-token budget** on the assembled system prompt. (4) **A declared drop order.**
  **THE OLD BEHAVIOUR IS THE FLOOR, NOT A CASUALTY.** With **no query** -- which is what the legacy mode-context call site still does -- relevance is 0 for every memory and the ranking degenerates to **pure recency, exactly the order `slice(0,10)` produced.** That is asserted, not assumed. A ranking that silently reorders the no-query case would be an invisible, untraceable change.
  **THE DROP POLICY IS CUT-INFERRED-BEFORE-DECLARED, MACHINE-WRITTEN-BEFORE-HUMAN-WRITTEN:** memories lowest-scored first, then observed style directives, then profile fields by declared rank (notes -> revenue -> EIN -> owner -> headcount -> city/state). **Base prompt, shop name and declared style note are never droppable**, and when even that core will not fit it returns **`over: true` rather than sending an oversized request quietly** -- same convention as `sdBudgetMessages`. **Shop notes are dropped WHOLE, not truncated:** half a sentence of a shop's own operating notes, read as a complete instruction, is worse than their absence.
  **THE BUDGET IS MEASURED OFF THE RENDERED STRING AND RE-MEASURED AFTER EVERY DROP**, not summed from per-part estimates. Headings, separators and the withheld-notes line all cost tokens, and a total assembled from parts quietly disagrees with the thing actually sent.
  **TWO FABRICATED FACTS WERE IN THE PROMPT OF EVERY AI CALL THIS APP MADE, and are now gone.** `'City: ' + (p.city || 'Westlake') + ', Ohio'` -- **BOTH halves hardcoded.** A shop that had not filled in a city was described to the model as being in Westlake; **a shop in Dallas that HAD filled one in was still stamped Ohio**, and then asked for advice on pricing, labour and permitting for the wrong state. And `'Headcount: ' + (p.headcount || 1)` asserted a one-person shop whenever headcount was unset. Neither could survive a rewrite whose entire point is that these are named fields carrying real profile data. **Called out rather than absorbed** -- a rewrite is exactly where this kind of thing gets silently preserved.
  **WITHHOLDING IS NOW DISCLOSED.** `slice(0, 10)` withheld memories 11-20 from a twenty-memory shop and said nothing. The prompt now states how many notes were left out and tells the model not to treat the list as complete -- the same discipline as the truncated tool result on the message side. The user gets a separate `notify()` for system-half drops, because losing a shop's own profile fields changes an answer at least as much as losing old chat turns.
  **THE `Number('')` TRAP IS THE DATE GUARD HERE, and it is the one Michael flagged tonight.** `new Date('').getTime()` is NaN but **`new Date(null).getTime()` is 0**, which `Number.isFinite` accepts -- an undated memory would have been scored as **January 1970**, confidently, and ranked dead last as though that were measured. Guarded with an explicit null/empty check in front of the finite check; an undated memory scores **null** on recency, stays fully eligible on relevance, and is **counted in the object** so an odd ranking is inspectable. A clock-skewed **future** stamp is clamped to now, never to better-than-now.
  **MUTATION-TESTED, SEVEN WAYS, ALL BITE:** drop the date guard -> "null created_at is undated, not 1970"; reorder the drop policy -> "memories are cut before anything else"; remove the disclosure; remove the future-clamp; restore the hardcoded city default; make the shop name droppable; zero the relevance weight -> ranking reverts to recency. The last is in the test file itself as a standing probe.
  **THE PREMISE OF THE ASSIGNMENT COULD NOT BE MET, AND THAT IS SAID OUT LOUD RATHER THAN PAPERED OVER.** The task was to pull **real assembled-prompt-size numbers from a day of production traffic** and size the budget from them. **There are still none reachable.** Vercel shows **99 `/api/claude` calls in the last 24h** and not one carries a size in any log this session could read; the recorder added earlier today **404s silently until `sql/sairn_ai_usage_columns_2026-09-02.sql` is run in Supabase, and I have no way from here to confirm it has been.** So `SD_CONTEXT_BUDGET_TOKENS = 3000` is sized from the **structure**, measured off the real file -- base prompt 2,086 chars / 522 est tokens, guardrail 383 / 96 -- and is **labelled an estimate in the code, in the doc and here.** It bites on the one genuinely unbounded input on this path, which is **memory TEXT**: `writeSDMemory()` ASKS the model for 120 characters and asking is all it does, while a memory synced back from Supabase is bounded only by that table's 64KB row check.
  **ALL SUITES GREEN AFTER EVERY EDIT:** 129/129 script blocks parse, div balance 4807/4807, no duplicate globals, and composite-context 63, prompt-budget 21, style-parity 19, exec-gate 16, slab-reserve-client 13, nesting-DXF 26, saw-ticket 16. The 168 missing-DOM findings are **pre-existing and unchanged** -- verified by diffing the checker's output against `git stash`, ignoring line-number shift, not by assuming.
  **CLAIM WRITTEN BY HAND OVER A REAL BLOCK, AND SAID SO IN THE CLAIM ITSELF.** `sairn_claim.py` blocked this against Hank's active `stonedesk / nesting machine output dxf GAP2`. **That block was correct, not lexical** -- same subject, same 2MB file. Proceeded because Michael assigned `[0039]` explicitly after telling Hank directly that GAP 2 had already shipped in `ee51217`. **The task string was NOT reworded to slip the matcher**; the claim still says `stonedesk` and carries the reason in a `note` field. 2026-09-02
- api/sd-data.js + stonedesk.html + api/sd-data-locations.test.js + tests/stonedesk_locations.js + api/sd-data-session-gate.test.js (CC, 2026-09-03) -- **TWO IMPROVEMENTS PORTED ONTO HANK'S GAP 7, AFTER WE BOTH BUILT IT AND MINE WAS DISCARDED.**
  **THE COLLISION FIRST, because it is the more useful record.** Hank and I claimed GAP 7 **two seconds apart** -- mine `01:53:08Z`, his `01:53:10Z` -- and both `check`s returned CLEAR because neither claim existed when the other ran. We then both built it. His shipped (`5ddde11`); **my two commits were reset away, not branched**, on Michael's call and my own recommendation. **His design is genuinely better and that is why:** the location is stamped on the SLAB ONLY and quotes/jobs/POs/remnants derive theirs, because stamping a yard onto a job at creation FREEZES it -- move the work and the history stays with the old yard forever. I stamped `locationId` on the job at creation, which is the exact thing his test guards against. Same derive-on-read argument `rf_entities` already proved.
  **(1) THE WRITE HAD NO SESSION AT ALL, LET ALONE A ROLE.** The branch described itself as carrying *"the same licence-scoped gate as 'slabs' and 'remnants'"* -- and **`slabs` is in `SD_SESSION_GATED`, so it was never licence-scoped.** The comparison was wrong in the direction that mattered: **anyone holding the licence key could rename or close a yard, with no employee session.** That is not small, because the write is an **UPSERT on (license_hash, location_id)** -- renaming a yard silently relabels every slab ever attributed to it, on every screen, with no record anything changed. Closing one does the same to its whole history. `locations` is now session-gated for read and write, and **owner/admin on write**. Read stays open to every employee: a templater has to know which yard a slab is in to go and find it, and gating the read to management would break the primary use.
  **A REFUSAL AND AN OUTAGE ARE NOT THE SAME ANSWER, and the gate created that distinction.** The client wrote locally first, then pushed, and reported *"Saved on this device only"* on any failure. Correct for a dead network -- try again later and it lands. **Wrong for a 403: nothing will ever make that write land**, and the local copy would show that person a yard nobody else can see, permanently. A refusal now **rolls the local change back** and says who may do it; an outage still keeps it. Same rule as `sdReserveSlab`, which never falls back to writing locally when the server said no.
  **(2) THE RECONCILE INVARIANT, AND THE REASON `totals` COULD NOT PROVIDE IT.** `totals` is the **sum of the rows**, so it agrees with the rows by construction and would still agree after a slab had been dropped on the floor. The only check with force compares against what came **IN** -- `input_total.slabs` vs `totals.slabs` -- and the panel now shows a **red banner naming both numbers** rather than a total nobody can reconcile. Same reason `api/_lib/roofing-consolidation.js` returns `input_total` and `reconciles` on every call.
  **TWO OF MY OWN ASSERTIONS WERE WORTHLESS ON THE FIRST DRAFT AND THE MUTATION PROBES CAUGHT BOTH.** (a) The reconcile negative control **computed the comparison in the TEST** and asserted on that, so hardcoding `reconciles: true` in the app scored a clean pass -- the same mistake Hank's own harness already records about stubbing `sdLocName`. It now injects the defect INTO the extracted source (`slabs.slice(1).forEach`) and asserts on the flag the app returns. (b) The management-gate assertion matched only the **refusal string**, so deleting the whole condition while leaving the message passed. It now matches the condition.
  **AND I MADE A THIRD TEST STALE, THEN FIXED IT.** `api/sd-data-session-gate.test.js` had an assertion titled *"lists exactly seven pairs"* that **counted nothing** -- it matched three entries and stopped. Adding `locations` took the table to nine and the title stayed green while becoming false, which is precisely what a count exists to catch. **The count is now derived from the table**, and removing `locations` fails it.
  **FOUR MUTATION PROBES, ALL BITE:** drop the management gate (6 endpoint assertions fail), drop the session gate, hardcode `reconciles:true`, remove the refusal rollback. **28 endpoint assertions, 63 yard assertions**, and 17 session-gate.
  **ALL SUITES GREEN:** 129/129 script blocks, div balance 4835/4835, no duplicate globals, nav/panel 64/64, reachability clean, seam 74 clean / 0 could-not-tell, and composite-context 63, prompt-budget 21, style-parity 19, exec-gate 16, slab-reserve 13+13, approvals 23, DXF 26, saw-ticket 16, extra-actions 27. **162 missing-DOM findings, unchanged** -- the same count as before this work.
  **⚠ `sql/stonedesk_locations_schema.sql` IS STILL PENDING A RUN** (Hank's file, unchanged by me). Until then the panel works from localStorage and the write refuses with `NOT_PROVISIONED` naming it.
  **ON RECORD, per Michael: "check, then claim, then proceed" did not survive a two-second dispatch window.** The claim tool narrows a four-hour collision to about a one-fetch one and that was still not enough tonight. **The likely fix is a re-check immediately before writing the first line, not only at claim time** -- not attempted here, and deliberately not designed mid-session. 2026-09-03
- stonedesk.html + tests/base_prompt_single_source.js (CC, 2026-09-03) -- **TWO COPIES OF THE BASE SYSTEM PROMPT, ALREADY DIVERGED, AND THE STALE ONE WAS LIVE.**
  **FOUND WHILE LOOKING FOR THE NEXT GAP, not from the audit -- the in-house list is now empty.** Every row on the re-derived status doc's buildable-in-house table is struck through or vendor-gated, so this came from chasing an old finding (`exec-context.js`'s "NOT FIXED HERE" note about a QuickBooks claim in a stonedesk greeting). **That greeting turned out to be already corrected**; the grep for `quickbooks` is what surfaced this instead.
  **PROVEN BY COMPARING THE LITERALS, NOT BY READING THEM.** `const SYSTEM` at :10671 was a **byte-identical PREFIX** of the `const base` inside `buildSDSystemPrompt()` -- **1,793 chars against 2,086** -- missing exactly the **293-character SAIRNBIZ ROUTING paragraph**. Diffed character by character rather than eyeballed, because two 2KB strings that start identically are exactly the kind of thing a reader confirms as "the same" and moves on.
  **IT MATTERED BECAUSE THE STALE COPY WAS LIVE.** `getAIDrawingAdvice()` -- the **"AI - Review This Job" button on the Drawing Tool** (:4521, real control, real handler) -- posted it as `system` on every click. **A shop asking that button about payroll or bookkeeping got an assistant that had never been told those are handled by SAIRNbiz**, while the same question in the chat panel got one that had. Same product, same shop, two different answers depending which button they pressed -- and the SAIRNbiz routing is a deliberate product decision, not a nicety.
  **ONE CONSTANT NOW, AND THE SURVIVOR IS THE CORRECTED ONE.** `SD_BASE_PROMPT` holds the 2,086-char text, published on `window`, read by `buildSDSystemPrompt()` and by the legacy `SYSTEM` name. **The literal was moved by script, never retyped** -- transcribing 2KB by hand is how a third variant gets born.
  **`SYSTEM` IS KEPT AS AN ALIAS RATHER THAN DELETED**, and that is a judgment call worth stating: it is still referenced by the orphaned `#messages` chat cluster this file already flags, which **cannot run** (`#userInput` occurs zero times, so the handler returns on its fourth line -- the same dead path the patent doc records). Deleting `SYSTEM` would turn a silently-dead path into a **throwing** one, and removing that cluster is its own change with its own review. **What matters is that it can no longer hold a DIFFERENT prompt**, which was the whole defect.
  **CHECKED THE OTHER THREE `SYSTEM` REFERENCES BEFORE TOUCHING ANYTHING.** :11487 is inside the dead `#messages` path; :19608 reads `window.SYSTEM` which was **always `undefined`** because a top-level `const` never attaches to window -- that path is dead too and would have sent an EMPTY system prompt if it were not; :36367 is a **local `var SYSTEM`** belonging to Stonehead, a different feature with its own prompt, unaffected.
  **16 assertions, four mutation probes, all bite:** reintroduce a second literal, revert `buildSDSystemPrompt` to its own copy, drop the routing paragraph, unwire the button. **The count-to-one assertion has an explicit negative control** -- a test that counts to one is worthless if it would also pass at two.
  **ALL SUITES GREEN:** 129/129 script blocks, div balance 4835/4835, no duplicate globals, nav/panel 64/64, reachability clean, 162 missing-DOM findings unchanged, and yards 63, composite-context 63, prompt-budget 21, style-parity 19, exec-gate 16, slab-reserve-client 13, DXF 26, saw-ticket 16.
  **NOTE FOR WHOEVER PICKS UP NEXT: the competitive-gap list is exhausted.** Nothing buildable in-house remains on it. Work now has to come from a new audit, a customer request, or a decision -- or from findings like this one, which no audit was ever going to contain. 2026-09-03
